Apparent 0 g in orbit." **He is right and PH-91 was wrong**: "a nadir-pointing station has artificial gravity for free at 3.49886 m/s^2" is true of the FIELD STRENGTH and false of what an occupant feels, because a station at 400 km is in FREEFALL and the frozen station of PH-94 is dynamically A TOWER ON A 400 KM PILLAR. **The first measurement is the one that sized the job, and it was taken in UNMODIFIED code: the walker does NOT degrade into a floating body.** At 3.2% gravity (deliberately just above the new float gate, so the ORIGINAL path answers) it reaches 4.6 m/s under thrust and then reads **0 m/s after one second of coast and 0 after three**, because the move term has always been a VELOCITY SERVO, and its descent samples are **[0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]** because no descent key has ever existed. So freefall got a thrust model rather than a tuned constant: momentum is kept (drift lost **exactly 0**), contact with structure is inelastic on the radial axis as well as the tangent (3.41 m/s into a wall goes to 0), and the radial axis went on Space and Shift, which have nothing else to do when there is no walk to make faster. **Gravity itself became a PORT rather than a number**: apparent g is `g(where I am) - a(the frame I ride in)`, `PlanetBody.gravityAccel` stays the sole authority, and a station carries TWO volumes, freefall and generator. The generator publishes no magnitude of its own, it cancels the cancellation, so a powered deck restores gravity **bit-exactly** (`apparentG === trueG`, 3.531600000000002) and every figure from PH-90 to PH-97 carries over un-retaken; unpowered it reads **exactly 0** on the deck and the player floats. **The residual is modelled rather than rounded away**: 100 m above the station it is **-0.0007062140661235894 m/s^2, matching the predicted tidal difference to 0 and pointing OUTWARD**, which is why "microgravity" is the word and "zero gravity" is not. An EVA 30 m off the hull floats and drifts 0.000000 m in three seconds; the 200 m control still falls 15.98 m against 15.89 m predicted, so `stationwalk.js` P3 now proves two things instead of one. Physics deliberately did not paper over it. **AND THEN THE DELTA-V READOUT STOPPED LYING, BECAUSE REID'S AUTOPILOT ASK RESTS ENTIRELY ON IT (PH-142 to PH-144, 2026-08-02, ABI 21 to 22).** R43 was raised by this lane and left for Admin, correctly, and Admin ruled it the moment it became the load-bearing number in a feature. **It was not an approximation, it was a quantity that does not exist**: `stagePerformance` pooled the FIRST engine's kind of propellant and divided that by EVERY engine's mass flow, so a solid booster beside a liquid engine published `propellantKg 4300, burnTimeS 11.203, dv 656.02` (4300 / (61.801 + 321.99) = 11.2028 exactly) against a flight that burned the booster's 9000 kg for 27.95 s and ran the liquid engine to 69.58 s. **A stage lighting two kinds has TWO burn times and ONE delta-v**, so the burn is now CUT at every flameout and Tsiolkovsky applied across each phase with that phase's own thrust-weighted Isp: `fullThrustS` is the first flameout, `burnTimeS` is the last, and the hand computation done BEFORE the code ran gives 27.948953 / 69.578182 s and **2232.4835 + 1657.8715 = 3890.3550 m/s** where the old code said 656.02, which is **17% of the truth**. **The one-line fix in the risk entry was REFUSED**: filtering the engine loop by `consumes` drops 672 kN out of a stage the booster dominates and publishes a pad TWR saying the vehicle cannot lift off, which trades an understated burn time for an understated thrust. **Every single-kind stage is bit-identical**, pinned with `==` against `tsiolkovsky` and not a tolerance, so 1857.79 + 3065.12 = 4922.91 has not moved and the ascent probe still stages at 69.7 s. **R44b came with it rather than costing a second bump**: the navball's table was read off the DESIGN, which `_of_fl_create` COPIES and which therefore never burns a gram, so it was the WRONG VESSEL rather than a stale one. New export `of_fl_stage_performance(f)` over `s->craft`, sharing ONE row writer with the design export, and the read moved into `sample()` beside the other three live instruments rather than into `refreshParts`, which bumps `partsRevision` and would have redrawn the whole vehicle to update a number. **`probes/stagedv.js` had been built to assert the DEFECT behind a flag and to name that flag on the day somebody fixed it; it went red exactly as designed**, and is now green with both branches gone: stage 0 falls **1857.79 to 1759.13 to 645.17 m/s** while its own tank drains 6194.4 to 3413.3 kg, its remaining burn seconds fall **64.78 to 19.78 over 45.00 s of MET**, and a departed stage reads **0 against a pad value of 1857.79**. **And running the suite found a stale assertion of this lane's own**: `probes/ascent.js` still checked that you CANNOT climb out in orbit, which PH-111 had shipped underneath it; flipped to assert the round trip, out and back in. **The transfer/rendezvous API is SCOPED and not yet built**: a subagent established that `maneuver::plan()` EVALUATES and never SOLVES, that there is no Lambert, no phase angle, no departure sweep and NO TARGET CONCEPT anywhere in the header, and that `propagate` / `park` / `resume` / `basisAt` / `estimateBurn` / `samplePath` are the pieces to compose. **AND THEN THE TRANSFER ENGINE'S FIRST TWO MILESTONES LANDED ON TOP OF IT (PH-145 to PH-146, 2026-08-02).** A subagent established the ground truth before anything was written: **`maneuver::plan()` EVALUATES and never SOLVES**, and the header has no Lambert, no Hohmann, no phase angle, no departure sweep and NO TARGET CONCEPT anywhere in it. So the missing layer is exactly the INVERSE. `orbital::lambert` went beside `propagate` because they share the same Stumpff functions, and **it takes a REFERENCE NORMAL rather than testing an axis**, because the textbook's `(r1 x r2).z` test would have been a THIRD inclination convention in a codebase already carrying two (PH-40). Verified against the propagator itself to **1e-8 m/s** and against the textbook Hohmann as a LIMIT (**378.330602, 367.719292, 364.150422, 364.001254 converging on 363.999746 from ABOVE**), because a 180 degree sweep is collinear through the focus and every Lambert solver in existence is singular there. **Its one confident-wrong-answer mode is pinned rather than assumed**: single-revolution, so a time of flight past one period silently answers a different question, and the caller keeps the rule. **Then [`transfer.h`](../../core/include/of/transfer.h), published as an interface a UI lane can call.** A BODY IS NOT A SPECIAL CASE BY CONSTRUCTION: Cinder orbits Forge at 1.2e7 m well inside its SOI, so the station and the moon are the SAME Lambert and differ in ONE function, `arrivalCostMS`, match versus capture. **Measured with the moon's gravity switched off and nothing else changed: `departDvMS` and `sweepRad` bit-identical, only the arrival moves, and capture is CHEAPER**, so a readout billing a moon like a station would refuse trips the vehicle can fly. **The chart is the feature, so the published thing is a SAMPLED CURVE**: 80 km parking orbit to Anchorage, 48 departures x 24 times of flight over one synodic period of 4268.135 s, **6.1 ms for 1152 solves**, leaving now **585.749 m/s**, best departure at **t 3995.7 s for 398.330 m/s**, **saving 187.419 m/s by waiting 66.6 minutes**, and the best is 2.32 m/s above the ideal Hohmann, which is the grid's resolution rather than an error. **`Verdict` separates Reid's three cases** and is driven with a vehicle drained to exactly `best + 2.0` m/s (refused now, allowed at the window) and an empty one where `anyTransferExists` stays TRUE because the GEOMETRY is fine and only the VEHICLE is not. **Milestones 3 to 5 (the path polyline, execution, and hold-this-orbit) are NOT built, and none of it is exposed through the ABI yet.** **AND THEN IT FLEW ITSELF (PH-150 to PH-153, 2026-08-03).** [`autopilot.h`](../../core/include/of/autopilot.h) is the one header that COMMANDS a vehicle, and the rule it exists to honour is that **it flies the plan the solver produced and does not re-derive it in flight**. **Measured, 80 km circular to a requested 200 km: 200295.1 x 199693.3, e 0.000376, semi-major axis 799994.2 against 800000, five point eight metres, with no hand on the throttle.** A burn is cut on MEASURED delta-v rather than a stopwatch, pinned by sabotaging the predicted duration 40% in both directions and finding the orbit unmoved. **Flying it found three defects that a human pilot would never have found, and the third one only appeared because the first two were fixed.** (1) `stabilityAssist` derives its axis from `cross(forward, target)`, which is the ZERO VECTOR when they are opposed, so **a vehicle commanded to flip exactly 180 degrees never turned** and held 180.000 degrees for 600 s while correctly reporting the error as pi. That is the normal case for an autopilot, because circularising at apoapsis points exactly retrograde by construction. (2) The attitude gate was on the ANGLE alone, so the engine lit as the vehicle SWEPT THROUGH the target and kept rotating under thrust: worst attitude with the engine lit **3.92 degrees against a 2 degree gate**. (3) **Fixing (2) made the orbit worse**, 6x on eccentricity, because holding for the rate pushed the burn late, which is exactly what PH-38's lead exists to prevent: the real fault was that a plan scheduling its first burn for RIGHT NOW cannot be flown by a vehicle that is not already pointed at it. `holdOrbit` now propagates to where the vehicle will be once it has had time to turn. **All three together: lowering lands 5.0 m out at e 0.000016, and an eccentric start goes from 1771.7 m to 72.1 m.** **And docking was CHECKED BEFORE BUILDING, on Admin's instruction, and it does not exist (R65).** The part data is complete and read by nobody, the rocket mesh is real, the station's `socket_dock` is a marker no client code reads, `mintStation` gives Anchorage `emptyDesign()` so it holds no parts at all, and there is ZERO logic: no proximity test, no capture test, nothing that merges two vessels. It is blocked on R26, this lane's own item, because the client can hold exactly one promoted vessel. **Rendezvous is deliverable; dock is a mechanism somebody has to design.** **AND THEN IT RENDEZVOUSED (PH-154 to PH-155, 2026-08-03).** An 80 km parking orbit to Anchorage's 400 km ring, planned by `transfer.h` and executed by `autopilot.h` through the real `FlightSim`, with the target propagated alongside every tick. **Measured against a plan that predicted zero for both: the match burn lights at 1306.61 m and 206.4555 m/s of closing speed, and the flight ends 108.87 m out at 0.23133 m/s relative, a factor of 892.** The negative control is the same flight with ONE thing removed and ends at **1367.98 m/s**: without the match burn it is a fly-past, and that one burn is the whole difference between a fly-past and an arrival. **Both defects found this pass were in the instruments rather than the physics**: the driver stopped when the PROGRAM did, so the control ended 66 s in reporting a 525 km approach to a target it had not set off towards; and `circularAbout` and `circular80km` run in opposite senses, so mixing them planned a retrograde rendezvous at 3818.69 m/s that the fuel gate correctly refused. **AND DOCKING GOT ITS REAL BLOCKER, WHICH IS NOT THE ONE THIS LANE NAMED.** Admin was right that R26 does not block the station case: `promotedId` is a hard singleton but Anchorage is never promoted, `installStation` puts its 57 proxies into the queryable set at BOOT with no gate, and both frames are body-frame absolute f64 metres so a raw distance needs no conversion. **The real blocker is R67, and it is PH-90's own decision coming back: the station is FROZEN in the body frame because the walker has no carrier term, so a ship in a real 400 km orbit passes it at 7.6 km/s and rendezvous with it is impossible.** A naive capture test tunnels as well, 127 m per tick against a 0.60 m radius. The shape of the answer is already in `stateOf`'s `fixed` case. **AND THEN THE ARM BUTTON STARTED WORKING (PH-156 to PH-158, 2026-08-03).** Admin reordered the moon behind this because the gameplay lane was blocked on it and the moon blocks nobody. **This lane published the execution contract FIRST**, the mirror of how the planning half worked: `of_ap_arm_hold_orbit`, `of_ap_arm_transfer`, `of_ap_cancel`, `of_ap_status` (18 words) and `of_ap_note`, declared in `vesselabi.ts`, additive and symbol-detected so **ABI stays 22**. **THREE CALLS AND NO TICK**: the autopilot rides inside `of_fl_step` and `of_fl_step_n`, because a separate tick is a second call in the hot loop that must be remembered in the right order with the right dt in both exports, and forgetting it under warp is a silent failure where the vehicle coasts through its own burn. **Driven in node with the client calling only `of_fl_step`: 680 km to a requested 800 km lands at a 799997.2 and e 0.000000 having spent exactly its planned 177.5676 m/s, and under `of_fl_step_n` at 200 ticks a call it is IDENTICAL** which is what makes a scheduled departure reachable, since one hours out is only ever flown warped. **A scheduled departure needed no mechanism**: the schedule is a number in the plan, not a state in the executor. Cancel cuts the throttle to a measured 0.0 N; a refusal returns 0 and names itself (`not enough delta-v aboard for this orbit`) while `status` still answers so the screen can show it. **The SOI seam is OPEN but unfinished**: `findSoiEntry` and `planCapture` are written and compiling in `transfer.h` section 4b, which is spike2's long-outstanding step 4, and the moon was deferred mid-task by the reorder. **And a third instrument defect in one night**: this lane's own node driver read `of_fl_telemetry` word 0 as thrust and reported 80000.0 N after a cancel, which was the ALTITUDE in metres; thrust is word 5 and was 0.0 all along. **AND THEN IT WENT TO THE MOON (PH-159 to PH-160, 2026-08-03).** **Measured: an autopilot flies an injection, a mid-course correction, an SOI handoff and a capture, and ends in a bound orbit about Cinder at a semi-major axis of 292750.2 m, e 0.106588, periapsis 61546.6 m above the surface, with no hand on the controls.** **Three defects, each only visible once the one before it was fixed, and all three were the PLAN being physically wrong rather than the code being buggy.** (1) **Aiming at a moon's centre is a crater**: a Lambert aims at a POINT, so periapsis came out **1214.7 m against a 200000 m body**, and `planCapture` refused it correctly. The fix is the impact parameter, 543144 m of offset for 4.61 m/s. (2) **A moon transfer cannot be flown open-loop**: the 861 m/s injection's finite-burn residue over a 7.4 hour coast is **468 km at the sphere of influence**, the same size as the 543 km aim offset it perturbs, and the flown arrival periapsis was **185669 m against a 200000 m body**. That is R66 at the scale where it becomes the mission, and it is why real flights budget correction manoeuvres. (3) **The handoff is a measurement, not a prediction**: coasting in Forge's frame past the boundary is the wrong two-body problem and landed 654 km out. **`flyTransfer` now refuses for a REASON rather than categorically**, three distinct sentences, and the miss case had to be provoked honestly because `solveTransfer` always lands on whatever target it was given. **One number does not agree and is asserted as a known gap rather than tolerated (R69): the re-planned capture is priced 232 m/s dearer than the burn actually flown**, against a suite where everything else matches to under a metre per second. **AND THEN R69 TURNED OUT TO BE AN INDEX (PH-161 to PH-162, 2026-08-03).** `replanCaptureAtHandoff` wrote the capture to `burns[1]` while re-arming at `burnCount - 1`, and once a mid-course correction is INSERTED at index 1 those are different slots: it overwrote the CORRECTION, left the STALE capture at index 2, and flew that. **Everything ran, nothing threw, the vehicle captured into a real bound orbit, and the total double-counted a burn it never flew** which is the same confidently-incorrect class as the night's other three. **The gap this lane refused to paper over was 232 m/s and is now 2.0e-07**, and the moon orbit went from 292750.2 m at e 0.106588 to **270379.5 m against a planned 270369.7, which is 9.8 m, at e 0.000448: a circle.** **AND THE CLIENT CAN NOW SEE A BODY.** The rendering lane hit a hard stop drawing Cinder and reported it exactly: `sim_world.h` is not in the wasm build and of 207 exports not one returned a body position. **They deliberately did not transcribe the constant**, so `kCinderOrbitRadiusM` MOVED into `orbital.h` and `sim_world.h` now aliases it, with new `of_body_state` and `of_body_facts` exports. Verified against their own sizing: **120.0 units, 1.91 degrees, and Forge from Cinder 5.72 degrees.** **R70 raised**: `sim_world` holds Cinder still and `transfer.h` flies to a moving one, reconciled for now by phasing t = 0 to the frame graph's offset bit-exactly, but two callers picking differently is the two-authority failure and it needs an Admin ruling. **AND THEN R68 GOT HALF WAY AND STOPPED HONESTLY (PH-163 to PH-164, 2026-08-03).** **The gap R68 really was: `of_ap_arm_transfer` COULD NOT EXPRESS A BODY.** Its nine target words describe an orbit and there is no vocabulary in them for "and it is a moon", so armed with Cinder's elements it planned a **vessel rendezvous with the moon's centre, a 1561.330 m/s velocity match against 200 km of rock**, running clean and answering confidently. New `of_ap_arm_body_transfer(f, t, bodyId, captureAltitudeM)` as a SEPARATE export rather than an appended id, because naming a body is a different question from describing an orbit and physics owns the ephemeris (PH-162): **verified through the bridge it now arms three burns for 1701.661 m/s.** The correction is RESERVED at plan time and FILLED at execution time, and a first version solved the Lambert from today's position with tomorrow's time of flight and produced a **2016 m/s correction against an 861 m/s injection**, which is a second mission with the wrong departure point. **TWO DEFECTS THEN STOPPED IT AND BOTH ARE RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER (R71, R72).** **BOTH ARE NOW CLOSED (PH-165 to PH-169, 2026-08-03) AND NEITHER WAS WHAT IT SAID IT WAS.** **R71 WAS NOT THE GATE AND NOT THE PITCH/YAW CONTROLLER: it is a ROLL instability, and it was shipped.** `stabilityAssist` built its rate term with the TRANSVERSE inertia while the integrator divides the roll component by `Iyy`, so the roll loop ran at `kd (Itrans/Iroll)` and one step at the browser's 1/60 tick turned +1e-3 rad/s into **-1.3132e-3**, larger than the input and pointing the other way. A rocket is long and thin so that ratio is large by construction: **the shipped Ascender is 46.26 against a threshold of 40.0, and an ordinary ascent with nothing injected reaches 41.98 deg/s at 1/60 and 9.9e-17 at 0.002.** Roll does not move the thrust axis, so every trajectory assertion was blind to it, including the step-independence test which flies 0.02 and 0.01, one either side of the 0.0144 s threshold; the client then hid the symptom with `levelWings`, whose own comment records `ROL -93 degrees with no roll input given at any point` and blames aerodynamics. **The autopilot's gate was telling the truth the whole time.** **R72 WAS THE HARNESS**: the driver flew a ship orbiting the OPPOSITE way to Cinder and departed at an arbitrary phase, and its closest approach to the moon was **11,320,004 m against a 2,400,000 m sphere**. **REID CAN NOW FLY TO THE MOON FROM THE CLIENT: driven through `of_fl_step_n` at 1/60, injection, mid-course correction, SOI handoff 37 m inside the boundary and capture, ending in a BOUND 84 km circular orbit about Cinder (sma 283814.9 m, e 0.000729) with the flight and its own plan agreeing to 9e-6 m/s.** · **CINDER ACTUALLY ORBITS NOW (PH-172, D-014): the frame graph held it static while the sky drew it moving, and the two separated at 542.5 m/s, which is not a tolerance but the whole of the moon's orbital speed.** **AND A MOON HAS A DEPARTURE CHART (PH-171, R74): `of_ap_body_departure_curve` and `of_ap_body_reach`, both priced with the same solve the arm uses, closing a 601.4 m/s hole a player could fall into in silence.** **AND IT LANDS ON THE MOON (PH-175, PH-176): a touchdown gate that says which of four ways an arrival was not a landing, and a powered descent flown onto REAL Cinder terrain at sites searched for by slope, touching down at 1.0006 m/s vertical and 0.0069 m/s lateral.** **AND DOCKING NOW HAPPENS BY ITSELF (PH-173, PH-174, D-015 layer two): a vessel could only ever push along its own nose, so translational RCS had to exist first, and the approach program then flies the last hundred metres from the rendezvous this project actually flew and mates the ports at 0.05000 m/s with 0.0000 degrees of cone error.** **DOCKING HAS ITS MECHANISM (PH-170, D-015 layer one): `docking.h` answers whether two ports mated during a tick and where the join puts the vessel, swept rather than point-in-sphere, with every refusal two-sided and the join checked by closing the loop. What is NOT built is the client half, and the blocker is named: `learnStationSockets` keeps a position and discards the axis (R76).** **PH numbering jumps 111 to 142 at Admin's direction.** · **AND THEN IT LEAVES THE MOON, AND THE RIBBON BECAME FLYABLE (PH-200 to PH-203, 2026-08-03):** new [`ascent.h`](../../core/include/of/ascent.h) is `descent.h` read upwards, and a 39th ctest suite flies it off REAL Cinder from pads a slope-and-rim search FINDS, reaching a 20.0 x 19.4 km orbit at e 0.001362 in 661.9 s for **740.6796 m/s**, and 761.65 from a pad 2,149 m down a hole with the rim 3,508 m above it. **Four defects, each only visible once the one before it was fixed**, and the third is the one worth keeping: **the small-angle time-to-apoapsis is valid only NEAR apoapsis and the program used it to decide whether it was near apoapsis**, so an insertion fired 14 km low; and the fourth cut a burn with 14.92 m/s still owed and **flew the lander 9,364 m through the middle of Cinder**, because `flight.h` has no contact model. **R63 CLOSED by flying it rather than asserting it**, and its prediction of "a few percent" was wrong: 0.343781, of which **0.093048 is arithmetic no ascent can avoid**, because the formula charges the ideal transfer to the loss term (R83, for Admin). **AND THE NAVBALL'S GUIDANCE RIBBON, whose own comment read "Shown, never flown", NOW HAS A MODE THAT FLIES IT** (PH-201, Digit9, `GDN`): tracked to 0.0396 degrees over a ribbon swinging 45.50, against a no-key control at 111.45. **And both browser flight acceptances turned out to be red at their own section 0** on a catalogue literal that went stale when a 25th part landed (PH-202). · **AND THEN THE ASCENT NUMBER'S FORM WAS RULED AND CHANGED (PH-204, R83 CLOSED):** the baseline is the ideal two-impulse transfer rather than the parking speed, so **Forge's published ascent moves 3724.649392 to 3720.907932** and the reference mission's margin gains 3.93 m/s. **My own evidence for asking had been taken from flights that never finished**; re-flown to completion, the new baseline removes **72.5 per cent** of the fraction's variation across parking altitude, and Forge is now exact at the radius the real vehicle reached. · **Last updated:** 2026-08-03 (PH-200 to PH-204: **the airless ascent, R63 CLOSED, the ninth SAS mode, and two dead acceptances revived**; R83 the loss-fraction FORM is Admin's to rule, R84 the clearance clamp costs 41.63 m/s and buys 0.15 m, R85 flightabuse's three content failures, R86 no bridge export and that is deliberate) (was 2026-08-03: PH-165 to PH-169; **R71 and R72 CLOSED, both mis-diagnosed in their own entries**; a drift-form ignition gate measured and REFUSED at a cost of 880 m of capture orbit for 0.28 degrees of attitude; one progress concept closes R71's hold and the gameplay lane's stuck burn together, and the executor gains a staging verb; `of_fl_state` word 17 is /core's sas mode and its exact-stride reader is defused; R73 `holdRoll` may no longer earn its place, R74 no departure chart for a body, R75 no stagings word; **PH-170: D-015's first layer, `docking.h` and a 36th ctest suite, capture SWEPT because two ports passing through each other at 7.6 km/s are 63.3 m apart at both ends of the tick**, R76 the station socket's axis is unreachable at runtime, R77 the capture test must ride inside the step, R78 the asset gate cannot see an axis error; **PH-171 closes R74: a moon has a departure chart and a reach row, and my own first version of both had to be driven before it was right**; **PH-172 closes R70: the frame graph's static Cinder is gone, because it and the drawn one separated at 542.5 m/s, exactly the moon's whole orbital speed**; R79 the carrier term is now reachable rather than hypothetical; **PH-173 gives a vessel TRANSLATIONAL RCS, which `rcsThrustN` has been authored for since the catalogue was written and which every consumer spent on torque**; **PH-174 is D-015 layer two: the auto-approach docks from PH-154's own rendezvous arrival at t=175.13 s, 0.6000 m, 0.05000 m/s, 0.0000 degrees of cone**; R80 a radially mounted port is refused because there is no roll command, R81 neither docking layer has a bridge export yet and that is deliberate; **PH-175 and PH-176 are the landing: a touchdown gate that names four ways an arrival was not a landing, and a powered descent that flies onto REAL Cinder at 1.0006 m/s vertical and 0.0069 lateral on measured 2.81 and 9.85 degree slopes**; R63 attempted and honestly NOT closed, with the three things that stopped it written down; R82 the gate decides and does not stop anything) (was 2026-08-02: PH-105 to PH-111; **R54 and R55 CLOSED**; R56 the sealed hall is a BLOCKER on the Blender lane, R57 the deck hole and R58 the 30 mm headroom margin raised for it, R59 the anchor has no radius raised for persistence; R53 KinematicBody still over its line cap; R48 updated, and zero g moves it both ways; R51 reload.mjs flag/verify gaps and R52 tunnelsink bore red open; R49 no spatial index and R50 the altM trap open; R22, R23, R26, R29, R30, R33 to R35, R37 to R43, R45 to R47 open, **R43 and R44b CLOSED at ABI 22, PH-142/PH-143**; PH-142 to PH-146; **`transfer.h` PUBLISHED**; R60 CLOSED by the gameplay lane, R61 two factory rows in ascent.js raised for other lanes, **R62 CLOSED, the trap is now a refusal**; R63 the ascent leg is Forge-only by refusal; PH-147 to PH-153; **R65 docking does not exist, routed to Admin**; R66 the finite-burn residue has no trim pass; **R67 the station is frozen so nothing can rendezvous with it, physics owns it and it needs an Admin ruling**; PH-154 to PH-160; **the of_ap_* execution face is PUBLISHED**; **R69 CLOSED**; **R68 HALF DONE: the plan side works through the bridge, the flight side is blocked by R71 the rate gate deadlocking at dt 0.02 and R72 the handoff never firing**; **R70 does Cinder orbit, for Admin**; PH-161 to PH-162)
> Read alongside: [MASTER_PLAN](../MASTER_PLAN.md) · [AGENT_ARCHITECTURE](../AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md) · [ADMIN](ADMIN.md)

## 1. Mission
Deliver believable, stable, time-warpable orbital flight and rigid-body vehicle/structure physics — KSP-grade credibility — within the active/on-rails framework, while staying cheap enough to coexist with the factory sim.

## 2. Scope & owned subsystems
- **Orbital mechanics** — patched conics (two-body + SOI switching), analytic propagation for on-rails bodies/vessels.
- **Rigid-body vehicle physics** — multi-part craft, joints, thrust, mass/CoM, aerodynamics & drag in atmospheres.
- **Surface & character physics** — walking, vehicles on terrain, collision against world-gen terrain.
- **SOI transitions** — handing a vessel between reference frames as it crosses sphere-of-influence boundaries (with [core-engine](core-engine.md)).
- **Active↔on-rails conversion** — produce/consume orbital element sets so a vessel can "park" on rails and resume under physics.
- **Non-goals:** the coordinate/frame substrate (→ core-engine); rendering of trajectories (→ rendering/gameplay map view).

## 3. Key design decisions
| # | Decision | Rationale | Status | Date |
|---|----------|-----------|--------|------|
| PH-1 | Patched conics, **not** n-body (mirrors D-002) | Time-warp + stable orbits + cheap + deterministic | Accepted | 2026-06-14 |
| PH-2 | Full rigid-body physics only for active vessel + nearby; rest on rails | Core scalability (D-003) | Accepted | 2026-06-14 |
| PH-3 | On-rails state = classical Keplerian element set + epoch; advance via **universal-variable** Kepler solve (one path for elliptic/parabolic/hyperbolic) | Analytic propagation; warp-free; trivial save/restore; hyperbolic transfers need no separate code path | **Accepted** (Spike 2) | 2026-06-14 |
| PH-4 | ~~Use Chaos for active rigid bodies; custom integrator for orbits~~ **REVISED → HYBRID (RC-4 call): custom fixed-step symplectic integrator owns the active vessel's flight dynamics (gravity/thrust/drag/joints); Chaos retained ONLY for collision detection + contact response.** Fallback: fully-custom analytic ground contact (Chaos-free) if touchdown contact is unstable. | CE-6 removes the velocity problem but not the integration problem; UE5 LWC jitters ≥3 km so Chaos is only trustworthy near origin and would accumulate error on 600 km gravity; closed-form-consistent integrator preserves the on-rails no-drift guarantee + Phase-3 determinism; matches core-engine's predicted "partial-pass" path. | **Accepted** (Spike 2, RC-4) | 2026-06-14 |
| PH-5 | Terrain collision = world-gen's **analytic** queries (`GetGroundContact`/`RaycastTerrain`/`SampleTerrainHeight`), NOT baked per-quad meshes; baked-mesh emission deferred (Phase-1 perf option, non-breaking). | Resident-free (need ground before LOD streams), floating-origin-clean, deterministic, point-local contact. | **Accepted (Spike 2, RC-3); SUPERSEDED for the character near-field** (2026-07-05, Admin D-010): M4.3/M5.2 cook complex-as-simple PMC collision on the chunk + voxel meshes and the character physically stands on it (the float/teleport bugs died the day the capsule got real geometry). The **vessel path remains analytic** as designed; this reversal is the character near-field only. | 2026-06-14 |
| PH-7 | **The walker requires BOTH oracle answers to agree before it treats a point as rock.** `solidAt` is quantised to the 1 m cell (a cell is solid when its CENTRE is at or below the designed surface, `surface_field.h` §5) while the walkable ground is the smooth `surfaceRadius`. A point ABOVE its own column's `surfaceRadius` is therefore air for the capsule, whatever the cell says. | The two answers are the same authority (R1 holds, nothing new is derived) but they are not the same SHAPE: the solid shell is a staircase that stands up to ~0.87 m proud of the ground being walked on. Measured on ordinary terrain: the air 0.15 m above the walkable surface reads solid on **60.6% of ticks**. `resolveEmbedded` ejected the capsule along the minimum translation, which out of a just-entered cell is back through the face just crossed, i.e. exactly the 7.7 cm the player had walked. Walking returned **0.44 m per commanded metre**. Fix restores 1.00. Below the surface nothing changes, so the dig mouth (15.2 item 48) and tunnel interiors are untouched. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-9 | **`floorBelow` may never return a radius ABOVE its query.** If the query point is itself solid the capsule is embedded, which is `resolveEmbedded`'s problem, not the floor march's. | The march returned `r + 0.1` at d = 0, which is not a floor but one march step of pure lift, and the caller reads any ground above the feet as a landing. Held against a vertical face it ratchets **0.1 m every tick, 6 m/s straight up**: that is how the walker climbed 12 m out of a 10.4 m shaft at a dead-constant 0.097 m/tick (0.1 less one tick of gravity). It reads as a walkable ramp in a screenshot and as a hard-coded constant in a trace. The capsule's `free` test samples 0.15/0.9/1.65 m and nothing at the feet, so a position with the feet a few cm inside rock passes and feeds the march. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-8 | **`CAPSULE.stepUpM` = 1.1 m is the heightfield's step-up AND its wall**: a horizontal move onto ground more than that above where the feet started the tick is refused, sliding along one tangent axis first. The radial half of the move is exempt, so a jump still reaches higher ledges. 1.1 m is sized to the 1 m quantum the derived lowering moves in, so a scrape you dig is not a trap, and it matches the ledge the voxel resolver already climbs. It never fires on ordinary ground: real relief there is 0.034 m per tick. 0.6 m was tried first and cost `tunnelwalk.js` 12 of its 22 dig strikes, because one strike digs a crater deeper than 0.6 m and the player could no longer step out of their own. | The heightfield path had NO wall: `gap <= 0` meant "landing", so one tick's 7.7 cm of travel into a cliff snapped the capsule to the top of it. Measured: the walker climbed **12 m out of a 10.4 m shaft it had just dug**. `slopeLimitCos` cannot catch this and never could, for two reasons: it is sampled AFTER the snap (so it reads the flat ground on top of the cliff) and its rejection projects the tangential velocity onto the RADIAL up, which is orthogonal to it by construction, so it removed ~6e-7 of a 4.6 m/s velocity. It has been a no-op since it was written. Left in place as the jump gate and as the reported `slopeCos`; the wall is now `climbGate`. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-10 | **The atmosphere is ONE exponential, `rho = rho0 * exp(-h/H)`, with Forge at rho0 = 1.225 kg/m^3 and H = 5600 m, ceiling 60 km.** Isothermal, so `p/p0 == rho/rho0` and one curve serves both drag and engine lapse. Cinder is airless. | H is not invented: D-006 fixes Forge's scale height at ~5.6 km and the rendering lane's scattering model already ships `rayleighScaleM: 5.6e3` / `thicknessM: 6.0e4`. Choosing a second scale height would have made the air you fly through disagree with the air you look at, which is the five-surfaces failure wearing a different hat. This is also DW-30's "kept real" list item: density genuinely falls off, so drag and Isp genuinely change as you climb. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-11 | **Density FADES to exactly zero over the top 10 km (50 to 60 km) rather than being truncated at the ceiling.** | The raw exponential at 60 km is **2.723e-5 kg/m^3**, which is **0.072 m/s^2** on a 2 t vessel at orbital speed. A hard cut therefore puts a step in the force at exactly the altitude every player circularises at: an orbit 100 m below the line decays and one 100 m above it is eternal. The fade buys three things and costs one: density is continuous everywhere (worst measured step **3.7e-8 kg/m^3 per metre**), it is EXACTLY 0.0 above the ceiling so "am I on rails" is a boolean rather than a threshold, and the raw exponential is bit-identical below 50 km where the whole ascent drag integral lives. The cost is slightly less drag in a band the vehicle crosses in about 12 seconds. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-12 | **DW-30 item 1 implemented as: DESTABILISING pitching moment is multiplied by `AeroTuning::unstableAeroGain`, which ships at 0.0. RESTORING moment is untouched.** The branch is the sign of the static margin `(CoP - CoM) . forward`. | Three properties, all measured. **(1) It is continuous**: the moment is proportional to the static margin, so it is exactly zero where the gain switches (swept the fins through the CoM in 400 steps, worst torque jump under 6 kN·m, torque at the crossing under 6 kN·m from both sides). **(2) Fins still do real work**, because a finned vehicle is on the restoring branch and gets the full physical moment. **(3) At 0.0 the claim is a theorem, not a race between two coefficients**: rate damping alone CANNOT hold the ANGLE of a statically unstable body, it bounds the rate and lets the angle grow linearly, so the only honest ways to a bounded angle are to remove the divergence or to fly a controller, and DW-30 asks for the former. Wind tunnel, 10 deg AoA at 36.7 kPa, unstable airframe (margin +3.188 m): **gain 1.0 tumbles past 90 deg in 1.34 s and reaches 180; gain 0.0 peaks at 21.66 deg, settles there with 0.0000 deg/s left; gain 0.0 FINNED peaks at 10.00 and weathercocks back to 0.00.** | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-13 | **Aerodynamic rate damping is a real derivative, generously sized**: `M = -k * 0.5 * rho * v * CdA_normal * L^2 * omega_perp`, k = 0.25 pitch / 0.05 roll. | Real vehicles have pitch damping (a rotating body meets its own rotation as a distributed angle of attack along its length) and KSP models it thinly. It scales with rho so it does nothing in space, which is correct and is what makes a coast feel different from an ascent. It is what settles a gust rather than letting the vehicle ring. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-14 | **The centre of pressure is weighted by NORMAL-FORCE SLOPE (Barrowman), not by broadside drag area.** A nose gets `2 x` its frontal area, a constant-diameter body tube gets `0.15 x` its side profile, an aerodynamic surface gets `2.5 x` its planform. | Broadside area was tried first and is wrong in a way that matters: a 12 m tube of 16 m^2 of flank drowns out 3.7 m^2 of fin, so **four fins moved the CoP 0.87 m and left the reference rocket statically unstable**. With the correct coefficient the same four fins move it **4.117 m** and the margin flips from **+3.188 m to -0.884 m**. Broadside area answers "how hard does the air push a vehicle flying sideways"; normal-force slope answers "where does the pushing start when it is barely off axis". They are different distributions and a rocket needs both. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-15 | **Axial drag is NOT a sum over parts.** `CdA_axial = Cd(nose) * A_max + 0.004 * wetted area + unshaded radial parts`. The BROADSIDE sum stays a sum. | Parts in a stack SHADE one another: air meets the nose and then flows along the tube. Summed, the 12 m reference rocket had `Cd*A = 3.274 m^2` on a 1.227 m^2 disc, an **effective Cd of 2.67, roughly a parachute**, spending **64 kN of drag at max-q against 180 kN of thrust**. It still reached orbit, which is exactly why it needed a number rather than a look. Now **0.887 m^2, effective Cd 0.72**. Broadside is left as a sum and that is not inconsistent: flying sideways, every part is in the flow. Consequence worth keeping: a nose cone (Cd 0.12) against a bare capsule (0.55) cuts total drag by about 60%, so the part earns its place. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-16 | **The `flight.h` / `orbital.h` boundary is a BIT-EXACT reduction, not a tolerance.** Above the atmosphere ceiling with the engine shut, thrust and drag are exactly zero and `FlightSim::step` is written in the same term order as `orbital::Integrator::step`, so the two produce identical bits. `onRailsEligible()` is the published predicate. | A tolerance would let the two paths drift apart silently and would make "which one is right" unanswerable. They really are the same arithmetic, so the test asserts that. Measured: 50,000 steps of 20 ms, all six components identical. Separately, one full 2042.6 s revolution integrated at 20 ms lands within **0.62 m and 0.0003 m/s** of the conic `orbital::resume` predicts, which is the integrator's own truncation error and not a disagreement about physics. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-17 | **Staging: ONE integer per part (`PartInstance::stage`, the stage GROUP it burns in), and the KSP press sequence** (stage k drops the hardware of burn k-1 and lights burn k). `fireStage` severs the tree at each named decoupler; the decoupler and its whole subtree leave, and the decoupler's mass goes with them. | "Which stage decouples me" was tried first and is off by one from "which stage do I burn in", which broke the delta-v filter. A stage that ignited and decoupled in the same press can never be burned. One integer with one meaning makes a per-stage delta-v sum a filter over it, and stating the split rule in terms of the TREE rather than in terms of up and down means a radial decoupler holding a strap-on booster needs no special case. Mass conservation is asserted for both the stack and the two-booster case. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-19 | **The part catalogue's names and dimensions are the ART LANE's, not physics'.** `PartDef::asset` is the exact glb node name and every geometric figure is read off `tools/blender/build_rocket_parts.py` / `rocket_common.py`. Two stack classes: S = 1.25 m, L = 2.50 m, exactly 2x, `StackAdapter` between them. | Written mid-task after the art lane landed an expanded `rocket_parts.glb` under different names while this work was in flight. Publishing a taxonomy that disagrees with the shipped meshes is the two-authority failure this project has paid for five times, and a renderer looking a part up by name would simply not find it. Their reading of DW-29's "engines in two sizes" (two DIAMETERS plus an adapter) is also better than the draft's (two performance tiers on one diameter), so this is not merely a rename. A ctest asserts every stack part's diameter is EXACTLY one of the two classes (14 class S, 4 class L), because 1.24 would mate visually and leave a seam. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-18 | **`atmo::kG0 = 9.80665` is a UNIT CONVERSION and is not a gravity.** DW-18's `BodyParams::muM3S2` remains the one gravity authority; nothing in `vessel.h`/`flight.h` derives a g from anything else. | Forge's surface gravity is 9.81 m/s^2 and the Isp reference constant is 9.80665, so the two look almost identical and would be easy to conflate: that is precisely why it is written down. A rocket flown to Cinder must burn at the same Isp it burned at on the pad. The reference vehicle's pad TWR (1.6567) uses `mu/(R+h)^2` for weight and never touches kG0, and the two constants differ by 0.03% in that number, which makes it a real discriminator in a test rather than a nicety. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-26 |
| PH-20 | **Ground contact is solved at the PAD end only, with a LAUNCH CLAMP, and the landing end is DEFERRED and named.** The clamp is client-side (`web/src/sim/FlightSession.ts`), needs no `/core` change and no ABI bump: it STEPS the sim and writes the pad pose back, releasing when the throttle is up AND the measured TWR is at or above 1. Coming down, `DOWN` is an ARREST: the state freezes at the surface with no gear compression, no friction, no tip-over and no impact tolerance. | R5 said the vessel core cannot sit on anything, and the honest split is that a hold-down clamp is a fully solvable problem and a touchdown is not. **The step-and-restore shape is not a detail, it is the bug**: `FlightSim::telemetry` is written by `step` and by nothing else, so the first version, which simply did not step while clamped, read zero thrust and zero mass, hence a TWR of zero, hence refused to release, forever, while every number on the HUD looked healthy. Stepping and restoring is also what a hold-down physically does, and with the throttle shut it is free because a step with no thrust and no motion burns no propellant. Landing legs stay data; half-building contact here would have produced a landing that looks right and is not, which is this project's most expensive failure class. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-21 | **Flight uses the FOUR-PASS scaled-space rig unchanged. There is no third render mode and `Frame.vabActive` stays a boolean.** The vessel draws into `scenes.near` at floating-origin engine metres. | The bay REPLACES the four passes because a rocket on a stand and a planet at 600 km share no depth range. Flight is the opposite case and the whole point of the milestone: the vessel has to be in the same 1:1 near scene the walker is, so the rig carries it from the pad to orbit continuously. A flight pass would have been a second seam to maintain and would have deleted the feature it was meant to serve. **Measured: 37 draw calls, 107,680 triangles, frame p50 2.6 ms / p95 4.0 ms from orbit, with 0 near chunks and 102 far ones.** | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-22 | **The VESSEL BECOMES THE STREAMING OBSERVER, through a `ViewSource` router (`web/src/player/ViewRouter.ts`), not through a branch in the loop.** `VesselObserver` is a chase camera implementing the same contract the walking capsule does; boarding swaps the one object `Loop` already talks to. | `Loop` owns ORDER and must never branch on player mode. Making the rocket the observer is what makes the near-to-far handoff need NO flight-specific code at all: the origin rebases around the vessel, chunks stream to it, the regime crosses off its altitude, the sky and the shadow fit follow. Anything else would have been a second definition of "where the player is", which is the failure this project has paid for five times. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |

In the horizon frame the two keys ARE the two numbers on the ball. **(2) Rate:** 45 deg/s overshot the ribbon by 20 degrees a side, because a slew rate the torque cannot follow is not generous authority, it is a marker that lies about where the nose is going. 20 deg/s tracks it. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-24 | **The local horizon frame's `east` is `player/ViewSource.tangentFrame`'s (`Y x up`), copied and not re-derived, and stability assist holds ROLL as well as pitch and yaw.** | Two sign bugs of the same shape, both of which read perfectly healthy. `up x Y` makes the triad (east, north, up) LEFT-handed; every angle READOUT survives that unharmed because heading and pitch are computed in the same broken frame, and only a ROTATION exposes it, which is why the ascent flew 180 degrees off its own ribbon while every number agreed. A renderer reading the raw position puts the model up to a whole tick of travel from where the camera is looking, which is **38 m at 2.3 km/s against a 21 m chase distance**, i.e. the rocket entirely out of frame. On the pad the two agree exactly, so it looks perfect until it moves, and it looks like a camera bug rather than a timing one. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-26 | **PHYSICS warp, not on-rails warp: `of_fl_step_n(f, dt, n)` takes n steps of the SAME dt.** Capped at 10x in the air and 1000x in vacuum. | Warping therefore costs the integrator exactly nothing, which is why the in-air cap is the LOWER one: what it costs is CONTROL LATENCY, the stick being sampled once per block. This is not KSP's time warp, which teleports along a conic and cannot be used under thrust, so it does not touch DW-30's concession list. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-27 | **`of_vs_*` and `of_fl_*` are SEPARATE handle registries that both number from 1**, so a flight handle passed to a vessel call silently answers about the DESIGN. | PH-28 | **`FlightSession.altitudeAglM` SUBTRACTS the base offset. Adding it made a rocket **standing still on the pad read ALT AGL 19.20 m, exactly twice its own 9.6 m base offset**, and armed the touchdown arrest only once the hull was **buried by the same 19.2 m**. It is the signature failure of this codebase in one operator: the SAME wrong number fed the navball, the guidance ribbon's altitude input, the regime band and the arrest trigger, so every instrument agreed with every other. Nothing caught it because every existing assertion is RELATIVE (`liftoffAltM` is seeded from the same function; `ascent.js` asserted `aglAfter > aglOnPad + 5`) and **no test ever asserted that a rocket on the ground is at zero.** `flightabuse.js` now does. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-29 | **On the pad, ONE stage press. A second while the clamp still holds is REFUSED and says so.** | The first press lights the engine, exactly as in KSP. Measured before the guard: four presses took the reference vehicle from **11 parts to 4**. This is the single most likely wrong thing a player does in the first thirty seconds, because Space is the stage key and Space is also the jump key they have been holding all game. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-30 | **A SAVE THE WORLD CANNOT DESCRIBE IS NOT WRITTEN.** While the player is aboard, `Persist.saveSlot` refuses, counts the refusal, and the navball carries a standing amber chip saying "flight is not saved: reloading returns you to the last ground save". The slot has **no field for a vessel** and the autosave fires every 20 s from above `Gameplay.fixedStep`'s `uiOpen \|\| suspended` return, so it fires while strapped in. A slot written in orbit is a **perfectly valid GROUND state**: reload it and the base, the pack and the tunnels all come back, the player is at the scenario spawn, and the rocket they were flying never existed. Every number in that save is correct and nothing says the flight was dropped, which is the same instrument-clean wrongness as PH-28. Refusing leaves the last GROUND save on disk, which is exactly where a reload should put somebody whose flight was not saved, and the chip tells them BEFORE they reload rather than after. **Not inhibited: a vessel PARKED on the ground.** That save describes the player and the base correctly and loses only a hull that costs one key press to put back, because the DESIGN is persisted separately (`VabStore`, localStorage); inhibiting there would mean a player who rolls out and then lays track for ten minutes never autosaves again. **This is the honest fallback, not the fix**: see R13 for what real persistence needs. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-31 | **The vessel's render position is seeded from the sim on ROLL-OUT and kept synced every frame it is parked (`VesselObserver.syncToVessel`).** | `renderPos` was written only by `interpolate()`, which is reached only through `ViewRouter`, which routes to the vessel only while somebody is ABOARD. Invisible to every instrument because `distanceToVessel` reads `session.state.pos`, which was right the whole time: the walk, the range refusal and the boarding all measured correctly against a rocket nothing was drawing. `VesselView.report().drawnEngineM` now publishes where the meshes actually are, so this is a number and not a screenshot somebody has to notice. | PH-32 | **Boarding range is measured to the vessel's BASE, not to its origin.** | The origin is the top of the stack, so measuring to it adds the whole rocket's height to the distance of a player standing at its feet. With an 18 m range that means **any vehicle taller than 18 m can never be boarded**: G answers "vessel is 24 m away" from the base, walking closer cannot help, and past 200 m it rolls out a second one on top of the first. The reference fixture has 9.6 m of offset so every probe passes; **the first player who adds a tank hits a wall with no diagnostic.** `frameFor`/`midOffsetM` already knew about this offset in the camera and the range check did not, which is the two-authority shape again. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-33 | **Only the ACTIVE stage's engines draw a plume (`nextStageIndex - 1`), and the plume is NOT gated on the clamp.** | The filter was the catalogue's thrust figure alone, i.e. "is this part an engine", so **every engine on the stack burned and the upper stage flamed inside the interstage from the moment the clamp released.** It reads healthy (the plume count matches the engine count) and it becomes accidentally correct at separation when the spent parts leave the tree, so it is wrong only in the window between liftoff and staging, which is the window every launch screenshot is taken in. Dropping the clamp gate is the other half: an engine lit under a hold-down IS burning propellant, and drawing no flame there was the one moment the picture disagreed with the propellant gauge. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-34 | **A warped block may not cover more than a TENTH of the height the vehicle has left when it is closing on the ground (`web/src/sim/FlightWarp.ts`).** | The in-air cap of 10x was gated on `this.tm.inSpace`, which is the PREVIOUS tick's telemetry. Climbing, the lag is harmless. Descending it is not: the block that crosses the atmosphere ceiling still runs at up to 1000x on the strength of a sample taken in space, which at 60 km and 2 km/s is **16.7 seconds and about 33 km of re-entry taken in one blind step**, with no telemetry read and no control sample inside it. The vehicle jumps from space to deep atmosphere or straight through the ground (the arrest then snaps it back up), and `maxQPa` misses the real peak entirely because it is sampled once per block rather than once per step. Bounding by the height REMAINING fixes it **without keeping a second copy of the ceiling altitude in the client**, which would be one more place for the air the vehicle flies through to disagree with the air it is drawn in. | PH-37 | **A MANEUVER NODE IS A PLAN AND NOT AN AUTOPILOT, and the line is drawn at ARITHMETIC vs DECISIONS.** `maneuver.h` computes what a burn costs, which way to point, when to light it, how long for, and the orbit it produces. | PH-38 | **The burn's DURATION is published and the burn is LED BY HALF OF IT**, as `Plan::burn.leadS` and `timeToBurnStartS`. R6 already recorded what that costs: starting a circularisation burn AT apoapsis instead of leading it by half caps the vehicle at **121 x 33 km**. Half the duration is the first-order correction, and "burn for 42 s" and "start burning 21 s early" are the same fact where only the second is actionable. **Measured, driven in `/core` at dt 0.002 with everything else identical**: an 800 m/s / 44.2 s burn from an 80 km circular orbit, LED, lands its periapsis **0.8 m** from the prediction; the SAME burn started at the node lands it **302 m** low, 377x worse, and its apoapsis 1.95% low against the led case's 0.61%. `test_maneuver.cpp` asserts the comparison, not a threshold. **`burnFractionOfPeriod` is published with it**, because the impulsive approximation is only as good as that is small (0.0026 of a period lands 19 m out; 0.023 lands 39.5 km out) and a caller that shows a predicted orbit without showing its own error is choosing not to say. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-39 | **ONE derivation of the prograde/normal/radial triad, `orbital::basisAt`, and it lives in `orbital.h` because TWO things need it.** SAS points at those directions (`sasTarget`) and a node's handles are expressed in them (`maneuver::deltaVVector`). `SasMode` gains `Normal(5)`, `Antinormal(6)`, `RadialIn(7)`, `RadialOut(8)`, **appended**, so 0..4 still mean what the bridge, `FlightAbi.ts` and every shipped probe think. **There is deliberately no `Node` mode.** | Two constructions of "which way is normal" is the two-authority failure this project has paid for six times, and here it would show up as a node marker that is not quite where the SAS holding it points, which nothing would catch because both look plausible. A `Node` mode is unnecessary rather than merely redundant: a node's burn direction is FIXED in inertial space once the node is placed, so `Command` plus `FlightSession.commandDirection` already IS hold-node, and adding a fifth way to say "point there" would put a maneuver concept inside the attitude controller. The node's basis is evaluated **at the node**, never at the ship: on an ellipse, prograde half an orbit away is very nearly reversed, and `test_maneuver.cpp` asserts that burning prograde at apoapsis raises the PERIAPSIS, which is an assertion with a wrong answer to fail against. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-40 | **Inclination is computed against the WORLD's +Y pole in `maneuver::samplePath`, and is deliberately NOT `orbital::Elements::i`.** | `orbital.h` takes the inclination from `h.z` and the node line from `cross({0,0,1}, h)`, i.e. **+Z is its pole**, while the planet's pole is **+Y**: `cubed_sphere.h`'s `dirToLatLon` reads `dir.y` for latitude and `flight.h` spins the atmosphere about +Y. The element set is internally consistent, so `state -> elements -> state` round-trips exactly and nothing that USES it is wrong; but its `i` is an angle to a plane this planet does not have. A test pins the discriminator: the demo orbit lies in the XZ plane, so its inclination against the real pole is 0 and `Elements::i` reads **90 degrees**. Printing that beside a latitude would have been a number that looks right and is not. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |

It also answers `FlightMode.ts:7`'s standing "there is no third render mode" argument by not raising it: `vabActive` would have had to become an enum, touching `Frame.ts`, `Scenes.ts` and `CameraRig.ts` for a picture that a canvas draws better. **The atmosphere annulus is drawn because "is my periapsis inside the air" is the question the player is actually asking**, and `atmosphereCeilingM` is read from `of_atmo_space_altitude` so the line the map draws the air at is the air the vessel flies through. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |

The cockpit being hull-locked is NOT a reversal of the chase camera's decision: a CHASE view welded to a rolling hull leaves a player unable to tell which way is up, and sitting inside one the hull IS the frame of reference. KSP draws the same distinction. **Not drawing the hull was found by screenshotting**: the eye sits 1.2 m below the top of the stack and looking down at the planet put a black wedge of the vessel's own interior across two thirds of the frame. There is no IVA geometry (art lane A-11), so an empty cockpit is honest and a hull seen from the inside out is not. Left alone, one press of M would open the map AND silence the game, which is the silent-second-effect class this project keeps paying for. | PH-44 | **A NODE IS ANCHORED TO AN ABSOLUTE MISSION TIME, never to "seconds from now."** Plus two readout defects the same driven probe found: the navball's CMD marker drew `state.forward` (the nose) rather than where SAS is aiming, and a pure ROLL input dropped the vessel out of PRO into CMD. | **The node one is fatal and it looked perfect on every instrument.** `MapMode` stored `tFromNowS` and handed that same number to `/core` every frame, so the node slid along the orbit WITH the vessel and stayed a fixed distance ahead for ever: measured, **40.0 s of mission time elapsed with the countdown bit-identical at 1004.2540207730244 s and the panel's "light it in" frozen at 16:44.** Every number was self-consistent, the conic was right, the burn was right, and the burn could never be flown because the countdown could not reach zero. It is now `atS`, an absolute point on the vessel's own clock, and `tFromNowS` is derived; it is allowed to go NEGATIVE, because "you missed it" is a true readout and a clamp at zero would be a second lie. **The CMD marker one is the same shape in miniature:** a marker pinned to the reticle centre cannot disagree with the nose, so it can never show a command the vessel has not reached, and it was measured **133.7 degrees off** the instant hold-node engaged while still drawing on the crosshair. `FlightSession.commandDir` publishes the real one. **The roll one:** `slew` routed any non-zero input through `commandDirection`, so pressing roll while in PRO silently changed the mode. Third person adds nothing: `springArm` marches against `surfaceRadius` alone, so 19 m under it the arm is pinned at 0 and the TP camera IS the FP eye, measured at 0.000000 m of spread with the mouse sweeping. **The mechanism reason the hypothesis cannot be right as stated:** `editsHandle` is written once in `VoxelWorld`'s constructor and cleared only in `dispose`, so the `deep` gate cannot flicker; and under a mountain `qr` sits 15 to 19 m below its own 1.5 m threshold, so the heightfield branch is not merely losing, it is never entered. | I have been wrong about causes on this project repeatedly and measurement has corrected me every time; this is that, applied to somebody else's hypothesis and then to my own. That is stepping off a ledge, it is correct, and it is one-shot. **The one structural quantity that does vary with TIME was chased and closed by reading:** `Solid.shut` is written by `Structures.step` (door swings) and by `LaunchPad.step` (clamp release, and Reid has 9 launchpads), but both advance only while `swing != want` / `clampT != want`, so both settle and stop. There is no auto-close and no proximity trigger anywhere in `web/src/game`, so neither can cycle. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-60 | **`VoxelCollider.floorBelow` NO LONGER ANSWERS ONE NULL FOR TWO DIFFERENT WORLDS.** It returns a discriminated `FloorAnswer`: `floor` (a radius), `shaft` (the feet are in AIR with no rock within `searchM` below, so fall) or `buried` (the feet are IN ROCK past the step rung with rock all the way up, so NEVER fall). `KinematicBody` mapped both nulls to `groundR = -Infinity` and fell on both. | **This is the whole of R18 stated at the interface.** Neither function was wrong: `floorBelow` is right to refuse to answer a radius for a buried capsule (answering one is what ratified the sink twice already, GP-53 and WG-31), and `resolveEmbedded` is right to decline when no one-cell axis push frees the capsule, because its answer IS the minimum translation vector and its whole value is that it can never exceed one cell. The defect is that the caller could not tell "there is nothing under me" from "I am inside rock", and only one of those is a fall. **DW-26's rule applied to an ANSWER rather than to a predicate: when one authority must say two things, it says which.** Measured before anything was changed, at the entry tick on seed 991733: all three capsule samples solid on both solidity predicates, rock 30.14 m up to the surface with no air pocket at all, no air within 40 m below, all six one-cell exits between 0.714 and 0.989 m and **not one of them freeing the capsule**, and the nearest whole-capsule-free position **3.5 m away along +Y**; 437 ticks later, 284 m down, **nothing free within 40 m on any of the six axes**. So the one-cell bound did not want widening. The case wanted an owner. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-61 | **TWO RULES OWN THE BURIED CAPSULE, and the second is why the first terminates** ([`web/src/player/RockEscape.ts`](../../web/src/player/RockEscape.ts)). **(1) A capsule may not BECOME buried:** a tick that would end with the feet in rock, no floor and no push out is REFUSED, and the capsule keeps the position it started the tick at, losing the velocity that drove it there. **(2) A capsule that is ALREADY buried is EJECTED UP THE LOCAL RADIAL**, to the first free radius within one capsule plus one cell, and otherwise straight to its own column's `surfaceRadius`. | **Rule 1's argument is an induction and it names no rung, no cell and no seed, which is exactly why it holds at any burial depth**: the capsule starts on the surface, and every tick after that either ends legal or ends where it started, which was legal. Rule 2 exists because the WORLD can take the fallback away (the levelling tool raises terrain, PH-62; a save written before tonight can hold a player 29 km down), and **the radial is the only direction with a termination proof**: `solidForWalker` is false for every point above its own column's `surfaceRadius` (PH-7), so an upward escape is bounded by `surfaceRadius - r` from any depth, while down and sideways are bounded by nothing. That asymmetry is precisely why `resolveEmbedded`'s six axes cannot own this and this can. The one constant, capsule height plus one cell, is the distinction the answer turns on rather than a tuned window: **rock thinner than the body standing in it is a FLOOR you are sunk into and its top is where you belong; rock thicker than that is a MOUNTAIN you are inside, and the only place a mountain lets you stand is on it.** It also bounds the deep case at twelve grid steps instead of the 116,000 a 29 km legacy save would march. **The two rules separated cleanly under measurement, which is the evidence they are two rules and not one with a patch:** the seed-991733 cave drive enters the buried state on **126 of 5976 ticks and rule 1 owns every one of them (0 ejects, 0 unrescued)**, while every levelled burial is owned by rule 2 with **exactly one eject of exactly the raise**. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-62 | **R18 IS NOT A CURIOSITY OF ONE DIG ON ONE SEED. IT IS ONE PRESS OF THE LEVELLING KEY ON FLAT GROUND.** `probes/burylevel.js` levels a pad to `ground + N` with the player standing in it, which buries them by N, and sweeps N. | R18 was found by a jittered dig that happened to bury the walker, on 1 of 3 seeds, and a fix held only to that run is a fix fitted to a sample. The levelling tool is allowed to RAISE terrain and `LevelAction.ts:180` already says out loud that levelling raises the ground under your feet; nothing anywhere said what happens to the person standing there. **Measured on the pre-fix build, flat ground, no seed in the probe: a 3 m raise and a 12 m raise each drop the player 508.052 m through the world in ten seconds, 0 of 600 ticks grounded, 0 pushes**, and at ten seconds they are still accelerating; the same probe run twelve seconds longer reports 728 to 738 m. **After PH-60/PH-61, at raises of 2, 3, 5, 8 and 12 m: one eject each, of 2.214 / 3.004 / 5.004 / 8.004 / 12.004 m, the player grounded on the new pad, and 0.000000 m of spread over the following 600 ticks with 600/600 grounded.** A raise of 1 m buries nobody and fires nothing, because it is inside the walker's own 1.5 m deep threshold and the heightfield still owns the ground there. The 20 m row is refused by the level tool itself and is excluded by the probe rather than counted as a pass. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-63 | **R18b IS REFUTED: a capsule embedded in a structural wall is NOT stuck, it walks out.** The recovery is `StructureBody.resolveStep`'s escape hatch, which accepts any destination when the capsule is already not free at its start. | Driven rather than read (`probes/wallstuck.js`), because the previous audit reasoned from `deckUnder` alone and never exercised `resolveStep`, and I relayed it without re-deriving it. **Placement genuinely will bury you**: `StructurePlacement.resolveTarget` refuses on five grounds (built, too high, unsupported, uneven, cost) and consults the capsule on none of them, so free placement (the shipped B key) aimed at your own feet puts a wall centre **0.062819 m** from your axis with the capsule column **1.75 of 1.75 m** inside solid. **What happens next is nothing bad:** 600 stationary ticks at **0.000000 m** of spread and 600/600 grounded, on a deck and on bare terrain both, and walking out covers **8.926664 m in 2 s in all four directions with blockedByBuild 0/480**, which is unobstructed walking at the full 4.6 m/s. Note the shipped wall is **3.50 m**, not the 4.00 m storey the audit quoted; the premise survives that correction because 3.50 m is just as far outside `deckUnder`'s 0.55 m rise window. **The real defect the claim was masking is the converse one, and it is worth more than the claim was:** while ANY capsule sample is inside ANY structural solid, ALL structural collision is off, not just for the part you are in, so an embedded player walks through every other wall and every closed door at full speed. See R20. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-27 |
| PH-64 | **THE VESSEL REGISTRY IS THE ONE ANSWER TO "WHERE IS THIS VESSEL", and the live `/core` flight handle is a CACHE of the promoted record rather than a second copy** ([`web/src/sim/VesselRegistry.ts`](../../web/src/sim/VesselRegistry.ts)). A vessel IS a record with a stable, never-reused `id`. At most one record is PROMOTED, meaning it is the one the client's single `FlightSession` is carrying. `saveVessels` calls the sync hook as its FIRST STATEMENT, so the record is brought up to date from the live sim before it is serialised, with no caller able to forget. | **DW-26 applied to an object instead of to a query.** Before this there were two answers and neither was written down: a rolled-out rocket existed as a live flight handle and NOWHERE ELSE, not in the slot, not in a list, not under an id, so closing the tab lost it in silence (R12), and `rollOut` destroyed the previous one as its opening statement. The reason the id is monotonic and never reused, unlike a `/core` handle: `Registry::add` in `of_core_api.cpp` scans for the first freed slot and hands the same integer back, so a reused id would silently alias one vessel onto another **the first time a save file named one**, which is PH-27's handle trap with a disk behind it. Sync-before-serialise is the mechanism half: it makes "you cannot save a vessel in a place it is not" a property of the code rather than of somebody's memory. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-65 | **A RECORD IS IN EXACTLY ONE OF THREE MODES AND `/core`'S OWN PREDICATE DECIDES WHICH. PARKED** (on the ground or clamped: its place is a position and it does not change). **RAILS** (on a conic, nothing simulating it: its place is NINE NUMBERS AND A CLOCK, and there is **no stored position at all**). **FROZEN** (in flight but not on-rails eligible, i.e. in the air or under thrust: held exactly at the state vector it had). The discriminator is `of_fl_on_rails_eligible` and never a client-side altitude test. **A rails record NEVER RE-FITS its elements**; advancing changes only the time asked for. | Three separate arguments. **(1) The discriminator.** `onRailsEligible` is true exactly where `flight.h` and `orbital.h` produce identical bits (PH-16), so the boundary at which a vessel becomes propagatable IS the boundary at which the two integrators become the same arithmetic. A client-side ceiling test would have been a second copy of the atmosphere. **(2) No stored position on a rails record**, because a stored one is the second authority this whole design exists to delete, and it would go stale the moment the clock moved. **(3) Never re-fitting is what makes the answer deterministic**, and it is measured rather than asserted: re-parking at every one of 3000 steps lands **8.98e-10 m and 1.53e-11 m/s** away from the single jump, so the two paths are NOT bit-identical and the choice is load-bearing rather than stylistic. Fit once and only the time moves, and one jump of an hour equals 216,000 jumps of a tick **bit for bit** (asserted with `==`, not a tolerance, in `core/tests/test_physics.cpp` and again in the browser). **FROZEN is the honest one and it is not a cheat:** a vessel under drag or under thrust is not a conic, so the only two available answers are "keep integrating it", which is the cost that leaving it was meant to avoid, and "hold it", which is what a closed tab does anyway. Holding it is therefore the truthful reading of a browser reload. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-66 | **ABI 18: THREE EXPORTS, `of_fl_set_propellant`, `of_orb_park`, `of_orb_resume`.
