Restoring a save the same way gets the right HARDWARE and **FULL TANKS**, i.e. free delta-v on every reload, and every instrument afterwards reads healthy, which is this project's most expensive defect class. The clamp is the safety property and it exists because **a save file is untrusted input**: without it a hand-edited slot puts 10^9 kg in a 2150 kg tank and the integrator flies that mass. The worst a corrupt slot can now do is a full tank, which one key press in the bay already produces. **The conic pair takes no handle** because a vessel on rails is not a `FlightSim`, it is nine numbers, and requiring a live flight handle to advance one would mean keeping alive exactly the object being retired; this is the shape `of_mn_*` already takes (PH-37). **`FlightSim::park`/`resume` are NOT exported**, because a flight handle that could park itself would be a second answer to "is this vessel on rails" living inside the object whose retirement is the question. Purely additive: `expected.json` moved by exactly one field, `"abi": 16 -> 18`, and no pinned value moved. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-67 | **THE SAVED FORM IS THE REGISTRY RECORD, not a second schema, and it is stamped at the `SaveGame.writeSlot` CHOKE POINT rather than in `Persist.snapshot`. `SAVE_VERSION` does NOT move. PH-30's save refusal is RETIRED.** The design travels as `VesselDesign`'s own `DesignJson`; the staging state travels as `fired` and is restored by REPLAYING `stage` that many times; the fuel travels per part, keyed by design-part INDEX and not by handle. | **The record already had to decide what a vessel is in order to answer "where is it", so a save format that re-decided would be a second answer to the same question in a file nobody reads until a reload goes wrong.** The only field dropped is `stampedTick`, a reference into a loop clock that restarts at zero on every page load and is therefore meaningless on disk; restoring it as -1 reads as "the world was not running, so it did not move", which is exactly right. **The choke point is GP-102's precedent and its argument transfers verbatim**: every write funnels through `writeSlot`, so a snapshot path written next month carries the vessel without knowing it exists, whereas a field filled in by `Persist.snapshot` is a field the second snapshot path (`pagehide`, the debug save) forgets. `Persist.ts` is untouched by this lane, which also keeps it out of a file another lane had already pushed over its line cap. **Replaying `stage` rather than adding a `nextStageIndex` setter is deliberate**: `fireStage` is a pure function of the tree (PH-17), so k presses reproduce exactly the hardware k presses produced, and a setter would have let a save claim a stage index the tree does not agree with. **Keying fuel by design index rather than by handle** is what survives a save at all, because handles are `/core`'s and are reallocated on rebuild. **PH-30's refusal is retired rather than reworded**: it existed because the slot had no field for a vessel, and it now has one. Retiring it is also load-bearing rather than tidy, because with the refusal in place no autosave can fire while the player is in orbit and the orbital reload could never have been proven. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-68 | **THE PLAYER'S BODY PARKS COHERENTLY WHEN CONTROL IS ELSEWHERE. It is not stepped, and its position is RECORDED as an anchor that survives a reload** ([`web/src/app/PlayerAnchor.ts`](../../web/src/app/PlayerAnchor.ts), `SaveSlot.player`). The anchor is read off `ViewRouter.walker`, never off the active view source. **This closes R13.** | The brief asked for a decision either way and said that leaving it unstated is what is not defensible. **Keep-simulating was rejected on three counts.** (1) It is a second answer to "where is the player": a stepped body can fall, slide, be ejected up the radial (PH-61), drown or be moved by a structure **while nobody is looking at it**, and the first anyone learns of it is on coming back. (2) Nothing observes it, so nothing can catch it going wrong; R20 is already exactly this shape, a player inside a wall reading healthy on every instrument, and an unwatched simulated body is that with no camera on it. (3) It costs a full collision step per tick for a body whose only job is to be somewhere when you get back. **The client was already doing the parking half by accident and that is the state the brief said not to leave it in:** `ViewRouter.step` delegates only to the active source, so the walking `Controller` is simply never stepped while aboard, and `Systems.ts` gates the dig and level rays off `aboard` because its aim ray is stale. That behaviour was inherited, never decided. What is added is the half that was missing: the parked position is WRITTEN DOWN. **Reading the anchor off the WALKER and not off the router is the one subtle line**: `ViewRouter.state()` reports whichever source is live, so calling it while strapped in returns the ROCKET's latitude and altitude and a save in orbit would record a body 80 km up. **R13 closes as a side effect**, and with a vessel now persisting it stops being an annoyance and becomes an incoherence: a rocket in orbit above a body teleported half a planet away. Measured before: in at lat 1.613 lon 107.822, back at 2.000 / 144.000. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-69 | **THE CONTROL HANDOFF IS PUBLISHED, NOT BUILT (`ResumeBoot.ts` §5), and `releaseControl` IS NOT `FlightMode.disembark`.** The seam: a vessel is named by `VesselRecord.id`, selected by `promoteVessel(flight, id, tick)`, enumerated by `registry.list()`, located without being promoted by `stateOf(...)`, and `mayLeave(rec)` refuses to abandon a FROZEN one. | **The two verbs are genuinely different and conflating them was a real defect, found by driving and not by reading.** `disembark` is CLIMBING OUT: it teleports the body to the vessel's own lat and lon, and it is refused above 2 m/s because a walker cannot survive stepping out at 2.3 km/s. Both are correct for a key press and both are wrong for a handoff, where the body is at the pad two hundred kilometres below and where **the only vessel you may walk away from is one moving fast enough to stay up**, i.e. the refusal is exactly backwards. Measured before the fix: `demote` called `disembark`, the speed guard refused, **`aboard` stayed true, `refusals` went 0 to 1, and the session was destroyed underneath it**, leaving the player strapped into a vessel that no longer existed. `reload.mjs` already carries a standing assertion against precisely that state, which is how well known this failure is here. **`mayLeave` is the other half and it is a design statement:** leaving a FROZEN vessel would produce a rocket hanging motionless mid-ascent for as long as you are away, which is neither a simulation nor a story, so the refusal is "cut the engine and coast above 60 km first", which is also exactly the skill DW-29 says reaching orbit is meant to teach. FROZEN still EXISTS because closing a tab is not a choice the game gets to refuse; `mayLeave` governs only the voluntary handoff. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-70 | **THE DESIGN SNAPSHOT IS VERIFIED, NOT TIMED.** Before any staging, the flight's own part list is a verbatim copy of the design's (`of_fl_create` deep-copies), so the part ids in order must match exactly or the snapshot is REFUSED and counted in `refusedSnapshots`. | The obvious implementation is "snapshot at roll-out", which is a claim about WHEN and therefore a claim nothing checks; the bay's design handle keeps being edited afterwards, so a late snapshot records a rocket the player is flying a different version of, and nothing downstream could ever tell. Checking the snapshot against the craft it claims to describe turns a timing hope into an assertion. **Refusing is the right failure**: a vessel with no design cannot be restored, and a counter a probe can read is strictly better than saving a plausible wrong rocket. It also bought the per-tick hook a home with no shared file touched: the check rides `FlightPad.stepPadClamps`, the one flight call the loop already makes whether or not anybody is aboard, and it is one integer compare on the common path. Measured `refusedSnapshots: 0` in every driven run of both probes and all three reload phases. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-71 | **A PROMOTED VESSEL WITH NOBODY ABOARD IS NEVER STEPPED, SO IT IS PRIMED WITH ONE `stepClamped` AT PROMOTE AND AT ROLL-OUT.** Parked vessels only (CLAMPED and DOWN), because only they are genuinely stationary. Guarded by [`probes/clamprestore.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/clamprestore.js). | **This is PH-20's launch-clamp bug with a new cause, and it was found by Admin ASKING rather than by a probe failing**, which is worth recording on its own. `FlightSim::telemetry` is written by `step` and by nothing else; `FlightSession.step` is reached only through `VesselObserver.step`; `ViewRouter` drives that only while `aboard`. So a rocket restored onto its pad at boot could have published `massKg 0`, hence `currentTwr()` 0, hence a clamp that can never release, with every HUD number reading healthy. **Reid spent a large part of a day behind exactly that screen** and GP-73 to GP-76 fixed it; handing it back with a different cause would have been worse than not restoring the vessel at all. **Driven, not reasoned about, and the answer was no**: a restored vessel reads **massKg 9845** and the clamp releases at **TWR 1.9457**. **The obvious repair does not work and the reason is worth knowing**: `of_fl_step` guards `!(dt > 0.0)` and returns 0, so a zero-length step is silently a no-op and the first version of this fix did nothing while looking correct. What works is the clamp's OWN mechanism (PH-20): step, then write the pad pose back. It is applied to parked vessels only, because zeroing 2.3 km/s to make an instrument read would be destroying the orbit to fix the gauge. **And the probe found the same defect where nobody had looked: a rocket that has just been ROLLED OUT and never boarded read `massKg 0` for the whole walk over to it.** It never blocked anything, because boarding steps the vessel long before anyone throttles up, which is precisely why it had never been seen. One integer says it: the fix is the same call. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |

Markers on the `VesselMode` enum, on the active/on-rails section and inside the on-rails branch of `step()`, each naming the client file that replaces it. | **Kept rather than deleted because the FILE is not dead, only the API is duplicated.** `SimWorld` is reachable from two green suites and `journey_dump`, and those cover the SOI crossing, the star to Forge to Cinder frame graph, the floating origin and a factory ticking under flight on one clock, none of which the client has. Deleting the header to remove a duplicate vessel API would have taken that coverage with it. **The markers are at the SYMBOLS and not only in the file banner, because the reader this protects arrives by grep and never sees a banner.** The section marker also records why this one could not be promoted even if someone wanted to: it holds a single `state_` and a single `elements_`, so it cannot represent many vessels with at most one active, which is the whole problem. **One line for the corroboration, per the ruling:** its on-rails branch and PH-65 independently reached the same rule, elements held fixed and only the time moving. That is a reason to trust the RULE and not a reason to keep two copies of the code. **R27 CLOSED.** | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-73 | **FOUR BOUNDS IN `test_flight.cpp` NOW ASSERT THEIR OWN MEASURED RESIDUE TIMES A MARGIN OF 2.0, AND THE MARGIN IS DERIVED.** Was `posErrM < 5.0` against a residue of **0.00570342259015 m**. | **A control that cannot fail is not a control, and this was the dominant failure catalogued in [`INSTRUMENTS.md`](../web/INSTRUMENTS.md).** The integrator could have degraded by two orders of magnitude and the test would still have passed. The three siblings were worse: the apoapsis bound was **6e8** times its own **8.03265720606e-09 m** deviation and the eccentricity bound **1e8** times its **7.02847016676e-14**. **The margin is derived rather than picked for looking safe.** The residue is DETERMINISTIC (repeated runs agree on every printed digit), so the margin covers only the same source built by a different compiler: instruction selection and libm differences, about 1 ulp per operation over 102132 steps, so below **2.3e-11** relative even if it accumulated linearly. The leading truncation term goes as dt squared, so the cheapest real regression (a doubled step) moves the residue by **4x**. The usable window is therefore [1+2.3e-11, 4) and **2.0 is its log-scale middle**. **Proved it has teeth rather than asserting it**: doubling the step to 0.04 fails three of the four, the apoapsis at 2.49e-08 against a bound of 1.61e-08. Reverted, 843 checks green, full ctest **35/35**. If a compiler change ever trips these, re-measure and restate the residue; do NOT widen the margin back into decoration. **R24 CLOSED.** | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-74 | **`reload.mjs`'s THREE PAD-OCCUPANCY ROWS ARE SPLIT BY MODE; `recover` KEEPS THEM CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER.** The `leave` side asserts the mirror image against the BEFORE measurement rather than a constant. `LaunchPadSave`'s stale clamp rationale corrected in place. | **Both modes stopped having the same right answer the moment vessels persisted.** "Nothing came back" is still the pass condition for `recover` and is now the FAILURE condition for `leave`. Split rather than deleted per Admin: the recover rows are the only thing between us and a pad that resurrects a recovered rocket, and deleting them to turn `leave` green would have thrown away the control the runner is built around. **Two things are deliberately NOT asserted for `leave`: `flightLive` and the pad's `holding`.** Neither has been measured across a `leave` reload and asserting an unmeasured expectation is how a control encodes a bug as the spec, which is PH-73's defect class exactly. The clamp row survives for BOTH modes in its mode-independent half: a reloaded pad is never caught mid-swing. **`LaunchPadSave`'s header said the clamp state is not saved BECAUSE the flight it was released for is not in the save either; both halves of that premise are now false.** The behaviour is right and stays, on a narrower reason: `clampT` lives on the pad and not on the vessel, so shut is the only value that cannot contradict the vessel row beside it. The rotted reasoning is kept VISIBLE rather than deleted, because a correct behaviour whose justification has failed is what the next reader removes. **UNVERIFIED and deliberately so** (see R28). **R25 CLOSED.** | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-75 | **R28 CLOSED: the `leave` branch is DRIVEN, and the two assertions held out of it are now asserted FROM MEASUREMENT.** Both modes green against HEAD. Against HEAD both modes exit **0**. Measured for `leave`: `flightLive true`, `aboard false`, `parts 7` against 7 before, pad `holding true, clampT 0, releasing false`. **The correction: `holding` was never mode-dependent.** I had put it in the recover-only set on the guess that a pad with a vessel back on it might report differently; it does not, both modes come back identically, so the row moved BACK to being shared and is stronger there than it was as two copies. That is the value of refusing to assert it the first time: had I guessed it into the `leave` branch as `false` it would have passed nothing and hidden the fact that the pad half is mode-independent. **The `leave` rows are provably not vacuous**: the recover run's own measured values (`live false`, `parts 0`) fail both of them, so the pair discriminates the two modes rather than merely passing. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |
| PH-76 | **THE CONTROL HANDOFF: `leaveVessel` / `resumeControl` / `takeControlRemote`, and the gap was NOT persistence.** New in [`FlightMode.ts`](../../web/src/app/FlightMode.ts) (`takeControlRemote`), [`FlightVessels.ts`](../../web/src/app/FlightVessels.ts) (`leaveVessel`, `resumeControl`), driven by [`probes/handoff.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/handoff.js) and `vesselreload.mjs --phase=handoff`. | **`board()` routes to `climbIn()` only inside `BOARD_RANGE_M` (18 m), so a vessel in orbit could NEVER be re-entered however well it was saved.** That was the whole gap; the persistence work (PH-64 to PH-75) had already done the rest. `board()` and the range gate are untouched, because walking up to a rocket on the ground must keep its check: what exists now is a SECOND explicit door for the case where distance is not the question. **`resumeControl` refuses to half-seat** (if `promoteVessel` returns false it seats nobody). **`leaveVessel` calls `syncPromoted` BEFORE consulting `mayLeave`, and that order is load-bearing rather than tidy**: `makeRecord` stamps `mode 'parked'` at roll-out and only `syncPromoted` ever updates it, so an unsynced guard would have waved a rocket under full thrust out of the world. **Driven on a clean HEAD archive with only these files overlaid**, so it depends on no other lane's tree. Across a real `page.reload()`: fuel **882.7804658633409 kg** before and after, **difference 0**; pose `fwd`/`right` bit-identical to the last digit; conic **a 724925.1064716058 m, e 0.06228720022942358**. **The number that proves the mechanism is `distanceToVesselM 417995.3` against `boardRangeM 18`**: the old path could not have produced that seat. **The refusal discriminates rather than decorates**: a frozen vessel refuses with `whyNotLeave`'s published sentence and `aboard`, `live`, `records`, `promotedId` and `demotions` are identical before and after the refused call, and cutting the throttle at the same altitude flips the record to `rails` and the same call then succeeds. `--phase=all` (pad, ascent, orbit, handoff) passes with zero page failures. `MapView.ts` deliberately untouched. | **Accepted** | 2026-07-28 |

| PH-91 | **"DOWN" IS THE PLANET'S RADIAL, IT IS DERIVED PER TICK FROM THE FEET, AND THERE IS NO OVERRIDE ANYWHERE. There is no up field, no parameter and no per-region hook. **Overriding it is NOT a small change and the reason is `deckUnder`**: the floor is found by solving where the line `t -> dir * t` THROUGH THE PLANET CENTRE exits a box, so the returned `t` IS a radius. A local up that is not the radial breaks that parameterisation outright, and `climbGate`, `slopeGate`, `escapeRock` and the voxel collider all carry the same assumption. **So the conventional answer is also the free one**: radial gravity is good fiction for a nadir-pointing station and costs nothing. A SPIN habitat is the expensive option, not the cheap one, because it needs a non-radial up plus a rotating frame; it is refused here, not deferred. Ceilings get nothing: the only thing above the feet is `free()`, three POINT samples at 0.15 / 0.9 / 1.65 m (`CAPSULE_SAMPLES_M`), **0.75 m apart, with no capsule radius involved at all**. A slab thinner than that gap fits BETWEEN two samples, so there is a band of feet heights at which nothing is inside it. **Measured with a thickness control, which is what turned a wrong guess into a result**: at 0.3 m the player jumps clean through and ends standing on the ROOF at local y 5.119928 (one apex, plus a second jump taken from the roof); at 0.8 m, just over the gap, the rise stops dead at **0.849904 m, which is exactly `2.5 - 1.65`**, the height where the topmost sample meets the underside. **The instrument was wrong twice before it was right and both errors were caught by magnitude, per the standing rule.** First a quaternion sign put the corridor upside down, and every assertion still passed because a slab upside down is still a floor: the only tell was `deckR - rStation = 0.501221`, one deck thickness. Then a ceiling-contact height was reported as an apex, giving a jump speed of 2.44 m/s. The fixed probe recovers **4.029158 m/s from the free trajectory against the authored `CAPSULE.jumpSpeedMps = 4.0`**, a constant no probe can read, which is the validation. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-94 | **THE STATION IS TWO SYSTEMS COMPOSED, NOT ONE WIDENED: its ORBIT is a `VesselRecord` on a conic and its INTERIOR is a set of `Solid`s in the shared `StructureBodies`.** New [`SpaceStation.ts`](../../web/src/game/SpaceStation.ts), installed from `Boot.ts` AFTER `resumeWorld`, with `?station=0` as the one-binary control. 400 km circular, `a = 1000000.0000000002`, `e = 1.3185561425635388e-16`, 1879.255172 m/s. | A vessel record cannot describe an interior (there is no asset field; a vessel's mesh is always derived per-part from the `/core` catalogue, and `takeControlRemote` only swaps the CAMERA and leaves the body parked) and a solid set cannot describe an orbit. Neither was widened to pretend otherwise. What the record buys for nothing is the map marker, the selectable orbit line, the panel row and the `writeSlot` save path. **The elements come from `/core`'s own `of_orb_park` through `fitConic` and are NOT written out by hand**, which for a circular orbit looks like pointless ceremony and is exactly why it is refused: a second Kepler fit in TypeScript is a second physics, and the day they disagree the map draws one orbit and the propagator flies another. Install order is load-bearing: after `resumeWorld`, so a restored world ADOPTS its saved record and only a genuinely new one mints. Installing first would mint a second station on every load and the two would share an orbit. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-95 | **THE INTERIOR IS FROZEN IN THE BODY FRAME, AND THE RECORD IS NEVER STAMPED, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THE FREEZE HONEST RATHER THAN A SECOND AUTHORITY.** | The freeze itself is forced: `KinematicBody.step` integrates an ABSOLUTE body-frame position and there is no carrier-frame term in the signature nor anywhere to put one, so a floor at 7.5 km/s leaves the player behind at **125 m per tick**. The subtler half is the clock. `VesselRegistry.clockAt` returns `rec.clockS` unchanged while `stampedTick` is -1, so an unstamped record never advances along its conic: nothing asks, so it never moves, which is PH-65's rule taken to its limit. The interior's frozen pose and `stateOf`'s derived position are therefore **the same number by construction and cannot drift**, which is the two-authority trap DW-26 exists to refuse. Measured as an assertion rather than asserted as a design: `stationwalk.js` P2 bisects the WALKER's own collision predicate for the floor and compares it against a Kepler solve off the conic, two entirely separate authorities, and gets **deltaM 0 and standMinusConicM 0**. **What is given up, stated: the ground does not slide past below.** PH-87 already established planet rotation is purely cosmetic (`bodySpinRadS` 0.0), so nothing dynamical couples to the omission; it is a missing view out of the window and not a missing physics. Freezing also collapses R47's rebase exposure: a walker inside a frozen station rebases at the WALKING rate, ~1200x less often than the 1.9/s a flown station would cost, and 60/s under warp. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-96 | **THE STATION IS WALKABLE AND ITS FLOOR IS WHERE ITS ORBIT SAYS IT IS.** New [`probes/stationwalk.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/stationwalk.js): stand 204/204 ticks grounded and `onDeck` with spread **0**, walker floor vs conic **0**, a 200 m sideways negative control that falls 15.98 m with 0 grounded ticks, and a 27.33 m walk out of the hub THROUGH THE DOORWAY (`alongLocalZM 27.326667` past the 6 m hub face) staying at `acrossLocalXM 0.00001`. | The doorway assertion is the one that earns its place: the door is a GAP between two wall boxes, the same three-box trick `col_Door_Jamb*` uses, and hulling it shut is the one authoring mistake that makes an interior unreachable while reading perfectly in a screenshot. Nothing but a walk through it can catch that. **The instrument was wrong once more and the correction is the finding**: the first run called a 0.000373 m spread along the corridor a failure against a bare 1e-4 epsilon. It is not a defect, it is geometry. **A flat deck is not a LEVEL deck on a round world**: the walker's floor is where the RADIAL leaves the plane, and a radial `d` metres from the centre exits at `sqrt(R^2+d^2)`, i.e. `d^2/2R` above the middle, which at 27.33 m on a 1e6 m radius is **0.000373 m exactly**. The assertion is now that PREDICTION and the residual is **0**. An epsilon would have called correct geometry a bug; this is the third time in two days that a threshold hid a physical quantity. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-97 | **IT SURVIVES A REAL `page.reload()`, AND WHAT SURVIVES IS NINE NUMBERS AND A CLOCK RATHER THAN A BOX LIST.** New [`stationreload.mjs`](../../web/tools/smoke/stationreload.mjs), the physics lane's fifth standalone real-reload runner. All nine elements bit-identical, derived position moved **0 m**, deck radius unmoved, clock unadvanced, `stampedTick` still -1, still walkable, player stands at the same radius. **Negative control `--control=1` wipes the slot and the second boot MINTS instead of adopting**, inverting exactly the field the proof turns on. | The interior is DERIVED from the record on every boot and is never itself saved, so there is exactly one thing on disk and the box list cannot drift from the orbit it hangs on. **The audit that forced a new runner is worth more than the runner**: `probes/decksink.js`'s leg called "reload" is `await of.save(); await of.load()`, an IN-PAGE round trip that never tears the document down, so it proves a serialise/deserialise re-adopts into the live collision set and proves nothing about boot order, world generation or the player anchor. **And no probe can ever do better**: a probe is one `page.evaluate`, and a real reload destroys the context that evaluate is running in. `reload.mjs` was considered and refused because its phase 2 is a fixed inline evaluate with no way to run a probe after the reload, which this proof needs (it re-bisects the walker's predicate on the far side). Two harness findings routed rather than taken: `reload.mjs` needs a `--verify=probes/x.js` flag, and unlike `run.mjs` it **silently discards unknown flags**, which is the failing-in-the-flattering-direction shape `run.mjs` line 261 warns about. This runner copies the guard rather than the omission, and its guard exits 2 on a typo. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |

| PH-98 | **APPARENT GRAVITY IS NOT GRAVITY, AND THE PREVIOUS PASS'S "ARTIFICIAL GRAVITY FOR FREE AT THE LOCAL INVERSE-SQUARE VALUE" (PH-91, `SpaceStation.ts:216`) IS WITHDRAWN.** True of the FIELD STRENGTH, false of what an occupant feels. A station at 400 km is in FREEFALL: it accelerates toward the planet at exactly the local g and so does everything inside it, so nothing inside has any weight. The model is ONE SUBTRACTION, `apparent = g(where I am) - a(the frame I ride in)`, published as the `GravityField` port ([`player/GravityPort.ts`](../../web/src/player/GravityPort.ts)) and implemented by [`game/GravityVolumes.ts`](../../web/src/game/GravityVolumes.ts). `PlanetBody.gravityAccel` remains the SOLE gravity authority and this port never computes one: it returns `trueG + sum(deltas)`. | On the terrain the carrier term is zero, so every existing number is bit-identical and `probes/zerog.js` Z0 asserts that with `===` on the raw f64 (9.65912619663363 both sides, 0 volumes in reach). The frozen station of PH-94 is dynamically a TOWER ON A 400 KM PILLAR, which is the one thing an orbit is not, and Reid caught it from the description alone. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-99 | **THE WALKER DOES NOT DEGRADE INTO A FLOATING BODY. Measured in UNMODIFIED code: at 3.2% gravity it STOPS DEAD in one second and has EXACTLY ZERO descent authority.** `KinematicBody.step`'s move term is `d = wantVelocity - haveVelocity` clamped to `accel * dt`, i.e. a VELOCITY SERVO, which on the ground is friction and in freefall is a lie with no mechanism. So freefall gets a thrust model in [`player/ZeroG.ts`](../../web/src/player/ZeroG.ts): `v += dir * thrustAccel * dt` with a GOVERNOR (declines to push past `maxSpeedMps`, never slows you), no drag, and contact with structure INELASTIC on the radial axis as well as the tangent. | **`probes/zerog.js` Z1b, driven at `gravityScale(0.0325)` which is deliberately just ABOVE `ZEROG.standG` so the ORIGINAL code path answers: thrust speed 4.6 m/s, speed after 1 s of coast 0, after 3 s 0, and `shiftSamples: [0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]`.** Z1a shows the same servo in fully shipped conditions (mid-air at full gravity, key released: 5.89 to 4.12 m/s with nothing touching the player). The one confound was found and killed: holding Space DOES move you 10.5 m radially in the old model, but that is a ballistic JUMP re-firing on each landing, not authority, and reading it as authority would have been the flattering answer. Descent is the question with no confound, because no key exists for it. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-100 | **A GENERATOR IS DEFINED AS CANCELLING THE CANCELLATION AND PUBLISHES NO MAGNITUDE OF ITS OWN.** Two volumes per station: FREEFALL (subtract the carrier's acceleration) and GENERATOR (add it back, while powered). Fully inside both, the deltas are `-carrierG` and `+carrierG` and sum to exactly 0.0, so `apparentAt` returns the caller's `trueG` BIT FOR BIT. | A generator with a tunable magnitude would be a SECOND opinion about how hard the planet pulls, and the day somebody tuned it the deck would stop agreeing with the tower PH-90 measured. As defined, every figure taken under PH-90 to PH-97 carries over unchanged and none has to be re-taken. **Measured, `zerog.js` Z4: `carrierG` 3.5316, `trueG` 3.531600000000002, `apparentG` 3.531600000000002, `restoredExactly: true`, asserted with `===` and not a tolerance.** Unpowered, Z5: `apparentG` exactly 0 on the deck, player floating, `grounded` false, `onDeck` still true. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-101 | **THE MOVEMENT MODEL IS THRUST PLUS INELASTIC CONTACT, AND HANDRAILS ARE ART RATHER THAN A SYSTEM.** Always-available 6DOF suit thrust at 1.5 m/s^2 governed to 4.0 m/s (deliberately under the 4.6 m/s walk, so a powered deck is genuinely faster and gravity reads as an upgrade); the radial axis goes on Space and Shift, which have nothing else to do in freefall, so NO binding is added and the controls screen needs no new row. **You stop by hitting something**: `resolveStep` already reported `blocked` and the walker already zeroed the tangent, and the only addition is zeroing the RADIAL half, which gravity used to own. | Of Reid's three options, drift-with-no-control is realistic and miserable (leaving a hatch would be a death sentence) and an unlimited jetpack makes gravity generation pointless. Handholds are the interesting middle, but authored rails would have made this wait on an asset AND stranded anyone who let go mid-module. **Catching any surface is what a suited human actually does with their arms**, it needs no grab key and no authored geometry, and it makes a rail an affordance saying "stopping is easy here" rather than a mechanic. **Measured, Z7: 3.41 m/s into the hub wall goes to 0 with `blockedByBuild` true, stopped at `acrossM -5.999967` against a wall at -6.** Deferred and named: a propellant budget, which is the right eventual scarcity and needs gameplay's resource system. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-102 | **THE FREEFALL REGION IS BOUNDED AT 120 m AND THAT IS A LABELLED CONCESSION, NOT A DERIVATION.** In reality a body leaving a station keeps its orbital velocity and co-orbits for ever; this model has nothing to keep, because PH-94 froze the station and its velocity is zero. 120 m is 3x the station's own 40 m corridor. | This is the SECOND concession of the same family as PH-94's first ("the ground does not slide past underneath") and is written down as one rather than dressed up. It is also chosen to sit inside the 200 m at which `stationwalk.js` P3 requires a player to FALL, so that negative control still holds and now proves two things instead of one (no deck AND no frame). **Measured, Z8: at 30 m off the hull the player floats with `apparentG` 0 and drifts 0.000000 m in 3 s with no input; at 200 m they fall 15.98 m against 15.89 m predicted.** The honest fix is a carrier frame in the walker, which is the same prerequisite PH-94 named for the station ever moving. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-103 | **THE RESEARCH GATE IS AVAILABLE AND IS NOT TAKEN YET, and the generator's power state is MODULE STATE that no save carries.** A subagent sweep established the tech tree is WIRED END TO END (`core/include/of/research.h`, 21 `of_rs_*` exports, `game/Research.ts`, `ui/ResearchPanel.ts`, `probes/research.js`, KeyJ), so a gate is genuinely two lines: a row in `FactoryKinds.GATED_BY_ITEM` plus the ItemId in a tech's `unlockItems`. | Not taken because a gate needs a PLACEABLE generator entity to gate, and today the station's gravity is derived from its record rather than built by a player. Shipping the boolean first is what makes `Anchorage` a working station (every green probe stays green) and the derelict case reachable by turning it off. **Stated plainly rather than promised: the research gate is OWED, and so is persistence of the powered flag.** Note also that `GameMode.researchGated` is `!sandbox`, so every gate in the game is already off in the mode most probes run in. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-104 | **`grounded` IS A CLAIM ABOUT WEIGHT, NOT ABOUT CONTACT: in freefall it stays FALSE and `onDeck` stays true.** With it true, the snap branch (`grounded && gap <= snapM`, reaching 0.35 m) would pull a drifting player back onto a deck they had already left, and report it as a landing. The floor also stops killing UPWARD velocity in freefall. | The second half is a defect that would have shipped and was found by READING rather than by playing: `vUp = vUp > 0 && wet.floating ? vUp : 0` zeroes an upward push every tick, so a weightless player resting on a deck could thrust away from it for ever and never leave. **`zerog.js` Z6 is its negative control and it lands on a second number: the push-off stops at exactly 0.85 m, which is `SpaceStation.ts`'s own authored prediction ("2.5 minus the 1.65 m top sample"), so the ceiling HELD.** | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-105 | **THE INTERIOR IS THE SHIPPED 57-PROXY MESH AND THERE IS NO FALLBACK SHAPE.** The placeholder's twelve hand-authored boxes are DELETED, along with its argument that 4.0 m of headroom "would make the corridor look like a lift shaft": the asset lane went to 4.0 m at 3.0 m wide and its scale render disproves it, so the comment goes with the boxes it was defending. `installStation` REFUSES with no proxies learned rather than standing in. Datums measured off the glb rather than trusted: deck tops at local y = **0.000** on all six runs (the datum the frozen pose and `stateOf` already share), headroom **4.000**, overhead proxies **0.800** which is R48's floor exactly, corridor **3.000** clear, hatch **2.100** wide by **2.300** high. ONE traversal produces the list and it KEEPS NAMES, because the gravity derivation has to tell `col_SpineAftFloor` from `col_SpineAftCeil`. `stationAxes.along` moved from local Z to local X because the spine did. The spawn is read from the asset's own `socket_hall` empty, because **the station's origin is no longer empty**: `col_HallCore` is a solid column from y 0 to 5.400 and the origin is inside it. | A hand-authored stand-in for an asset that failed to load is a second authority that is silently wrong rather than loudly absent: the player would walk around inside a twelve-box ghost of a fifty-seven-box station with every instrument agreeing with every other. Running `proxiesOf` for the collider and a second traversal for the gravity would be two answers to "which boxes does this asset have". A name that lies (`along` pointing across) is worse than a number that is wrong, because nothing measures a name. `stationwalk.js` P2 went red with "the air above the deck reads solid", which was exactly true and the right complaint. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-106 | **ARTIFICIAL GRAVITY IS DERIVED FROM THE DECKS, ONE BOX PER FLOOR PROXY EXTRUDED BY THE AUTHORED HEADROOM, NOT FROM THE HULL'S BOUNDING BOX.** The box starts at the deck slab's UNDERSIDE and not its top face, and that 0.3 m is not padding: a standing player's feet are sampled EXACTLY on the deck top, and the body-frame round trip at radius 1e6 m carries ~2e-10 m of error, so with both planes coincident the feet land outside the volume as often as in. Measured, `zerog.js` Z4 red: apparent **3.5315999991723803** against a true **3.531600000000002**. Match on `includes('Floor')` and NOT `endsWith`, also measured: `endsWith` gave FIVE boxes where the station has NINE, because the mezzanine slabs are `col_GalleryFloorN/E/S/W` and a compass letter is not the word Floor. Fringe RAISED 0.5 to **1.5 m**. | On twelve boxes in a line an AABB is very nearly the interior; on the real station it is a 67 x 25 x 62 m slab enclosing five branches, four breaches and every cubic metre of vacuum between them, so it would have held a player at full weight while they floated in open space a metre off the hull. **The feature this lane exists to deliver would have been switched off by its own bounding box.** Two properties then fall out that nothing had to be written for: every deck EDGE is an EVA exit, and the vented section has no gravity for free. Gravity plating under a floor is also what a deck physically IS. The `endsWith` miss would have left the whole gallery weightless in a powered station, reading as "zero g is broken" rather than as one suffix not matching. The old fringe invariant ("no point a player can stand on is ever at partial strength") is deliberately broken, because a deck box's faces ARE the room's surfaces and the only walkable boundary left is a doorway onto an unpowered region, which is exactly where the transition should be felt. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-107 | **THE AIRLOCK IS THE VENTED SECTION AND THE ASSET AUTHORED IT (R55 CLOSED).** `build_space_station.py` names its own aft end: `AFT_X = -36.00` "the torn aft rim, open to space", `BLOWN_X = -28.00` "the bulkhead that failed". `col_SpineAftFloor` runs to x = **-28.000** with NO cap beyond it. `col_JambAftFrame{L,R}` at x = **-20.000** is the last bulkhead that HELD, and everything aft of it is open to space through that blown bulkhead or through the 146 degrees of reactor-drum roof `_reactor_drum` deliberately never emits. ONE rule produces both regions: **a deck is powered only forward of the aft-most bulkhead jamb**, derived from the shipped proxies so an asset that moves its bulkhead moves the airlock with it. **NO DOOR STATE.** | R55 said the station has no way out. That was true of the PLACEHOLDER (sealed by `col_CorrCap`) and FALSE of the mesh that replaced it: the way out had been in the file since it landed and the GRAVITY did not know. The moment worth building is not "you can get out", which every deck edge already gives. It is that **you become weightless while there is still a floor under you.** At a deck edge footing and weight go in the same tick and a player cannot tell which happened; here they are separated by 8 m of lit corridor with walls to push off, before there is 400 km underneath, which is a tutorial made of architecture rather than of text. A door you could shut would claim the chamber can be re-pressurised, and the far end of it is gone. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-108 | **THE GENERATOR'S POWER STATE IS PERSISTED, THROUGH THE ONE `writeSlot` CHOKE POINT AND NO SECOND WRITER.** Additive and optional on `dayT`'s exact pattern, `SAVE_VERSION` deliberately UNMOVED, and `undefined` rather than `false` is what means "this slot predates the field" (`stashStationPower` takes `unknown` and accepts only a real boolean). Retires the PH-103 debt. Proved by [`tools/smoke/powerreload.mjs`](../../web/tools/smoke/powerreload.mjs) across a REAL `page.reload()`, with two controls. | A station that is powered before a reload and dead after is worse than one that was never powered: the world silently undid the only thing the player did to it. Collapsing a missing field to `false` with `=== true` would have switched the gravity off in every existing save on its first load, which is why the tri-state is not fussiness; the `--control=noflag` leg manufactures exactly that slot (confirmed `had: false`) and it boots lit. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-109 | **AN EVA CARRIES ITS OWN FREEFALL VOLUME, AND IT IS THE STATION'S TWO-VOLUME MODEL MINUS ONE VOLUME.** [`VesselGravity.ts`](../../web/src/game/VesselGravity.ts): freefall alone, **60 m** half-extent, NO generator, because a rocket has no plating and is not meant to. Axis-aligned with an identity rotation, deliberately. 60 m is sized off two numbers this lane already owns: comfortably over `BOARD_RANGE_M` (18 m), and well inside the 200 m at which `stationwalk.js` P3 requires a body to FALL. | Without it a body beside a rocket at 200 km reads `gravityAccel(r)` = 8.6 m/s^2, which is **57x `ZEROG.floatG`**, so the float gate never fires, the walker believes it has traction, and it falls out of the sky in the most literal possible sense. A sphere of freefall has no orientation, so giving it the vessel's attitude would mean the region a player floats in changed shape when the rocket rolled, which no player could predict and no probe would think to check. Sizing it under the board range would have meant a rocket you may climb into while falling past it. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-110 | **EVA IS A DOOR BESIDE `disembark`, NOT A LOOSENING OF IT (R54 CLOSED). One key, two doors, and the VESSEL chooses which.** `disembark`'s 2 m/s guard is untouched and still owns every ground case; `evaOut`'s own guard is STRICTER, being `mayLeave` (`/core`'s `onRails` predicate) plus a not-on-the-ground test `mayLeave` alone would miss, because a rocket on the pad is `parked` and `parked` is permitted. The vessel is NOT demoted. Order inside is load-bearing: sync THEN guard, place the body BEFORE `releaseControl`, install the volume before the first tick the walker owns. | The shape `takeControlRemote` established beside `board()` rather than by loosening the 18 m range check. `disembark`'s guard reads the state vector, sees 7.8 km/s and refuses, which is the correct answer to the question it asks and the wrong answer to the one the player asks. `demoteVessel` destroys the `/core` FlightSim and disposes the design, and a rocket you cannot see is not one you can EVA around. `releaseControl` re-arms the on-foot HUD, so a body still parked at the pad for one tick of that is a player standing on the ground with a navball shutting behind them. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |
| PH-111 | **AN EVA UN-STAMPS THE VESSEL'S RECORD (`stampedTick = -1`), WHICH IS WHAT `mintStation` DOES AND FOR THE SAME REASON.** The expected blocker did NOT arise: `flight.step(dt)` is called from inside `VesselObserver.step`, the observer is stepped only while it is the router's source, so **a promoted vessel with nobody aboard does not advance** and is already frozen in the body frame. What advanced was the RECORD: `syncPromoted` stamps it and a stamped record's clock runs with the world's, so `stateOf` kept solving the conic forward while the thing it describes sat still. Measured by `eva.js` E4 before the fix: **6829.55 m in three seconds against 6867.76 m of "at orbital speed"**, and the 0.6% between those two is the conic's arc against a straight-line chord, which is the tell. `FlightReport` now publishes the live sim's `pos` UNROUNDED. | Two authorities for one rocket, 6.8 km apart in three seconds: the map draws the marker where the player is not, and the next demote/promote teleports the hull there. Un-stamped, the frozen sim and the derived conic position are the same number BY CONSTRUCTION. Exactly the two-authority trap DW-26 exists to refuse, arriving for the third time in this lane. Every other figure on that report is rounded, which is why the drift had no instrument: the assertion is "these two agree", and rounding to centimetres conceals a sub-centimetre disagreement while appearing to prove there was none. | **Accepted** | 2026-08-02 |

| PH-142 | **R43 CLOSED. A STAGE THAT LIGHTS TWO PROPELLANT KINDS AT ONCE HAS TWO BURN TIMES AND ONE DELTA-V, AND `stagePerformance` PUBLISHED NEITHER.** `vessel.h` `StagePerformance` gains `fullThrustS` (the FIRST flameout) beside `burnTimeS` (now the LAST), and the whole burn is PHASE-INTEGRATED per propellant kind. Three new tests in [`test_vessel.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_vessel.cpp). ABI 21 to 22 (`STAGE_PERF_WORDS` 12 to 13). | **The old number was not approximate, it was a quantity that does not exist.** It pooled the FIRST engine's kind of propellant and divided that by EVERY engine's mass flow: a solid booster beside a liquid engine published `propellantKg 4300, burnTimeS 11.203, dv 656.02` because 4300 / (61.801 + 321.99) = 11.2028 exactly, against a flight that burned the booster's 9000 kg for 27.95 s and ran the liquid engine to 69.58 s. **Hand-computed off the authored masses BEFORE the code ran, the way the Ascender's numbers were: mdot 322.015646 and 61.800983 kg/s, t 27.948953 s and 69.578182 s, m0 17145, m1 3845, phase 1 Isp 231.671186 s to a mass of 6417.727273 giving 2232.4835 m/s, phase 2 on the liquid engine alone giving 1657.8715 m/s, total 3890.3550 m/s vacuum.** The old 656.02 was **17% of the truth**, and it is the number Reid's autopilot fuel gate was about to be built on. **THE ONE-LINE FIX WAS REFUSED**: filtering the engine loop by `consumes`, as the risk entry proposed, drops the booster's 672 kN out of a stage it dominates and publishes a pad TWR saying the vehicle cannot leave the ground. Trading an understated burn time for an understated thrust is not a fix. **Every SINGLE-kind stage is bit-identical across the change**, pinned with `==` against `tsiolkovsky` rather than a tolerance, because the last phase lands on `endMassKg` itself instead of on an accumulated product; the reference vehicle still reads 1857.79 + 3065.12 = 4922.91 m/s and 69.578 / 126.506 s, and the ascent probe still stages at 69.7 s. |
| PH-143 | **R44b CLOSED IN THE SAME ABI BUMP. THE NAVBALL'S PER-STAGE TABLE IS A LIVE INSTRUMENT.** New export `of_fl_stage_performance(f)` over `s->craft`, writing the SAME 13-word row as `of_vs_stage_performance` from ONE shared writer (`pushStagePerfRow`). `FlightReport.readStagePerformance` now takes the FLIGHT handle, and the read moved from `refreshParts` into `FlightSession.sample()`. | **Two bugs wearing one coat, and only one of them was a cadence.** The table was read off the DESIGN and `_of_fl_create` does `f->craft = *p`, so the blueprint never burned a gram: calling `refreshParts` every frame would have produced bit-identical numbers. But moving the read INTO `refreshParts` was also wrong, because that call bumps `partsRevision` and `FlightMode` diffs it to decide whether to rebuild every drawn mesh on the craft, so a per-frame refresh there would redraw the whole vehicle to update a number. It belongs in `sample()` beside `flightState` / `flightTelemetry` / `flightOrbit`, which is where the other three live instruments already are. **Driven, not reasoned: `probes/stagedv.js` had been written to assert the DEFECT behind a flag and to say which flag to flip on the day it was fixed. | PH-151 | **A DEFECT IN SHIPPED SAS AND IT IS REID'S BUG, NOT THE AUTOPILOT'S: A SHIP COMMANDED EXACTLY BACKWARDS CANNOT TURN ROUND.** Any player flying perfectly prograde who presses retrograde hits it; it is rare because humans do not fly perfectly prograde, not because the code was safe. `flight::stabilityAssist` derives its axis from `cross(forward, target)`, which is the ZERO VECTOR for two opposed unit vectors, so the demanded torque was zero. Fixed by choosing an axis at exactly pi; new test in [`test_flight.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_flight.cpp). | **`errorRad` was reported correctly as pi the whole time, which is what makes it nasty: the controller knew, said so, and did nothing.** It is not a corner case for an autopilot, it is the NORMAL case: circularising at apoapsis points exactly retrograde relative to the burn that raised the orbit BY CONSTRUCTION, so the second burn of every hold-orbit program lands on it. **Found by flying one**: the vehicle held **180.000 degrees for 600 s of sim** and the burn never happened. **It survived every hand test this project has run, and the reason is structural rather than luck: a human at the controls JITTERS, so hand testing samples the neighbourhood AROUND a singular case and routes past it without the tester knowing one was there. Automation goes straight through the middle.** The general form is now written into [INSTRUMENTS.md](../web/INSTRUMENTS.md): automating a control loop is itself an instrument, and its first job is to walk the exact cases hand-testing structurally cannot reach. At exactly pi every perpendicular axis is equally valid, so one is CHOSEN from `forward` alone (cross with the cardinal axis it is least aligned with, which can never itself be degenerate) and it therefore stays bit-deterministic. The `sinA > 1e-12` path is untouched, so every existing number is unmoved and 35/35 ctest stayed green. |

Adding a rate gate fixed the pointing (**3.92 to 0.32 degrees**) and made the eccentricity **6x worse** (0.000305 to 0.001862), because holding for the rate pushed the burn LATE and a late burn is exactly what PH-38's lead exists to prevent. The real fault was upstream: **a program that schedules its first burn for RIGHT NOW cannot be flown by a vehicle that is not already pointed at it**, and after a 180 degree flip it never is. `holdOrbit` now PROPAGATES to where the vehicle will be once it has had time to turn and plans from there, so the whole thing stays one forward solve. **All three together: 700061.94 to 700004.99 m, e 0.000305 to 0.000016, and the eccentric case 1771.7 m to 72.1 m.** |

Sampled then bisected on the FIRST inward crossing, because a trajectory that grazes the SOI, leaves and returns must hand off at the first entry: handing off at the second integrates the wrong conic through the gap. The sample count is stated rather than tuned, because too coarse MISSES a crossing rather than mislocating it (Cinder's 2.4e6 m bubble is crossed in about 1500 s, so 15 s a step has two orders of magnitude of margin). **Time to periapsis is bisected on `r.v` through the PROPAGATOR** rather than by inverting Kepler a second time, so the answer agrees with the trajectory that will actually be flown. `planCapture` captures at the arrival hyperbola's OWN periapsis and publishes that radius, rather than pretending to hit a requested altitude: aiming the periapsis is a mid-course correction, not a capture, and a burn that claimed otherwise would not produce the orbit it advertised. |

The fix is the impact parameter, `b = r_p sqrt(1 + 2 mu / (r_p v_inf^2))`, which for a 250 km periapsis is **543144 m** of offset and costs **4.61 m/s** on the injection. (2) **A MOON TRANSFER CANNOT BE FLOWN OPEN-LOOP.** The 861 m/s injection's finite-burn residue over a 7.4 hour coast is **468 km of position error at the sphere of influence**, and since the aim offset is only 543 km the error is the SAME SIZE as the thing it perturbs: the flown arrival periapsis came out **185669 m against a 200000 m body**, i.e. the planned capture orbit was a 14 km deep hole in the ground. That is R66 arriving at a scale where it stops being a residue and becomes the mission, and it is why every real interplanetary flight budgets correction manoeuvres. One Lambert from where the vehicle actually is to the aim point it was always going to, at 35% of the way, under 10% of the injection's cost. (3) **THE HANDOFF IS A MEASUREMENT, NOT A PREDICTION.** The first driver coasted in FORGE's frame all the way to the capture burn and then subtracted the moon, which is the wrong two-body problem inside Cinder's own well and landed 654 km and e 0.34 off. D-002 says one body at a time; this is what one body at a time costs. |

Torque and translation are billed from the same tank and are not otherwise coupled. **AND EVERY EXISTING FLIGHT IS BIT-IDENTICAL**, asserted rather than assumed: the command defaults to zero and a 600 tick flight that touches the field and one that does not agree to the last bit on position, velocity and propellant. |

That is R66 at the scale where it stops being a residue, the same argument that made the lunar mid-course correction compulsory. **THE TARGET POSE IS HANDED IN EVERY CALL, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS INDEPENDENT OF THE CARRIER-TERM WORK (R77/R79)**: the program never asks where the station is, so it inherits the correct pose the day the frame work lands, with no change here. **THE APPROACH IS A CORRIDOR AND NOT A STRAIGHT LINE**, because the shortest path to a port goes through the station: it flies to a standoff point out along the port's axis first, and the closing rate is gated on the lateral error so it cannot arrive off-axis by closing faster than it centres. Asserted as the INVARIANT rather than a magnitude (the cone is `captureRadius + along*tan(10 deg)` and widens with range, so a bare metre bound would be vacuous far out and impossible close in): **the worst lateral error in the final approach is 5.24 m, at 30 m out where the cone allows 5.89, and it never breaches the cone at all.** **THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS THE SAME FLIGHT WITH ONE THING REMOVED** (the thruster command zeroed): it does not dock and does not come within 5 m, which is what says the acceptance was not a fixture already pointed at the target. Two more refusals driven: an approach started BEHIND the port plane reports `swinging round to the port's own side` and commands a push AWAY from the station, and a radially mounted port is refused by name rather than aimed approximately. |
| PH-175 | **THE TOUCHDOWN GATE, AND THE GROUND UNDER IT: new [`core/include/of/landing.h`](../../core/include/of/landing.h)** (`SurfaceFrame`, `surfaceFrame`, `TouchdownLimits`, `Touchdown`, `evaluate`). Layer one of the landing, on the same split docking got. | **PHYSICS R5 SAID THE LANDING END WAS DEFERRED AND THIS IS NOT IT ARRIVING**: `flight.h` still has no contact model, nothing bounces or bends or carries a load. What this decides is whether an arrival was a landing, and **which of four ways it was not**: came down too fast, too much sideways speed so it tipped, not upright, or the ground was too steep to stand on. Every one is driven from a state and **the gate is two-sided at each bound** (4.99 m/s lands, 5.01 does not). **THE GROUND'S REFUSAL IS FIRST, DELIBERATELY**: a slope nobody can stand on is not the pilot's fault and is the one refusal a better approach cannot fix, and it is driven on a real 24.5 degree Cinder slope where the vehicle arrives at 0.2 m/s perfectly aligned to the hillside and is still refused. **TILT IS MEASURED AGAINST THE TERRAIN NORMAL AND NOT AGAINST RADIAL UP**, which is the whole reason `surfaceFrame` computes a normal: a vehicle plumb with gravity on an 8 degree slope is 8 degrees off the ground it is about to put its legs on, and it is the ground that decides whether a leg takes the load. Asserted both ways in one test. **AND THE BASELINE IS PART OF THE SLOPE (WG-146)**: the normal is a three-sample plane fit over a stated baseline, never a one-direction difference, which would report a north-south ridge as flat. The baseline is the vehicle's FOOTPRINT, because a 40 degree ripple two metres wide does not tip a lander whose feet are six metres apart. **Measured: the same Cinder point reads 24.500 degrees over 6 m and 26.627 degrees over 1000 m.** |
| PH-176 | **THE POWERED DESCENT FLIES ITSELF DOWN ONTO REAL MOON TERRAIN: new [`core/include/of/descent.h`](../../core/include/of/descent.h)** (`Profile`, `Leg`, `Guidance`, `guide`) and a 38th ctest suite, [`test_descent.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_descent.cpp), 60 checks. | **MEASURED, twice, onto ground the test SEARCHED FOR BY SLOPE and then stated: from 2 km up carrying 20 m/s of sideways speed, it lands at 1.0006 m/s vertical and 0.0069 m/s lateral on a 2.81 degree site, and at 1.0005 / 0.0073 on a 9.85 degree one, in 115.2 s and 108.0 s for 192.1 kg and 181.5 kg of propellant.** **THE FIXTURE IS THE POINT OF THE FILE.** Admin's warning was that a descent tested only onto flat ground is the flat-spawn trap this project has been caught by four times, so nothing here uses a plane: every landing goes through `worldgen::sampleHeightField` on real Cinder, the sites are found by a slope search rather than chosen, and the terrain under the vehicle is **resampled every tick** because the vehicle is moving sideways over a crater field and the ground beneath it is a different height every second. **THE DESCENT RATE IS A CEILING AND NOT A SETPOINT, AND GETTING THAT WRONG COST A WHOLE TANK.** The first version tracked `gain * altitude` as a target, so a lander arriving gently at 5 m/s from 2 km was commanded to **THRUST DOWNWARD** to reach 60: it emptied a 2150 kg tank and aborted at 126.5 s because it could no longer hold itself up. Now a vehicle under its ceiling is left alone to fall, with the engine out of the way **including the gravity term**, because holding against gravity under the ceiling is a hover and a hover does not land. Pinned by asserting that the same vehicle at the same height falling slower than allowed commands **exactly zero throttle**. **It refuses a vehicle that cannot hold itself up before flying it rather than after**, with a positive control one per cent the other side of the same bound. **The negative control is the same fall with the throttle removed**: it arrives at over 40 m/s against a 5 m/s gate, which is what says the acceptance measured a flight and not a gentle moon. |

| PH-200 | **THE AIRLESS ASCENT PROGRAM: new [`core/include/of/ascent.h`](../../core/include/of/ascent.h)** (`ArcPeak`, `arcPeak`, `Profile`, `Leg`, `Guidance`, `pointingErrorRad`, `guide`) and a 39th ctest suite, [`test_ascent.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_ascent.cpp), 58 checks. It is `descent.h` READ UPWARDS: a stateless control law with named legs (Vertical, Turn, Coast, Circularise, Orbit, Aborted) that takes the surface frame under the vehicle as an input, so a round trip to Cinder is two headers rather than one header and a test. | **MEASURED, off REAL Cinder through `worldgen::sampleHeightField`, from pads the test SEARCHES FOR and then states. Flat pad (slope 0.876 deg, terrain +12.1 m): orbit in 661.9 s at 20.0 x 19.4 km, e 0.001362, for 740.6796 m/s and 682.3 kg of propellant, cutting off at 13,182 m after 228.0 s. A pad 2,149 m DOWN A HOLE with the rim 3,508 m above it: the same orbit for 761.6506 m/s, and the 21 m/s is the depth.** **THE PITCH IS DRAWN AGAINST APOAPSIS PROGRESS AND NOT ALTITUDE**, because `flight::GravityTurnProgram`'s `turnCompleteM = 45000` is a Forge number and is nonsense on a 200 km moon with a 20 km parking orbit; apoapsis progress is self-terminating and needs no per-body constant. **The exponent is 0.30, which is NOT the cheapest**: swept over an 11 x 8 grid of pad TWR (1.15 to 10) and exponent (0.15 to 0.55) the delta-v falls monotonically as the exponent falls, so cost alone would pick 0.15, and 0.15 and 0.20 have cells that do not reach orbit at all while their neighbours do. A launch program with a hole beside its default is worse than one costing 5 m/s more. **The TWR ceiling is 2.0 and it is a `Profile` field rather than a fixture convenience**: the reference lander leaves Cinder at TWR 10.2 rising to 25.3, and at full throttle it passes the 807.47 m/s escape speed before an ascent profile has happened. |
| PH-200b | **FOUR DEFECTS, EACH ONLY VISIBLE ONCE THE ONE BEFORE IT WAS FIXED, AND THE THIRD IS THE ONE WORTH KEEPING.** | (1) **The cutoff straddled its own threshold**: the insertion nulls the residual climb, which lowers the apoapsis a few metres, the bare `>=` flipped back to the ascent, and the commanded direction alternated every tick between the turn's prograde and the insertion's slightly-downward vector. Measured: legs alternating 1,3,1,3 for forty seconds and **the vehicle burning at 151.60 degrees off its own command**, which put 3.6 m/s of climb into an orbit that was meant to be circular. Fixed with a 50 m band whose negative control is REACHABLE and driven (band 0: 15 returns to the ascent legs and a worst burning attitude of 26.81 degrees; band 50: 0 and 15.00). (2) **The burn's lead was priced at FULL THROTTLE while the burn is flown at the TWR ceiling**, so it predicted 7.4 s for a burn that took 56.6 s and ran the whole thing past apoapsis. PH-158's shape: a plausible number read off the wrong quantity. (3) **TIME TO APOAPSIS WAS APPROXIMATED, AND THE PROGRAM USED THE APPROXIMATION TO DECIDE WHETHER IT WAS NEAR APOAPSIS.** `vUp / (gravity - centripetal)` is right near apoapsis and nowhere else; at the PERIAPSIS of a 20 x 6 km ellipse the centripetal term EXCEEDS gravity and it returns zero seconds, so the insertion fired 14 km below where it belonged, could never finish, and thrashed between two legs for a thousand seconds until the tank was dry. **That is why three cells of the robustness grid failed while their neighbours passed**, which is a defect signature and not a physical limit. It is now the exact eccentric-anomaly solve, and it still never touches `summarize().bound`. (4) **The burn window was the REMAINING burn, which shrinks faster than the burn finishes**: it cut out with 14.92 m/s still owed and the periapsis still 2,601 m BELOW the surface, and since `flight.h` has no contact model the lander coasted **STRAIGHT THROUGH CINDER, 9,364 m below the datum**, and came back for another go. Three passes, 7,715 s. The window is now the burn's own length plus 22.5 degrees of mean anomaly (a 7.6 per cent cosine loss, worth paying to FINISH a burn and not to START one), and one pass takes 661.9 s. |
| PH-200c | **R14 IS PINNED FROM BOTH SIDES, because it is the trap that stopped the previous attempt and a comment about it is not a test.** | `summarize()` really does report a 100 m/s vertical climb as **unbound, e = 1.000000000000, apoapsisAltM = 1e+308**, which is false for the first seconds of every launch. `arcPeak` really does return the peak anyway: **203218.405128 m against a hand computation of 203218.405128 m**, because specific energy and angular momentum are continuous through the radial case that eccentricity is not, and `a(1+e)` tends to `-mu/E` as `h` tends to zero. The time to it is asserted at BOTH ends of the same function: 62.69 s straight up against a flat-gravity 61.35, and **at the periapsis of a real ellipse exactly half a period**, which is the case the approximation got catastrophically wrong. |
| PH-201 | **THE NINTH SAS MODE, SO THE ASCENT RIBBON IS SOMETHING YOU CAN FLY AND NOT ONLY LOOK AT.** Admin reordered this ahead of the R63 calibration on the strength of the gameplay lane's end-to-end playthrough, and the reorder was right. | `Navball.ts` said of the guidance ribbon, in its own comment, **"Shown, never flown"**. Eight SAS modes point at the orbital triad and none pointed at the one marker the game draws to tell a player where to put the nose, so reaching orbit was the single link a player had to hand-fly while everything downstream of it flies itself. **IT IS NOT A `/core` MODE, AND THE REASON IS AUTHORITY RATHER THAN EFFORT.** The ribbon's PITCH stays in `flight.h` and is read through `_of_fl_guidance_pitch`; nothing here recomputes it. What a ninth `flight.h` `SasMode` would ALSO have needed is EAST, and east is the client's (`horizonFrame` is the frame the ball, the ribbon and the pitch/yaw keys share), and PH-40 already records this codebase carrying two inclination conventions. So it is `SAS_COMMAND` re-aimed every tick, which is exactly what hold-node already is. **THE INTERLOCK IS ONE LINE AND IT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN**: `commandDirection` clears the follow UNCONDITIONALLY and the follower re-arms itself immediately afterwards, so `slew`, hold-node and every future aimer take control correctly WITHOUT KNOWING THE MODE EXISTS. PH-44 is that file's founding bug and this is the shape that does not repeat it. New key on Digit9 (`sasGuidance`), and `sasName` reads `GDN` while `/core` still reads `CMD` in `of_fl_state` word 17, deliberately. **Measured, [`probes/ribbon.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/ribbon.js), driven through the real keys: the nose is slewed 30.00 degrees OFF the ribbon first, the ribbon's own pitch then swings 45.50 degrees over the sampled window (asserted BEFORE the tracking is believed, because a stationary ribbon cannot tell following from a single aim), and across twelve samples the worst command-vs-ribbon separation is 0.0396 degrees, which is one tick of altitude change and not zero. The nose closes from 30 degrees to 2.4. The negative control is the same flight with the same slew and no key: 111.45 degrees, a factor of 2816.** The interlock is driven both ways: a pitch input takes GDN to CMD, it re-arms, and a mode key takes it to PRO. |
| PH-202 | **BOTH BROWSER FLIGHT ACCEPTANCES WERE RED AT THEIR OWN SECTION 0 AND NOTHING SAID SO.** | `probes/ascent.js` and `probes/flightabuse.js` each carried `catalogue === 24`. A part was added, the catalogue became 25, and **both probes have been failing at setup ever since, before a rocket is built and before anything is flown**. The whole of this domain's browser acceptance had stopped running and the symptom read as a setup detail. The question the line exists to ask is whether the catalogue crossed the bridge at all, and a catalogue that GREW is not a regression, so it is a bound now and the count is logged. R60 recorded the same stale-literal shape in `vessel_tests`; this is the other two places that change landed. **`flightabuse.js` now runs to its own assertions and fails three of them on content**, all pre-existing and all previously invisible (R85). |
| PH-203 | **R63 CLOSED, AND THE PREDICTION IT MADE WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH KEEPING.** `transfer::ascentDvMS` now answers for Cinder as well as Forge (`kAscentLossFractionCinder = 0.343781`), and a third body still gets nothing. | R63 said an airless low-gravity body's losses would be **"a few percent"** against Forge's 35. Flown: **0.343781 in this formula's own terms**, two fifths of Forge's and not a tenth. **TWO SEPARATE REASONS AND SEPARATING THEM IS THE FINDING.** (1) **The formula's baseline is not the ideal cost**: `vPark` is the destination orbit's speed, and reaching it costs more even with an infinite engine, because the two-impulse Hohmann from Cinder's surface to a 20 km circular orbit is **597.5202 m/s against a vPark of 544.3929**, so **0.093048 of vSurf is arithmetic that no ascent can avoid** and that this "loss" fraction is charged for. On Forge the same term is 0.1230. (2) **Gravity loss does not care about air**: what an airless body saves is DRAG, the smaller half of Forge's number. The genuine guidance-plus-gravity loss is **0.250733 of vSurf against Forge's 0.4730** under the same decomposition. **WHAT THE NUMBER IS AND IS NOT**: it is what THIS PROJECT'S ascent program spends, exactly as Forge's is what this project's reference ascent spent, and it is not an optimum. Apollo's ascent stage left the Moon at a comparable pad TWR of 2.06 and spent about 0.15 of surface circular speed over its own impulsive ideal against 0.25 here, so roughly a third of this number is the difference between a scheduled gravity turn and real explicit guidance. Recorded so nobody later reads it as physics. **The constant is a hand-typed literal and the test RE-FLIES rather than recomputing it**, agreeing to 0.0001 m/s, so a change to either side goes red; and Forge is bit-unchanged at **3724.649392**. `test_maneuver.cpp`'s `CHECK(!moon.calibrated)` went red exactly as an assertion about a refusal is supposed to, and is now the other way round with the refusal kept live on a body nobody has flown. |
| PH-204 | **R83 RULED BY ADMIN AND TAKEN: `ascentDvMS`'s baseline is the IDEAL TWO-IMPULSE TRANSFER, and Forge's published number moves from 3724.649392 to 3720.907932.** New `transfer::idealAscentDvMS(mu, R, rPark)`, which has no fitted constants in it at all. | **WHAT THE PUBLISHED ROW BECOMES, stated because `of_ap_design_reach` draws it**: ascent **3724.649392 to 3720.907932** (-3.741460 m/s, -0.1005%), reserve 206.032939 to 205.845866 (5% of four legs, so it tracks), total 4326.691711 to 4322.763178, margin 637.943506 to **641.872039**, and the player is better off. Transfer and arrival unmoved. **THE CLIENT WILL KEEP DRAWING THE OLD NUMBER UNTIL THE WASM IS REBUILT**, because `transfer.h` is compiled into the blob; no probe pins the value, so nothing goes red in the meantime. **WHY**: `vPark` is the SPEED OF THE DESTINATION and not the cost of reaching it, that cost GROWS WITH rPark, so the old fraction was a physical quantity plus a bookkeeping artefact added together and was accurate only where it was calibrated. The VAB lets a player choose the altitude, which makes that a trap. **THE EVIDENCE WAS RE-FLOWN BECAUSE THE FIRST VERSION OF IT WAS BAD, and that is the part worth keeping**: R83's own entry quoted a spread from a sweep whose time limit three of seven rows exceeded, so their delta-v was a PARTIAL ASCENT. Re-flown to completion at five parking altitudes with a `CHECK` that each row finished: **5 km 765.03, 10 km 716.98, 20 km 740.68, 40 km 780.86, 80 km 841.28 m/s; the old fraction spans 0.348460 and the new one spans 0.095983, so the new baseline removes 72.5 per cent of the variation**, and the suite asserts that rather than claiming it. **ADMIN'S FIRST CONDITION IS MET AND IT EXPOSED A SECOND ERROR**: the Forge figure now returns **3722.910000 at the flown mean radius of 681,200 m**, exact by construction, and the old constants were calibrated at 680,000 m **against a flight that reached 681,200**, so the "0.047% off" on record for them was itself measured at the wrong radius (at the flight's own radius the old form was 0.2688 m/s low). **AND A LINE WAS DELETED RATHER THAN RETUNED**: `test_maneuver.cpp` held the formula at 680 km against a flight at 681.2 km, the same category error, passing only because the two are close. **The first draft of its replacement comment claimed the new form is "closer to the flight" there. It is not** (2.002 m/s against the old 1.739), and the comment now says so. **The gravity/drag split is preserved IN RATIO and labelled a convention**: PH-147 published 0.3500 / 0.2459 with no measurement separating them, nothing since has separated them, and one flight cannot tell gravity loss from drag loss. Cinder becomes **0.250732713**, the genuine loss. **39/39 ctest green, 68 checks in `ascent_tests`.** |

## 4. Architecture & approach
- **Two regimes:** (a) **On-rails** — vessel = Keplerian elements in its dominant frame; advance analytically, including under time-warp; (b) **Active** — full rigid-body sim near the player. core-engine's `SimProxy` triggers promotion/demotion; physics fills the content of each side and must make the handoff smooth (KSP's load-in wobble is the cautionary tale).
- **Patched conics:** vessel feels gravity from exactly one body (its SOI). Crossing an SOI boundary = a frame switch event (core-engine) + re-derivation of elements relative to the new body. Predicted trajectories (for the map/maneuver UI) computed by propagating conics forward and intersecting SOIs.
- **Vehicle structure:** parts as rigid bodies joined by constraints; thrust applied at engine parts; aero forces from atmosphere density (world-gen) × velocity. Joint stability/wobble is a known KSP pain — consider rigidified part-trees or substepping.
- **Surface:** character controller + vehicle contact against world-gen collision (heightfield, or voxel patch where dug). Gravity from the local body.

## 5. Interfaces & dependencies
**Depends on (inbound) — Spike-1 contracts CONSUMED verbatim:**
- core-engine: `FUniverseCoord`(+`FFrameId`), `FReferenceFrame`/`IFrameGraph` (μ/SOI/spin, D-006), `ISimProxy`/`ISimRegistry` (we implement the four hooks; core-engine owns promotion/demotion + hysteresis), `ISimClock` (warp, `TickIndex`), `OnOriginRebased`, **`OnSOIChange` (carries pos+vel re-expressed in new frame — we consume both)**, **CE-6 frame-velocity subtraction** (we read TRUE velocity, integrate the slow residual). All honored; no requested change.
- world-gen: `SampleTerrainHeight`/`GetGroundContact`/`RaycastTerrain` (analytic, resident-free), `FBodyParams`/`FAtmosphereProfile`. Adopted as the collision source (PH-5).

**Provides to (outbound) — the VESSEL programme's published C++ surface (new, 2026-07-26):**

| Header | What it publishes |
|---|---|
| [`core/include/of/vessel.h`](../../core/include/of/vessel.h) | `PartId` / `parts::` (20 authored parts, §5.1 below), `PartDef`, `PartCatalogue` + `catalogue()`, `PartInstance`, `Stage`, `Vessel` (tree + `addRoot`/`attach`/`layout`/`assignSubtreeToStage`), `MassProperties` + `massProperties()`, `staticMarginM()`, `activeEngines()`, `fireStage()` + `StageResult`, `StagePerformance` + `stagePerformance()`/`allStagePerformance()`, `tsiolkovsky()`, `totalDeltaVVacuumMS()`, `remainingDeltaVVacuumMS()`, `thrustToWeight()` |
| [`core/include/of/atmosphere.h`](../../core/include/of/atmosphere.h) | `kG0`, `AtmosphereProfile`, `makeForgeAtmosphere()`/`makeCinderAtmosphere()`/`atmosphereForBody()`, `density()`/`densityRaw()`, `pressureRatio()`, `dynamicPressure()`, `spaceAltitudeM()`/`inSpace()`, `lapse()` |
| [`core/include/of/flight.h`](../../core/include/of/flight.h) | `FlightState`, `FlightEnvironment`, `PropulsionOutput` + `evaluatePropulsion()`, `feedGroup()`/`drawPropellant()`, `AeroTuning`/`AeroForces`/`aerodynamics()`, `SasMode` (**9 values at W12: Off/Hold/Prograde/Retrograde/Command/Normal/Antinormal/RadialIn/RadialOut, appended**), `ControlAuthority`/`controlAuthority()`/`SasTuning`/`stabilityAssist()`, `FlightSim` (`step`/`stage`/`park`/`resume`/`onRailsEligible`/`telemetry`), `OrbitSummary` + `summarize()`, `GravityTurnProgram`, `clampToMaxAoA()` |
| [`core/include/of/orbital.h`](../../core/include/of/orbital.h) | **W12 addition: `Basis` + `basisAt(StateVector)`, the ONE prograde/normal/radial-out triad (PH-39). PH-145 addition: `LambertSolution` + `lambert(r1, r2, tofS, mu, referenceNormal)`, the ONE inverse in the codebase.** It takes a REFERENCE NORMAL rather than testing an axis, and it is SINGLE-REVOLUTION: past one period it answers a different question confidently, which is pinned in `test_physics.cpp` and is the caller's rule to keep. Everything else unchanged. |
| [`core/include/of/maneuver.h`](../../core/include/of/maneuver.h) | **NEW, W12.** `Node` (the KSP handle decomposition + `tFromNowS`), `Basis`/`basisAt` (aliased from orbital), `deltaVVector()`, `BurnEstimate` + `estimateBurn()` (duration across the REMAINING stages, lead, feasibility against `remainingDeltaVVacuumMS`, `burnFractionOfPeriod`), `Plan` + `plan()`, `Path` + `samplePath()` (the conic as a polyline for the map, E-uniform, plus apsis positions/times and the orbit pole). Pure functions; no state, no FlightSim, no execution. |
| [`core/include/of/transfer.h`](../../core/include/of/transfer.h) | **NEW, PH-146, and this is the interface the gameplay lane's VAB destination picker and autopilot gate bind to.** `Target` (`el` + `muM3S2`/`soiRadiusM`/`bodyRadiusM`/`captureAltitudeM`/`dockingRadiusM`; `isBody()` is `mu > 0 && soi > 0`, so a station and a moon are ONE struct), `targetStateAt`, `arrivalCostMS` (match for a vessel, capture for a body, 0 for a flyby: the ONLY place the two differ), `Transfer` + `solveTransfer(vesselEl, tgt, departS, tofS)` (publishes `departDvMS`/`arriveDvMS`/`totalDvMS`, both delta-v VECTORS, all four state vectors, `sweepRad`, and `missDistanceM` as an honest residual), `Budget` + `budgetFor(craft, transfer)` (`availableMS` is `remainingDeltaVVacuumMS` and nothing else; `departBurnS` and `arriveBurnS` billed CUMULATIVELY), `hohmannTimeS`, `synodicPeriodS`, `WindowSample`/`Window` + `scanWindow(vesselEl, tgt, nowS, horizonS, departSamples, tofSamples)` (**the sampled dv-vs-departure-time curve, which is Reid's chart**; `horizonS <= 0` asks for one synodic period; the time-of-flight sweep is bounded to [0.25, 1.75] Hohmann times because Lambert is single-revolution), `Verdict` + `verdictFor` (`feasibleNow` / `feasibleLater` / neither, plus `savingByWaitingMS`, **plus `firstFeasibleIndex`/`firstFeasibleDepartS`/`firstFeasibleTotalDvMS`: the EARLIEST flyable departure, which is not the cheapest one and which the gameplay lane raised by name**, GP-262). **Answers the gameplay lane's GP-262 ask at the math level.** Their four requested exports (`of_ap_design_reach`, `of_ap_flight_reach`, `of_ap_departure_curve`, `of_ap_plan`) are the wasm face over this header and are NOT yet written; their 10-word reach row also splits the budget into `ascent` and `planeChange` legs, which this header does not compute yet. **Pure functions. No FlightSim, no clock, no command.** |

Consumers this is aimed at: **gameplay** (assembly UI, navball, per-stage delta-v readout, DW-30 item 4), **rendering** (part transforms via the tree layout, plume from `PropulsionOutput`), **persistence** (a vessel is its tree plus per-part propellant plus `nextStageIndex`), **networking** (fixed-step + pure functions, determinism asserted).

### 5.1 The published part taxonomy (the art lane and the assembly UI bind to this)

Ids are a separate opaque space starting at `0x0100` so a `PartId` and a gameplay `ItemId` can never be confused. **`asset` is the exact glb node name and every dimension is read off what `tools/blender/build_rocket_parts.py` actually builds**, not invented here.

**Reconciliation note, 2026-07-26:** the first draft of this table used its own names (`TankSmall`, `EngineMain`, ...) and its own guesses at the Tier-2 heights. The art lane landed an expanded `rocket_parts.glb` mid-task with **two diameter classes** (S = 1.25 m, L = 2.50 m, exactly 2x, with a `StackAdapter` between them), 17 stack parts and 6 radial ones, under different names. The catalogue was **realigned to theirs**, because a taxonomy that disagrees with the shipped meshes is the two-authority failure this project keeps paying for, and because their reading of DW-29's "engines in two sizes" (two diameters plus an adapter) is better than the draft's (two performance tiers on one diameter). Heights corrected: monoprop tank 0.60 -> 1.00, reaction wheel 0.30 -> 0.40, battery 0.30 -> 0.60, solid booster 1.00 x 5.00 -> 1.25 x 6.00 with no bottom node.

| PartId | `parts::` | asset | diam x height (m) | dry kg | propellant | engine (T_sl/T_vac kN, Isp_sl/Isp_vac s) | notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
