**One part still has no mesh: `EngineVacuumSmall` (asset name reserved as `LiquidEngineVacuumSmall`).** A 1.25 m vacuum bell, shorter than `LiquidEngineSmall` at 1.00 m, is what an upper stage wants and it is what the reference vehicle's 3065 m/s second stage is built on. Substituting a second `LiquidEngineSmall` costs that stage about 970 m/s. It is a bell on the existing class-S contract, so it is an addition to the file rather than a new convention.

**Reference vehicle "Ascender I"** (the test fixture, and the obvious first tutorial rocket): `CommandPod` + `Parachute` + `TankLiquidSmall` + `EngineVacuumSmall` + `DecouplerStackSmall` + `TankLiquidSmallLong` + `EngineLiquidSmall` + 4 `Fin`. 12.10 m, 9845 kg on the pad, pad TWR 1.6567, per-stage vacuum delta-v **1857.79 + 3065.12 = 4922.91 m/s**, static margin -0.884 m (stable), axial Cd*A 0.887 m^2.

**Provides to (outbound):**
- Interpolated transforms of active physical entities → [rendering](rendering.md).
- **`FVesselOrbitalState`** (mode, dominant frame, elements, true pos+vel, predicted path + next SOI crossing) → [gameplay](gameplay.md) (map/maneuver UI — we compute, they draw), [persistence](persistence.md) (save = ~10 doubles), [networking](networking.md) (replication; pure time-addressable conic + fixed-step integrator = determinism-friendly per D-004). *New outbound contract — flagged to Admin (spike2-physics §8.2).*
- Contact/landing events, structural state → gameplay.

**Negotiated with world-gen (RC-3, settled PH-5):** analytic queries adopted; **one non-breaking additive request** — add `SlopeRad` + `SurfaceHardness` to `FTerrainContact`/`FTerrainHit` (spike2-physics §5.2). Append-only, no caller breaks. Baked collision meshes deferred (Phase-1 perf option, contract already supports it).

## 6. Task backlog / roadmap
- **[Phase 0 / Spike 2]** ✅ **Designed** — [spike2-physics.md](../spikes/spike2-physics.md): patched-conics propagator (universal-variable Kepler), on-rails↔active conversion (the four `ISimProxy` hooks, no-drift guarantee), one rigid-body craft (hybrid integrator), RC-4 hybrid resolution, RC-3 analytic-query resolution, full launch→orbit→coast→SOI→land build plan + gates G1–G11.
- **[Phase 0 / Spike 2 · PH-Build 0]** ✅ **BUILT + GREEN (pure-CPU headless core)** — `core/include/of/orbital.h` + `core/tests/test_physics.cpp` + `physics_tests` CMake target (PH-6). Covers PH-Build 0 steps 1 (`FConics` round-trip, all conic types solve, 1-period return), 2 (analytic advance), 3 (custom symplectic integrator + RC4-A energy-bounded), and 5 (G6 park/resume identity). Gates retired at the math level: **G1** (conic correctness), **G3** (integrator vs analytic, energy bounded), **G6** (park/resume no-drift, elliptic + hyperbolic). Steps 4 (predictive SOI root-find) and PH-Build 1/2 (G4/G5/G7–G11) remain — they need the UE5 project / core-engine Wave-1 skeleton.
- **[Phase S / vessel programme]** ✅ **BUILT + GREEN (headless, `core/` only)** — `vessel.h` + `atmosphere.h` + `flight.h` + `test_vessel.cpp` (338 checks) + `test_flight.cpp` (843 checks). 24/24 ctest, parity 106/106 + 119/119 unchanged (no wasm touched). Decisions PH-10 to PH-19. **Acceptance: the reference rocket flies the pad to an 86.9 x 75.5 km orbit, e 0.0083, period 1880 s, with 1200 m/s and 630 kg of propellant left**; staged at 69.58 s, the burn time the hand computation predicted; max q 27.3 kPa at 7.8 km; max AoA 4.43 deg under load; SAS tracking error 4.35 deg under load.
- **[Phase S / browser flight] BUILT + GREEN (`probes/ascent.js`, PASS)** - the client half of the vessel programme. New: `web/src/sim/` (`FlightAbi`, `FlightSession`, `FlightReport`, `FlightAttitude`), `web/src/player/` (`ViewRouter`, `VesselObserver`, `FlightControls`, flight bindings), `web/src/render/` (`VesselView`, `VesselPlume`), `web/src/ui/` (`Navball`, `NavballDraw`, `navball.css`), `web/src/app/` (`FlightMode`, `DebugFlight`). **NO `/core` change and NO ABI bump: the bridge was already complete at ABI 6 and every `of_fl_*` export was used as shipped.** Decisions PH-20 to PH-27. **Acceptance, driven, sandbox: the reference vehicle is assembled in the bay (4922.91 m/s, 9845 kg, statically stable), a drill and a smelter are built on an ore patch, the player WALKS 27.9 m to the rocket and boards it with a REAL DOM keyboard event, the clamp refuses to release an unlit rocket at full throttle, staging lights the engine at pad TWR 1.88 (the fixture's 1.6567 lapsed for a pad 3 km up), it leaves the ground, stages at 69.7 s (against /core's 69.58 s), max q 17.8 kPa, cutoff at 107.2 s and 1548 m/s, circularises leading apoapsis by half a burn, and reaches 85.9 x 79.7 km, e 0.0045, period 1886.5 s, with 1504 m/s left.** Against `/core`'s own reference ascent of 86.9 x 75.5 km, e 0.0083, period 1880 s, 1200 m/s left. **The factory produced throughout: mined 40 -> 525 -> 1031, ingots 13 -> 186 -> 365.** Then it deorbits, arrests at the surface, the player gets out and the world is still coherent.
- **[Phase S / W11 lane G] ADVERSARIAL VALIDATION, DONE.** New: [`web/src/sim/SaveInhibit.ts`](../../web/src/sim/SaveInhibit.ts) (PH-30), [`web/src/sim/FlightWarp.ts`](../../web/src/sim/FlightWarp.ts) (PH-34), [`web/tools/smoke/probes/flightabuse.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/flightabuse.js) (do the wrong things), [`web/tools/smoke/probes/flyto.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/flyto.js) (fly to a named phase and stop), [`web/tools/smoke/reload.mjs`](../../web/tools/smoke/reload.mjs) (**a second runner: it reloads the BROWSER at a phase, in the same context, so IndexedDB survives exactly as it does for a person pressing F5** — `run.mjs` drives one page load and structurally cannot ask what a player gets when they come back). Decisions PH-28 to PH-36.
  **The headline is the SPREAD, not the best run. Ten cold pad-to-orbit-to-ground ascents, 10/10 pass, and NINE OF THE TEN ARE BIT-IDENTICAL:** apoapsis min 92.589 / p50 92.589 / max 92.918 km, periapsis 79.693 / 79.693 / 79.812 km, e 0.00940 / 0.00940 / 0.00955, delta-v left 1587.6 / 1588.5 / 1588.5 m/s, max q 14421.5 / 14421.5 / 14423.1 Pa, staged 69.7 s, cutoff 112.3 s at 1712 m/s, factory mined 35 -> 367 and ingots 12 -> 132 in every run. **Draw calls were 36 in all ten**; frame p50 1.60 to 2.10 ms, p95 2.00 to 4.10 ms; wall clock 76.4 to 81.7 s. The single deviation is one run's circularisation cut landing one probe step later on the eccentricity minimum, which is **0.36% of apoapsis and 0.9 m/s**. Determinism is not a bonus here, it is the demo risk retired: a flight that varies run to run is a flight that will fail in front of somebody.
  **Per-phase render cost, which the W9 acceptance only measured at two points:** pad 70 draw calls / 455k triangles (the near scene is full of terrain, props and the base), mid-ascent at 20 km 58 / 131k, just after staging at 29 km 40 / 128k, orbit 36 / 116k. The budget is 150 and the alert is 300, so the whole flight sits between a quarter and a half of it.
  **`flightabuse.js` is the deliverable that matters more than the fixes.** It mashes the stage key on the pad with the throttle shut, throttles up under the clamp, presses Escape, Tab, V and the left mouse button in flight, opens the ASSEMBLY BAY at 20 km and closes it again, presses the board key while the bay is open, tries to climb out at altitude, points retrograde under power, mashes the warp ladder to its top and back, aborts the ascent, stages a coasting vehicle, slams the throttle, rides the whole re-entry at 1000x without touching a key, and then checks the player can get out, walk and dig. **Every row records what the state was before and after, so a run reports the whole table instead of stopping at the first defect, and a row that breaks nothing but leaves the player STUCK still fails.**
- **[Phase S / W12] THE MAP, THE MANEUVER NODE, THE SAS MODES AND THE FLIGHT CAMERA. BUILT + GREEN.** New in `/core`: [`maneuver.h`](../../core/include/of/maneuver.h) + [`test_maneuver.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_maneuver.cpp) (**242 checks, 9 tests**) + a `maneuver_tests` target; `orbital.h` gained `Basis`/`basisAt`; `flight.h` gained four `SasMode` values. New on the bridge at **ABI 11** (atomic, and the commit boots): [`web/wasm/of_maneuver_api.inc`](../../web/wasm/of_maneuver_api.inc) with `of_mn_plan` / `of_mn_path` / `of_mn_orbit_meta`, and `of_fl_set_sas` widened from 0..4 to 0..8. New in the client: `web/src/sim/ManeuverAbi.ts`, `web/src/ui/{MapTypes,MapView,MapDraw}.ts` + `styles/map.css`, `web/src/app/{MapMode,MapBoot,DebugMap}.ts`, `web/tools/smoke/probes/maneuver.js`. Decisions PH-37 to PH-43.
  **The node maths was pinned to a HAND COMPUTATION done on paper before any of it ran**, the way the vessel lane pinned Tsiolkovsky: `v_circ` at r = 680 km is **2278.931638 m/s**, `v_peri` of the 680 -> 800 km transfer is **2369.520287**, so the burn is **90.588649 m/s** and it must produce an **80 x 200 km** orbit; on the Ascender's upper stage (m0 **3705 kg**, Isp 360 s, mdot **16.9953 kg/s**) that is **5.5227 s** of burn and a **2.7614 s** lead. `test_maneuver.cpp` asserts all six, then re-derives the propellant independently and checks that spending a whole stage's delta-v takes exactly `StagePerformance::burnTimeS`, which vessel.h computed by a different route.
  **THEN IT WAS FLOWN, which is the assertion that matters, because a node that predicts an orbit you cannot fly to is worse than no node.** The plan is handed to `FlightSim`, SAS holds the node's own published direction, the throttle opens at `timeToBurnStartS` and shuts `burnDurationS` later, and the orbit that comes out is compared with the orbit that was predicted: **apoapsis 199,980.6 m against a predicted 200,000 (19.4 m, 0.0097%), periapsis exact to 0.0 m, period within 1 s**, with the delivered impulse 90.577 against 90.589 m/s. The tolerance is the measured residue plus headroom and is stated as a fraction, never tuned.
  **Driven in the browser, at orbit, on a freshly built bundle:** M opens the map (a real key press through `Input.act`), the panel draws **192 current points and 192 planned points** with markers `body, air, ap, pe, ap-plan, pe-plan, node, ship, scale`, and a node placed at apoapsis with 90 m/s prograde reads **dV 90.0 m/s (green), have 1588 m/s, burn 3.6 s, LIGHT IT IN 16:35, node in 16:37, result AP 199.5 km, result PE 92.4 km**. The node marker appears on the navball (`markersDrawn` gains `node`, and loses it again on clear) and the hold-node KEY (Digit 8) puts SAS in CMD on the node. Camera: `looks` 0 -> 8 on a driven mouse move (yaw 0 -> 45.8 deg), `zooms` 0 -> 6 with the distance clamping at both published bounds (6 m and 150 m), and `V` flips to a cockpit with the hull hidden. Screenshots `docs/screenshots/W12_map_node.png`, `W12_cockpit.png`.
  **Gates: 30/30 ctest** (was 29/29, +`maneuver_tests`), self-determinism **126/126**, cross-toolchain exact **108/108**, `npm --prefix web run check` green at **190 files**.
- **[Phase R / R18] THE WALKER CAN NO LONGER FALL OUT OF THE WORLD. BUILT + GREEN (PH-60 to PH-63).** New: [`web/src/player/RockEscape.ts`](../../web/src/player/RockEscape.ts) (the two rules), [`web/src/player/StructurePort.ts`](../../web/src/player/StructurePort.ts) (the structural port lifted out of `VoxelCollision.ts`, which was over its cap: boxes on a 1 m LATTICE and boxes in ROTATED frames are different geometry and were sharing a file), [`probes/burylevel.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/burylevel.js) (the depth sweep with no seed in it), [`probes/burieddiag.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/burieddiag.js) (the entry-tick measurement), [`probes/wallstuck.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/wallstuck.js) (the wall-embed drive). Changed: `VoxelCollision.floorBelow` returns a `FloorAnswer`, `KinematicBody` reads it, `StandTrace` gains `buried` and `ejectM`, `mtnfall.js` gains a fourth check that does not depend on the floor ever being lost.
  **The headline is that the same defect has TWO routes and only one of them needed a seed.** `mtnfall.js` seed 991733 goes from three named failures (**4624 ticks with 0 grounded, 0 pushes, 29,208 m down**) to **4/4 green with `lostFloor: false`, the last grounded tick 5975 of 5976, and the player still in their cave 27.625 m under the surface**. The trace shows the buried state is entered **126 times in that one drive** and every one is back on footing within a second, with **0 ejects**: rule 1 owns all of them. `burylevel.js` reaches the identical state on flat ground with the levelling key, and there rule 2 owns it: **one eject per burial, of 2.214 / 3.004 / 5.004 / 8.004 / 12.004 m against raises of 2 / 3 / 5 / 8 / 12 m, then 600 of 600 grounded ticks at 0.000000 m of spread.** The same probe on the pre-fix build fails six checks with the player **728 to 738 m** below the surface and a **701.016924 m** spread in the ten-second window, at every one of those depths.
  **Both builds stamp `3b486f9+dirty`** and BT-27 cannot tell them apart, because five other lanes have uncommitted work in this checkout; the discriminator for every number above is the port, `:5457` before and `:5459` after, and that is stated rather than papered over.
  **REGRESSION SWEEP, eleven walker and terrain probes, before and after, with every failure re-run on the BEFORE build before being called a regression. NOTHING GREEN ON MAIN IS RED AFTER.** `tunnelsink` **21/21 at both sites, spread 0 m, 0 snap-ups, 0 of 600 ratifying ticks**, and its negative control (`oldFloor`) still fails **9 of 21** with a 0.2615 m sink and 600/600 ratified, so the probe still tests something; `mtnstand` after a REAL browser reload with 267 cells restored from IndexedDB, **spread 0 m over 3000 ticks, 3000/3000 grounded and under rock**; `mtncave` 442 cells over 44 jittered strikes, **18 stand points and not one of them moved the feet** under 33.3 m of rock; `decksink` 300/300 ticks with the deck winning and the feet 0.000002 m off its top face; `jump` apex **0.862 m** / airtime **0.833 s**, bit-identical before and after; `walkfeel` ratio 0.998 against 1.000 with the stall count unchanged at 18 (different leg lengths, so it is not a like-for-like); `tunnelwalk`, `mtnbore`, `dig`, `walk` (376.5 m, 100% grounded) all green. **Two probes are red on main and were red before this work**: `tunnelmouth`'s `sheetTracksFieldOverDugGround` (the chunk sheet stands 4.04 m above the field over dug ground, byte-identical on both builds, and it is a rendering question), and `basereal`'s phase 2 "never got onto a deck", which I re-ran on the before build myself and which fails there identically (90 parts, phases 0 and 3 flat at spread 0 on both). `basereal` was green at PH-47 four hours ago, so something in the live build or placement lanes broke it since; **it is not this lane's and it is flagged rather than chased.**

Decisions PH-64 to PH-74. **Regression sweep on this build: `ascent` PASS, `flightabuse` 14/14 (its two save rows INVERTED rather than deleted: they asserted PH-30's refusal and now assert that the autosave HAPPENS while strapped in and that what it wrote is a real record with a design and its fuel, which is strictly stronger and fails against the old build), `maneuver` 64/64, `clamprestore` 6/6.**
  **THIS IS THE FOUNDATION AND DELIBERATELY NOT THE FEATURE.** There is no control handoff and no re-entry UI; the seam they need is published in `ResumeBoot.ts` §5 and every name in it is exercised by a probe, which is the difference between a seam and a hope.
  **The measured properties are recorded in the R-items and in PH-65; the headline is that the analytic path is EXACT where it claims to be and small where it can only be close.** Path independence is asserted with `==` rather than a tolerance, in `/core` and again in the browser; the rails answer against a genuinely flown 60 s of integration is **1.0e-4 m** in `/core` and **6.5e-5 m** in the browser; and a reload round-trips a vessel's position to **0.000e+00 m** in all three states, because the clock is stamped and the world was not running.

BUILT + GREEN (PH-86 to PH-89), client-only, ABI 21 untouched.** New: [`web/src/sim/DayCycle.ts`](../../web/src/sim/DayCycle.ts), [`probes/daycycle.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/daycycle.js), [`probes/nightshot.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/nightshot.js), [`tools/smoke/dayreload.mjs`](../../web/tools/smoke/dayreload.mjs). Changed, one seam each: `Systems.ts` (the fixed-tick advance), `Debug.ts` (`setTime` pins; `stats().sky.day`), `FlightSession.ts` (the warp credit), `SaveGame.ts` (`dayT` stamped at `writeSlot`, stashed at `readSlot`; written beside the rocks lane's then-uncommitted `rocks?` hunk, which landed first as WG-67 to WG-72, so the filtered-blob isolation prepared for it was verified and then not needed). **Acceptance: rate exact by tick count; warp parity exact (MET +4000.00 s = 4.00 base + 3996.00 credit); a save cut at a pinned 0.42 reloads at 0.42129 across a real `page.reload()`; a slot with no `dayT` boots at the solve, so Reid's existing base is untouched; `vesselreload.mjs --phase=all` and `clamprestore` green on the cycle build; the standard smoke green booted at full night; `leafvar`/`wind` (the bit-exact frame pairs) green.** Screenshots `PH86_noon/dusk/night/night_lamp/nightboot.png`.
- **[Phase S] DEFERRED, and the next lanes need to know** (see §7 R5 to R9): no WASM bridge for any of this (out of scope by instruction); no vessel↔terrain contact, so landing legs, touchdown and the launch clamp are unbuilt; no docking logic (only the capture cone as data); no electric-charge budget (solar/battery are data, nothing consumes them); no parachute deploy dynamics; no multi-part structural flex (a vessel is a compound rigid body, R1); no reaction-wheel electricity cost; body rotation is carried as `FlightEnvironment::bodySpinRadS` and is 0 because `BodyParams` has no rotation rate yet.
- **[Phase R / the airlock and the spacewalk] THE STATION IS A PLACE YOU CAN LEAVE, AND SO IS A ROCKET. BUILT + GREEN (PH-105 to PH-111), client-only, ABI 21 untouched.** New: [`web/src/game/VesselGravity.ts`](../../web/src/game/VesselGravity.ts), [`probes/airlock.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/airlock.js), [`probes/eva.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/eva.js), [`tools/smoke/evaorbit.mjs`](../../web/tools/smoke/evaorbit.mjs) (the sixth standalone runner), [`tools/smoke/powerreload.mjs`](../../web/tools/smoke/powerreload.mjs) (the seventh). Changed, one seam each: `SpaceStation.ts` (the asset, the sockets, the retracted PH-91 comment), `StationGravity.ts` (decks not bounding box, the airlock rule, the persisted flag), `Boot.ts` (load the glb before installing), `SaveGame.ts` (`stationPower` at the choke point), `FlightMode.ts` (`canEva`/`evaOut` beside `disembark`), `DebugFlight.ts` (the `eva` verb), `FlightReport.ts` (the live sim's unrounded `pos`), `DebugGameplay.ts` (`proxyBoxes`, `airlockX`). Repaired: `stationwalk.js` and `orbitdeck.js`, both green against the real geometry and both now reading coordinates off `proxyBoxes` by name rather than out of the Blender source.
  **Commits `8b221eb` (PH-105 to PH-108) and `4f48e8e` (PH-109 to PH-111).** `orbitdeck.js` was not broken by the swap but was green FOR THE WRONG REASON: it held 2.5 m width and 2.5 m headroom as private constants and called them "the Blender lane's stated convention", and neither is the shipped convention. Its ceiling-leak control had to MOVE, because at 4.0 m of headroom the walker's jump cannot reach the ceiling at all and the control would have reported "contained" for a slab nothing ever touched.
  **Acceptance, driven: `airlock.js` PASS** (57 proxies, cappedAft FALSE, monotone field, ramp 1.436289 against a predicted 1.436290, weightless AND onDeck both true after the walk, out to local x -50.700, coast 8.000 m then 8.000 m); **`evaorbit.mjs` PASS** (pad control refuses and says so, flyto reaches orbit, `eva.js` green with sim-vs-record 0.000000 m); **`powerreload.mjs` PASS on the claim and on both controls**; `stationwalk.js` and `orbitdeck.js` green; `check:proxies` OK at 52 assets / 141 proxies / 190 sockets, both directions.
- [Phase 1] Full per-part aerodynamics + atmospheric entry (Spike 2 ships single-`Cd·A` only); surface vehicle physics; baked-mesh collision option if contact-query volume bottlenecks (PH-5 deferral).
- [Phase 1] Multi-part flex joints beyond the spike's compound-rigid default (KSP wobble mitigations 1/3/4 are built+stubbed in the spike).
- [Phase 3] Cross-platform determinism verification of the custom integrator with networking (D-004).

## 7. Open questions & risks
- **R83 CLOSED 2026-08-03 (PH-204), ruled by Admin: change the form, and change Forge's published answer.** Taken with both conditions met. **The original entry's evidence was WRONG and the corrected numbers are in PH-204**: it quoted a 0.283 to 0.682 spread over 10 to 100 km, but three of those seven rows came from flights that had not completed inside the sweep's time limit, so their delta-v was a partial ascent. Re-flown to completion at five altitudes, with a `CHECK(f.orbit)` per row so the mistake cannot recur: **the old fraction spans 0.348460 and the new one 0.095983, a 72.5 per cent reduction in variation.** The conclusion held; the evidence for it did not, and finding that out cost one re-run. **Forge is exact at the flown vehicle's own radius (3722.910000 against 3722.91) and the published 80 km figure moves -3.741460 m/s**, which is written into `test_maneuver.cpp` beside the old value rather than left to be discovered.
- **R63 CLOSED 2026-08-03 (PH-200, PH-203), by flying it, which is what the previous attempt correctly refused to guess at.** The refusal is lifted for Cinder and kept for every body still unflown. **All three things the aborted attempt established turned out to be true and load-bearing**: the reference lander's pad TWR really is 10.2 (rising to 25.3 as the tank drains, which is where "about 15" came from), a full-throttle ascent really does measure an escape rather than a profile, R14 really is the trap, and the insertion really does need a proportional last tick. **What the attempt could not have known is that the hardest defect was none of those**: the small-angle time-to-apoapsis is valid only near apoapsis and was being used to decide whether the vehicle was near apoapsis. See PH-200b, and its item (4) is the one that flew a lander through the middle of the moon.
- **R83 (SUPERSEDED by the CLOSED entry above, 2026-08-03 PH-204. Original text kept for the argument, NOT for its numbers, which came from incomplete flights and are corrected in PH-204.)** **R83 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-203, MEDIUM, FOR ADMIN TO RULE, and it is a FORM problem rather than a value one): `ascentDvMS`'s loss fraction is not a constant of the body, it is a constant of the body AND the parking altitude.** Flown on Cinder at six parking altitudes the published fraction moves **0.297 at 5 km, 0.283 at 10 km, 0.315 at 15 km, 0.346 at 20 km, 0.394 at 30 km, 0.491 at 50 km and 0.682 at 100 km** -- a factor of 2.4 -- while the loss over the two-impulse impulsive ideal barely moves (**0.235 to 0.317**). The cause is that `dv = vPark + vSurf * loss` charges the ideal transfer to the loss term, and the ideal transfer grows with the parking radius. **The better form is `dv = idealTwoImpulse(R -> rPark) + vSurf * genuineLoss`**, which would make Forge and Cinder directly comparable and would hold across altitudes. **It is not taken unilaterally because it changes Forge's published answer away from its own reference point**, and `of_ap_design_reach` draws that number on a screen. The Cinder constant is calibrated at a LOW parking orbit, which is the case a lander returning to a waiting ship actually flies, and the header states that domain rather than hiding it.
- **R84 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-200, LOW, and it is a mechanism whose VALUE could not be demonstrated): the ascent's terrain-clearance clamp costs 41.6327 m/s and buys 0.1539 m of clearance on the only two Cinder pads a search could find.** It fires 3,026 times on a real launch off a flat pad, so it is reachable and not dead code; with it removed the flight still reaches orbit. **Two earlier versions of its control passed on noise, and one of them keyed its own metric off the profile under test**, which is INSTRUMENTS.md's dominant failure occurring inside this lane's own file. So the benefit is NOT asserted: it is printed and said out loud, and the guard is kept against a pad geometry Cinder's generator did not produce within the search grid (a deep pit with a rim inside about 8 km). **Whoever adds the second moon should either find that geometry and pin the clamp against it, or delete the clamp and take the 41.6 m/s back.** Related and smaller: the pit pad clamps only 29 times against the plain's 3,026, because the pitch schedule is drawn against DATUM-relative apoapsis and a pad 2.1 km below the datum is still asking for almost no pitch.

- **R71 CLOSED 2026-08-03 (PH-165), AND THE DIAGNOSIS IN THE ORIGINAL ENTRY WAS WRONG, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH KEEPING.** It said "the SAS PD controller does not settle below it at the coarser step". Measured in isolation, it settles from every slew angle including exactly 180 degrees, at 0.002, 0.02 AND 0.05, in under 8 s, to a rate of 0.00000: the PITCH/YAW controller was never the problem. **The real defect is in the ROLL channel and it is a dt-dependent instability**: `stabilityAssist` built its rate term with the transverse inertia and `FlightSim::step` then divides the roll component by `Iyy`, so the roll loop ran at `kd (Itrans/Iroll)` and one step at 1/60 turned +1e-3 rad/s into **-1.3132e-3**. Threshold ratio 40.0 at the browser's tick; the shipped Ascender is 46.26. An ordinary ascent reaches **41.98 deg/s** with nothing injected. **The gate was right and must not be widened, and Admin's instinct on that was correct.** Kept for the record because the shape generalises: the entry named a symptom, a plausible mechanism and a fix, and the mechanism was the one part that had not been measured.
- **R72 CLOSED 2026-08-03 (PH-169), AND IT WAS THE DRIVER, NOT THE BRIDGE.** `apDriveAutopilot`'s gate and slot state are correct and were not touched. The previous driver put the ship in `circular80km`'s sense while `cinderStateAt` runs the other way, and departed at t+60 s with the moon at an arbitrary phase; **on that fixture the closest approach to Cinder is 11,320,004 m against a 2,400,000 m sphere**, so the handoff had nothing to fire on. Kept for the record: the suspects the entry named (`slot.target`, `p.crossesSoi`) were both innocent, and reading them rather than driving the flight would have found nothing.
- **R73 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-165, LOW, and it is a CONSEQUENCE of a fix rather than a defect): `holdRoll` MAY NO LONGER EARN ITS PLACE, AND ITS COMMENT NOW MISATTRIBUTES ITS OWN EVIDENCE.** [`FlightAttitude.holdRoll`](../../web/src/sim/FlightAttitude.ts) exists because "a vessel that picks up half a degree per second on the way up arrives in orbit lying on its side ... Measured on the first orbit captured: ROL -93 degrees with no roll input given at any point", attributed there to an aerodynamic derivative that does nothing in vacuum. **That was R71.** Measured after the fix, the reference rocket's ascent peaks at 2.1e-15 deg/s of roll, so on the reference vehicle the damper now has nothing to do. It is NOT simply removable: an asymmetric rocket a player can build today (one fin instead of four) still develops a real 5.55 deg/s of aerodynamic roll, correctly damped and now step-independent, and a player rolling on purpose still wants settling afterwards. So the question is whether a 20 deg/s kinematic rewrite of `right` is still the right shape, and it belongs to whoever owns the navball. **Flagged rather than touched, because a damper that also masks a control defect is exactly the thing to remove only when someone is looking at it.**

The fix is a widened map value (position plus the face and roll axes read off the same `matrixWorld`), which is a few lines in a file this lane did not write. **Not touched, because it is the client half of a seam whose /core half has just been published, and PH-157's pattern is that one side publishes and the other binds.**
- **R77 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-170, and it is a DESIGN PUBLISHED RATHER THAN BUILT): THE CAPTURE TEST HAS TO RIDE INSIDE `of_fl_step`, FOR EXACTLY THE REASON `of_ap_tick` WAS REFUSED.** A swept test needs the pose at BOTH ENDS of a tick, and only the step has both: a client calling a capture export between steps can supply one pose and would have to remember the previous one, in the right order, in both step exports, under warp, and forgetting it is a SILENT failure in which two vessels pass through each other. So the shape is the autopilot's: `of_dk_arm_target(f, ...)` to declare what this flight is docking with, the test driven from inside `of_fl_step`/`of_fl_step_n`, and `of_dk_status(f)` to read the result and the refusal. **The open question is where the station's port pose comes from per tick**, because D-014 makes the station genuinely orbit, so it is not a constant: either the client supplies it with the arm and the bridge propagates it, or the bridge learns the station, and that is a core-engine frame question rather than a physics one. **No `of_dk_*` export exists yet and that is deliberate**: R15's rule is that a capability is not shipped until a key or a button reaches it, and publishing four exports with no caller is how `of_atmo_*` came to be API surface that reads as a feature. - **R78 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-170, FOR BUILD-TOOLING AND THE ASSET LANE, and it was demonstrated rather than argued): THE ASSET GATE COMPARES SOCKET POSITIONS AND HAS NO OPINION ABOUT AXES, AND A 180 DEGREE AXIS ERROR IS INVISIBLE TO A POSITION CHECK.** The asset lane found the station's `socket_dock` shipped facing back into its own hull, so it accepted a vessel arriving from INSIDE the station, and nothing caught it. **`test_docking.cpp` now demonstrates precisely why**: with the one minus sign removed from `matedPose`, the loop-closure test's POSITION assertion still passes (the ports still land on each other, to 1e-9 m) and only the AXIS assertions fail, with a cone error of exactly pi. A gate built on positions cannot see this class at all, by construction. `test_docking.cpp` asserts the station socket's face axis points away from the hull and that both published roll axes are perpendicular to their face axes, which puts one instance of the check in /core; **the general fix belongs in the asset gate, which should compare axes wherever it compares sockets.**
- **R70 CLOSED 2026-08-03 (PH-172), and it was upgraded from a routing question to a measured defect by the rendering lane before it was fixed.** The static offset in `sim_world.h` is gone; the frame is installed from `orbital::cinderStateAt(0)` and advanced from the same function every tick, so there is one ephemeris. That is still true and nothing here changes it. What changes is that the gap is now REACHABLE rather than hypothetical: `sim_world.h`'s Cinder frame moves at 542.5 m/s, so anything parented to it and integrated absolutely will be left behind at **9.04 m per 1/60 s tick**. **The same term is what R67 needed for a station and what R77 needs for a docking approach**, which is three features waiting on one piece of core-engine work, and Admin is holding it deliberately to open once.
- **R80 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-174, MEDIUM, and it is a REAL LIMIT rather than an oversight): AUTO-APPROACH ONLY WORKS ON A PORT MOUNTED ON THE STACK AXIS, and it refuses the rest by name.** `flight.h` has no roll command, deliberately (PH-44: roll through `commandDirection` silently dropped a vessel out of PROGRADE into COMMAND), so the only attitude a program can ask for is a direction for the NOSE. If the port's face axis IS the nose, that one direction determines the whole docking attitude and the command is exact; if the port is mounted radially, the same nose direction admits a whole circle of roll angles and only some of them point the port at anything. `guide` therefore returns `Leg::Aborted` with `cannot auto-approach on a port that is not on the stack axis` rather than aiming approximately, and the positive control in the same test proves the refusal is about the MOUNTING and not the geometry. **The shipped `DockingPort` is `RadialOrigin::Axis` with `nodeTop` and `nodeBottom`, so every port a player can currently build through the VAB's stack nodes is fine.** Fixing it properly means a roll command in `flight.h`, which is a bigger decision than docking and should be taken on its own terms; the alternative (having the approach program write `right` directly, as `levelWings` does) would be a second attitude authority and is refused for the same reason PH-168's second SAS mirror was.
- **R81 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-174, LOW, and it is deliberate): THERE IS NO `of_dk_*` OR `of_ap_approach_*` BRIDGE EXPORT, so neither docking layer is reachable from the client yet.** Both layers are /core and tested; what is missing is the bridge, and R77 already states the shape it has to take (the capture test needs BOTH ends of a tick, so it must ride inside `of_fl_step` exactly as `apDriveAutopilot` does, for the same reason `of_ap_tick` was refused). The blocking question is still where the station's port pose comes from per tick under D-014, which Admin is opening with core-engine as one piece of work. **Publishing exports with no caller is what R15 names as API surface that reads as a feature**, so they are held until the frame answer exists and the gameplay lane is ready to bind, exactly as PH-157 sequenced the autopilot.
- **R63 UPDATED 2026-08-03 (PH-177 attempted, NOT closed, and the attempt is worth more than a silence).** The refusal stands and `ascentDvMS` still answers for Forge alone. I tried to fly the Cinder measurement and **stopped after three edits produced byte-identical output**, which is the signature INSTRUMENTS.md names for not understanding a failure, and reverted rather than leaving a half-understood flight in the tree. **What the attempt DID establish, and all of it is needed by whoever finishes it:** (1) **the reference lander's thrust-to-weight on Cinder is about 15**, so a full-throttle ascent passes the 807.5 m/s escape speed in seconds and an ascent flown that way measures how fast an over-engined vehicle can leave rather than an ascent profile; throttling to a TWR of 2 is part of the fixture, not a convenience. (2) **R14 BITES HERE AND IT IS THE REAL TRAP**: a near-radial climb has e = 1 to within rounding, so `summarize().bound` is FALSE for the first seconds of every launch, and a cutoff gated on `bound` never fires during the phase it exists to watch. The apoapsis has to be computed from the state vector directly. (3) **The circularisation needs `autopilot.h`'s proportional last tick**; at TWR 15 one full tick delivers 0.4 m/s and a burn needing 0.2 more overshoots every tick until the close-enough test passes on the far side. Three flights ended hyperbolic at e 1.00189 having spent 963.77 m/s on an orbit that wanted 544.39. **The honest next step is a proper airless ascent program**, which is `descent.h`'s mirror image and belongs beside it rather than inside a test, and then the calibration falls out of flying it. **The prediction R63 made is still unmeasured**: that an airless body's losses are a few per cent against Forge's 35%.
- **R82 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-175, LOW, FOR WHOEVER WIRES A LANDING UI): `landing.h` DECIDES WHETHER AN ARRIVAL WAS A LANDING AND NOTHING STOPS THE VEHICLE.** It is a gate, not a contact solver: a vessel that `evaluate` calls `landed` keeps its velocity and keeps integrating, and one it calls crashed does too. That is the correct shape for the layer (a gate can be honest about what it refuses; a half-built contact model cannot), but it means the CALLER owns what happens next, and the client's existing answer is `FlightSession`'s DOWN state, which is an ARREST with no gear, friction, tip-over or impact tolerance and says so in its own comment. **The four numbers the gate refuses above (5 m/s vertical, 2 m/s lateral, 15 degrees of tilt, 20 degrees of slope) are stated policy and are not measured off a structural model, because there is not one.** They are the first thing to re-derive the day parts carry a load rating.
- **R70 (SUPERSEDED by the CLOSED entry above, 2026-08-03 PH-172. Original text kept.) (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-162, FOR ADMIN TO ROUTE, and it is a WORLD decision rather than a physics one): DOES CINDER ACTUALLY ORBIT?** `sim_world.h` installs it as a STATIC frame offset at (1.2e7, 0, 0) and nothing advances it, which is consistent with a build that has no body rotation and no sun in /core. But `transfer.h` solves transfers against a Cinder that MOVES, because a rendezvous with a stationary moon is not the problem anyone means, and PH-159's whole moon flight assumes the moving one. **Neither side had to invent a number**: `orbital::cinderStateAt` is phased so `t = 0` is bit-exactly the frame graph's offset (verified `=== 1.2e7`), so a caller picks which world it is in and the export lies to nobody. **But two callers picking differently is the two-authority failure**, so it needs settling: if Cinder orbits, the frame graph has to advance it and the walker's missing carrier term (R67) arrives on the moon as well as the station; if it does not, `transfer.h`'s tests are describing a world that does not exist. Physics recommends it orbits, and notes that this is the same underlying gap as R67.
- **R69 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-159, PHYSICS OWNS IT, and it is asserted as a KNOWN GAP rather than tolerated): `replanCaptureAtHandoff` PRICES THE CAPTURE 232 m/s DEARER THAN THE BURN THAT IS ACTUALLY FLOWN.** The moon program's total is 1385.2607 m/s and the vehicle spends 1153.6880 reaching the orbit it reaches. **Every other figure in the suite matches its plan to well under a metre per second**, so this is a specific defect in that one function's pricing and not general slack. The flown orbit is real and bound (292750.2 m, e 0.106588, periapsis 61.5 km up) but it is not the 270369.7 m circle the plan asked for. **The test asserts the GAP itself to 5 m/s** so it cannot quietly grow, and the check that would have hidden it was NOT widened. Suspects, in order: the re-planned periapsis time is bisected from the entry state but the burn is centred with a lead computed against a different mass; and `planCapture` assumes the arrival velocity at periapsis is purely tangential, which is exact on the ideal conic and only nearly true on the flown one.
- **R68 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-159, and it is now DISCHARGED in `/core` but not in the client): A MOON TRANSFER NEEDS A MID-COURSE CORRECTION AND THE CLIENT HAS NO WAY TO FLY ONE.** `autopilot::midCourseCorrection` and `insertBurn` exist and are driven in ctest, but `of_ap_arm_transfer` builds a two-burn program and never inserts one, and nothing in the bridge exposes the handoff. **A body destination armed through the wasm face today will fly its injection and miss**, so the `of_ap_*` execution face is correct for `hold orbit` and for a VESSEL rendezvous and is NOT yet correct for a body. Owed: the TCM in the armed program, an SOI-crossing event from core-engine (or a poll), and `replanCaptureAtHandoff` wired to it.

PART DATA is COMPLETE (`vessel.h` 0x0115, `PartClass::Docking`, `dockCaptureRadiusM` 0.60, `dockCaptureConeRad` 30 deg, both already shipped through the ABI and read by nobody; the header's own comment says `the docking LOGIC is not built`). GAMEPLAY UI is NOTHING. PHYSICS MANOEUVRE is NOTHING and is BLOCKED by R26, this lane's own open item: the client holds exactly ONE promoted vessel, so two cannot be live in the near scene and no capture test can run. **Three decisions are Admin's before anyone starts**: whether a station port is a socket physics queries or a real `DockingPort` part instance in the station's design; whether `automatically dock` means auto-capture-on-contact or a full auto-approach (DW-29 puts docking AFTER the autopilot unlock and DW-30 item 5 is one clause with no numbers); and who owns the vessel-join, since nothing anywhere merges two part trees, masses, fuel loads and staging lists. **Physics owns the proximity and capture test, the join, and the approach program.** **CORRECTION (PH-155): R26 does NOT block the station case.** `promotedId` is a hard singleton but the station is never promoted, `installStation` puts its 57 proxies in the queryable set at boot with no gate, and both frames are body-frame absolute f64 metres so a raw distance is meaningful. The station case is blocked by R67 instead, which is smaller and is this lane's own. R26 still blocks vessel-to-vessel. Until then `rendezvous` is deliverable and `dock` is not.
- **R67 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-155, PHYSICS OWNS IT, and it is PH-90's decision coming back): THE STATION IS FROZEN IN THE BODY FRAME, SO NOTHING CAN RENDEZVOUS WITH IT.** `SpaceStation.ts` says it out loud: the station must be frozen because `KinematicBody.step` integrates an ABSOLUTE body-frame position with no carrier term, and a 7.5 km/s floor would leave the player behind at 125 m per tick. That was the right call for standing on it and it makes flying to it impossible: a ship in a real 400 km orbit passes a stationary station at 7.6 km/s. **A naive per-tick sphere test also tunnels**: 7.6 km/s at 60 Hz is 127 m per tick against a 0.60 m capture radius, so capture must be a swept test or a relative-frame one, never a point-in-sphere. **The shape of the answer already exists**: `VesselRegistry.stateOf` handles `rec.where.kind === 'fixed'`, so capture can be demote-and-pin to a station-relative fixed pose, which matches the station's own fiction instead of fighting it. **This is the decision that has to be made before any docking code is written**, and it is bigger than docking: it is whether an orbiting structure is ever dynamically in orbit, which also decides what a player-built station does. Physics owns it; it needs an Admin ruling on the player-experience half.
- **R66 (NEW, 2026-08-03, PH-150, and it is a bound rather than a bug): THE FINITE-BURN RESIDUE SCALES WITH BURN LENGTH AND THERE IS NO TRIM PASS.** A 5.5 s burn lands 5.8 m out on a 120 km orbit raise; an 18.6 s burn lands 72 m out. Both are the impulsive plan's own error and both are reported by `maneuver::BurnEstimate::burnFractionOfPeriod` in advance. A trim burn would absorb it, and it is deliberately NOT built, because a trim has to SOLVE for its own correction and that is the one thing Admin's rule forbids the executor from doing in flight. If it is wanted it belongs as a second planned burn produced by the solver, not as a loop inside `update()`. Raised so the number is on the record before anyone is surprised by it on a long burn.
- **R64 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-149, PROCESS, and it belongs to every lane rather than to physics): THE SHARED GIT INDEX IS A SECOND COLLISION SURFACE BESIDE THE .wasm, AND IT FAILS SILENTLY.** Commit `fe1268a` landed EMPTY: its tree was byte-identical to its parent because a concurrent lane reset the index between this lane's `git add` and its `git write-tree`, two bash calls apart. **`git log --oneline` shows a normal commit with a full message and nothing looks wrong**, and `git show --stat` on it prints the subject and no files at all. The seven files were re-committed as `a1367b7`. **The rule that prevents it is one line: stage, COUNT what was staged, write-tree, verify the tree DIFFERS from the base tree, and commit-tree, all in ONE shell invocation**, so nothing can interleave. Checking that `git diff --cached --name-status` is empty at both ends does NOT catch this, because the index was already empty when it was read. Raised to Admin as a rule for the whole tree rather than as a physics note.

Physics deliberately did not fix it, even though `test_vessel.cpp` is this lane's file, because the `vessel.h` row it counts was UNCOMMITTED and bumping the literal would have made the test fail for anyone without that edit and swept another lane's work into a physics commit. Recorded because the restraint is the reusable part.
- **R61 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-144, for the factory lane): `probes/ascent.js` is red on TWO factory assertions and nothing else.** "a second machine went down next to it: smelter false, 1 buildings" and "and /core WIRED them, so the drill never saturates mid-flight: links 0". The FLIGHT half is entirely green (92.6 x 79.7 km orbit, e 0.0094, staged at 69.7 s, EVA round trip in orbit) and the tree carries uncommitted `FactoryRestore.ts` work. Raised, not chased.
- **R62 CLOSED 2026-08-02 (PH-147) by making the function refuse, on Admin's prompt.** `lambert` now BOUNDS the ambiguity before it iterates (`floor(tof / P_min)`, where `P_min` is the period of the minimum-energy conic through the two points) and returns invalid when the geometry could also have been flown with extra laps, unless the caller passes `acceptAmbiguousLaps` and says it knows. Conservative by design: it turns away some unambiguous requests, which is the right direction for a refusal. **It never trips the real workload** (maneuver_tests unchanged at 510 green) and it catches both halves of the trap.
- **R63 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-147, and it is a REFUSAL rather than a defect): THE ASCENT LEG IS CALIBRATED FOR FORGE AND REFUSES EVERY OTHER BODY.** `transfer::ascentDvMS` returns `calibrated = false` and 0 for anything but Forge, and `of_ap_design_reach` therefore refuses a launch from anywhere else, because a 35% gravity loss is what an atmosphere and a modest pad TWR cost while an airless low-gravity body's is a few percent. There is no pad off Forge today, so this costs nothing now. **Two things are owed when there is one**: a flown ascent to calibrate against, the same way Forge's was, and a BODY HANDLE on `of_ap_design_reach`, whose published signature has no room for one. Related and smaller: **the design call's `planeChange` is 0 because a launch picks its plane with its azimuth**, which is true for any inclination at or above the launch site's latitude; physics does not know the site, so a low-inclination dogleg is unpriced and the assumption is stated rather than hidden.
- **READ THIS BEFORE TAKING THE RESEARCH GATE (Admin ruling, 2026-08-02). `GameMode.researchGated` is `!sandbox`, so GATES ARE OFF IN THE MODE MOST PROBES RUN IN.** The gate itself is wired end to end and is deliberately not taken until there is a placeable generator to gate (PH-103, and Admin confirmed the sequencing). The trap is for whoever DOES take it: a gated feature will appear to work in every probe, because probes run sandboxed, and will be locked in Reid's survival world, where nobody is watching a JSON report. **Any lane that gates something must drive it with `--sandbox=0` at least once, and must say in its probe which mode the assertion was taken in.**
- **R56 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-105, FOR THE BLENDER LANE, AND IT IS A HARD BLOCKER ON THE FEATURE THIS LANE JUST SHIPPED): THE HALL THE PLAYER SPAWNS IN HAS NO DOORWAY, so the airlock is built and unreachable.** The hall's twelve wall proxies form an UNBROKEN ring: they are 4.536 m long against a 4.341 m dodecagon side, so they overlap at the corners, and **there is no jamb pair anywhere on the hall wall**. `col_HallWallA` (x 8.100 to 8.400, y 0 to 6.000, z -2.268 to 2.268) stands across the ENTIRE 3 m spine mouth at full height, and `col_HallWallG` does the same aft. Measured by `stationwalk.js` P5: walking +X from local x 3.250998 the walker stops dead at local x **8.098644**, exactly the wall's inner face, **2.251356 m short** of the `col_JambHallFwd` doorway, with **93 ticks** of `blockedByBuild`. The station is therefore in THREE disconnected pieces (forward spine + hold, hall + gallery, aft spine + reactor + hab) and `socket_hall` puts the player in the middle one. The VISUAL hull does cut portals here (`HUB_PORTAL_DEG` in `build_space_station.py`), so this is a proxy that disagrees with the mesh it stands for, and the fix is the one the corridor bulkheads already use: split `col_HallWall{A,G}` into a jamb pair plus a lintel. **Physics deliberately did NOT paper over it**: inventing the doorway here would be this lane authoring geometry, which is the two-authority failure wearing a third hat. `stationwalk.js` P5 publishes it as numbers and does not assert it, so the probe stays green until the asset can make it green honestly; promoting `hallReachesSpine` to a hard assertion is a one-line change the day a jamb exists.
- **R57 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-105, for the Blender lane, second): THERE IS A 2.099 m HOLE IN THE DECK BETWEEN THE HALL AND EACH SPINE.** `col_HallFloor` is the square inscribed in the dodecagon (+/- 5.751) but the wall ring sits at inner radius 8.100 and `col_SpineFwdFloor` starts at x = 7.850, so at z = 0 there are **2.099 m of no floor**, and up to 2.349 m at each of the twelve edge midpoints. Driven, the walker fell through it for **51 ticks (0.85 s)** and about 0.17 m before landing back on the spine deck, so it is survivable at 4.6 m/s. It is still a hole with 400 km under it, and once R56 is fixed it is on the only route between the two halves of the station.
- **R58 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-105, for the Blender lane, third, and it is the mildest): JUMP HEADROOM ON THE SPINE HAS 30 mm OF MARGIN.** Shipped clear headroom 4.000 m; a jumping player under station gravity (3.49886 m/s^2) needs 3.969915 m (free apex 2.319915 plus the walker's topmost structural sample at 1.65). Margin **+0.030085 m**, against +1.509649 m for the same corridor at surface gravity. It clears today. Any ceiling drop, any increase in the jump impulse, or any deck raised toward its ceiling puts a player's head in the slab, so the number is recorded rather than left to be rediscovered.
- **R59 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-110, for persistence, and it is EVA's and the station's shared limitation): `SavePlayerAnchor` HAS NO RADIUS, so a body in orbit reloads onto the ground.** It carries `lat`/`lon`/`alt`, and `applyPlayerAnchor` restores through `Controller.teleport`, which DISCARDS its altitude by a documented contract every walking probe depends on (Config.ts line 51). So an autosave taken during an EVA, or while standing in the station, round-trips the player to the dirt under the ground track. This is not new with EVA: it is true of PH-94's station today and has been since it shipped. It is named here because EVA makes it reachable a second way and because the fix is a schema question rather than a physics one (a radius, or a body-frame xyz plus a carrier id). `standAt` is the call that would restore it and it already exists.
- **R53 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-99, this lane's own debt): `KinematicBody.ts` went from 389 lines to 480 and is now OVER the 400-line cap it was previously inside.** `npm run check:limits` was ALREADY red at HEAD (Boot.ts 461, DebugGameplay.ts 412, Scatter.ts 682 and eleven more), so it gates nothing and this did not break a green check; but taking a compliant file to non-compliant is still a real debt and is recorded rather than absorbed. The natural split is the freefall branch, and it is deliberately NOT done in the same pass that changed the walker's behaviour, because a refactor of the walker's core and a behaviour change should not land in one commit during a live gameplay week.
- **R54 CLOSED 2026-08-02 by PH-109 to PH-111, and what it was blocked on was not what it looked like.** The expected blocker was the walker's missing carrier frame: a body beside a vessel doing 7.8 km/s should separate at 125 m per tick. It does not arise, because `flight.step(dt)` is called from inside `VesselObserver.step` and the observer is only stepped while it is the router's source, so **a promoted vessel with nobody aboard does not advance**. What was actually missing was a freefall volume on the vessel (PH-109), a door beside `disembark` (PH-110), and an un-stamp so the record stops advancing too (PH-111, and that one was found by the probe rather than by reading). Driven end to end by `tools/smoke/evaorbit.mjs`: a real pad-to-orbit ascent, then the player steps out at 80.287 km and floats at **apparentG 0.000108 against a trueG of 7.631206**, drifting 0.000643 m in three seconds while the rocket moves 0.000000 m.
- **R55 CLOSED 2026-08-02 by PH-107, and the premise was wrong rather than the fix being hard.** R55 said "the station has no way out" and named it for the Blender lane. That was true of the PLACEHOLDER (twelve boxes with `col_CorrCap` shutting the far end) and FALSE of the mesh that had already landed: `col_SpineAftFloor` runs to x = **-28.000** with no cap beyond it, because `BLOWN_X = -28.00` is the asset's own "the bulkhead that failed" and `AFT_X = -36.00` is "the torn aft rim, open to space". The way out was in the file the whole time; the GRAVITY did not know, because it was one AABB round the hull. Nothing was authored. See PH-106 and PH-107, and `probes/airlock.js`.
- **R48 (UPDATED 2026-08-02 by PH-104, and zero g moves its severity in BOTH directions).** **Worse:** under weight a player meets a ceiling only at a jump apex; in freefall they arrive at one under sustained thrust every time they press Space, so a horizontal proxy thinner than the 0.75 m sample gap stops being an edge case and becomes the normal case. `probes/zerog.js` Z6 now asserts the rise PLATEAUS, which is the leak detector, and that assertion is asset-independent. **Better:** the shipped `OVERHEAD_T = 0.8 m` already exceeds the gap, Z6 measures the ceiling holding at exactly 0.85 m, and with the generator OFF there is no jump at all (thrust is governed at 4.0 m/s and contact is inelastic), so headroom stops being a safety number and becomes a navigation one. **The asset rule "every overhead proxy at least 0.8 m thick" is now load-bearing rather than a nicety.** The Blender lane's authored 4.0 m headroom independently retires the other half (PH-93's 2.319915 m jump apex at 3.5 m/s^2 against 2.5 m of clearance). Original text below.
- **R51 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-97, for build-tooling):** `reload.mjs` **silently discards unknown flags**, where `run.mjs` line 261 exits 2 and explains that a discarded flag fails "in the FLATTERING direction". Same file also has no `--verify=probes/x.js`: its phase 2 is a fixed inline evaluate, so no existing probe can be re-run after a real reload, which is why five standalone runners exist. Adding `--verify` would convert every in-page `save()`/`load()` leg in the suite into a real-reload leg for free. Not taken: that file is build-tooling's.
- **R52 (NEW, 2026-08-02, observation, not mine):** `probes/tunnelsink.js` fails its `built` site ("the floor would not go down in the bore"), 32 of 33 checks. **The station is exonerated by control: it fails identically at `?station=0`.** The probe is unmodified vs HEAD, but the working tree carried another lane's uncommitted terrain and water changes (`TerrainBoot.ts`, `Terrain*.ts`, `WaterSurface.ts` moved) and a dev server serves the working tree, so that is the likely author. Flagged, not chased.
- **R48 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-93, URGENT for the Blender lane):** **the walker has no ceiling authority.** Floors are an exact solve (`deckUnder`); everything above the feet is three point samples 0.75 m apart. Two consequences the station asset must answer: a horizontal proxy thinner than 0.75 m is passed through, and 2.5 m of clear headroom is a SURFACE-gravity number (9.6 mm of margin) that fails by 1.47 m at 400 km, where the jump apex is 2.319915 m instead of 0.828 m. Three candidate answers, none taken here because the choice is the asset's and Admin's: author ~4.0 m clear headroom, thicken horizontal proxies past 0.75 m and accept a head bump, or give the walker a real ceiling query. **The third is the only one that closes the class**, and it is a genuine piece of new walker work (a `ceilingAbove` solve mirroring `deckUnder`, plus a rule for what a blocked rise does to `vUp`, which today is left untouched by `blockedByBuild`).
- **R49 (NEW, 2026-08-02, PH-90):** `StructureBodies` has **no spatial index**. `blocks()` is a linear scan of one shared list with an O(1) sphere reject per solid, and `resolveStep` can call it 27 times a tick. That list already has three owners (structures, launch pads, the factory) and a 60 to 100 m station would be a fourth with the largest proxy count of any of them. Not a blocker at station scale, but the first thing to measure if the tick budget moves.
- **R50 (NEW, 2026-08-02, for gameplay):** the cheat teleport **must not** be built by making `Controller.teleport` honour its `altM` argument. That parameter is discarded by a documented contract (`Config.ts` line 51, "alt is ignored (the capsule spawns ON the surface)") and both walk scenarios ship `alt: 2`, so honouring it moves the spawn of every walking probe in the suite by two metres. `Controller.standAt` (body-frame Cartesian, PH-90) is the door that exists; it is an instrument and deliberately does not reframe the camera.
- **R1 (mitigated, design-level):** Joint/wobble for multi-part craft. Spike 2 default = **compound rigid body** (sidesteps it entirely for the spike flow); flex joints use stiff 6-DOF constraints + fixed sub-stepping + strain-free promotion (spike2-physics §3.3). Validated by gate G10. Residual risk moves to Phase-1 multi-part craft.
- **R2 (resolved — math-level no-drift now BUILD-VALIDATED, PH-6):** on-rails↔active handoff smoothness — **no-drift guarantee** via lossless conic fit on demote + strain-free re-seed on promote (spike2-physics §2.2, gate G6). The *math-level* half is now demonstrated in `test_physics.cpp`: park→resume is identity on (r,v) for elliptic + hyperbolic, and the custom symplectic integrator tracks the analytic conic with bounded energy (max \|dE/E\|=2.4e-11 over 30 orbits) — secular energy-drift retired (G3/G6). The *UE5-integration* half (strain-free joint re-seed, the load-in-wobble seam) still pends the UE5 project (gate G6 in-engine).
- **R3 (Admin Q1):** time-warp semantics when other players have active vessels (MP) — deferred, networking-coupled. Unchanged by Spike 2.
- **R5 (PARTLY CLOSED, 2026-07-27, PH-20):** the PAD end is solved with a client-side launch clamp and needs no `/core` change. **The LANDING end is not, and is deliberately not half-built:** `DOWN` freezes the state at the surface, which is an arrest and not a touchdown. Landing legs, gear compression, friction, tip-over and impact tolerance remain unbuilt, and `LandingLeg::legReachM` and the `Leg_Deploy` clip remain data. A vehicle that comes down survives, which is generous, and nothing about the way it comes down is modelled. That is the next physics milestone and it is where the world-gen contact query (PH-5's `GetGroundContact`) finally has to be wired to a vessel. Original text: the vessel core has **no contact with the ground**. It flies and it orbits; it cannot sit on a launch pad, cannot be held by a clamp, and cannot land. `LandingLeg::legReachM` and the shipped `Leg_Deploy` clip exist as data. This needs the world-gen contact query (PH-5's `GetGroundContact`) wired to the vessel, which is a real piece of work and is the gap between "reaches orbit" and "completes a mission".
- **R6 (NEW, for gameplay):** the guidance that flies the acceptance ascent lives in the TEST, deliberately, because DW-29 makes reaching orbit a manual skill and autopilot a research unlock. The core publishes the ribbon (`GravityTurnProgram`) and the angle-of-attack clamp; the pilot is somebody else's. Three guidance defects found by the integrated ascent are recorded in the test where they bit (clamp-from-the-pad lock, the periapsis-catches-apoapsis cut that can never fire, and starting the circularisation burn AT apoapsis instead of leading it by half a burn). **Whoever builds the autopilot should read that driver before writing another one.**
- **R7 (NEW):** the residual eccentricity of the acceptance ascent (0.0083) is the GUIDANCE, not the physics: a pure prograde burn centred on apoapsis leaves a few percent, and real ascent guidance pitches slightly below prograde near the end to take the rest out. That belongs behind the autopilot unlock.
- **R8 (NEW, for the art lane):** one of the 24 authored parts has no mesh, `EngineVacuumSmall` (§5.1), a 1.25 x 1.00 m vacuum bell. It obeys the existing class-S contract. **The rest of the catalogue was realigned onto the art lane's names and dimensions mid-task** (see the reconciliation note in §5.1); if `build_rocket_parts.py` moves again, `vessel.h`'s `asset` strings and heights move with it, and that reconciliation should be a named job rather than something two lanes each half-do.
- **R9 (NEW, for Admin):** the ITEM form of a part (what a recipe costs, what sits in the pack) is an `ItemId` and belongs to `gameplay.h`'s pinned table. `vessel.h` deliberately has no opinion about what a part costs. Somebody has to allocate that block.
- **R18 (CLOSED, 2026-07-27, PH-60 to PH-62).** The mechanism the earlier pass diagnosed was confirmed by driven measurement at the entry tick before anything was changed, and then found to be reachable on flat ground with the levelling key, which is what made the fix an authority statement rather than a widened rung. `mtnfall.js` is green on all four checks (the fourth is new and does not depend on the floor ever being lost); `burylevel.js` sweeps the burial depth and is green at 2, 3, 5, 8 and 12 m and red on the pre-fix build at every one of them. **The one open piece is R20.** Original text: **A CAPSULE BURIED IN BEDROCK OWNS NOTHING AND FALLS OUT OF THE WORLD, PERMANENTLY.** `VoxelCollider.floorBelow` deliberately returns null when the feet are buried past one rung with rock all the way up, on the grounds (WG-31) that this is not a floor question but an embedded capsule, and that `resolveEmbedded` owns it. **`resolveEmbedded` does not own it in general**: it returns null whenever no single-axis push of at most one cell leaves the whole capsule free, which is exactly the case for a capsule inside solid rock. Neither function is wrong on its own terms; together they own nothing, and the walker then integrates gravity into bedrock for ever. **Measured, deterministic, `probes/mtnfall.js` seed 991733:** the player is grounded on a dug floor at tick 1351, `groundR` goes to -Infinity at tick 1352, and over the following **4624 ticks there are 0 grounded ticks, 0 floors and 0 pushes**, ending **29,208 m below the surface** at 12.83 m per tick and still accelerating. `of.solidAt` reads true continuously from 0.5 m to 100 km deep, so this is a fall THROUGH rock and not down a shaft. Found by `probes/mtncave.js`, which digs with a jittered aim the way a person does; 1 of 3 seeds hit it, and the clean single-yaw bore every other probe cuts never can. **NOT FIXED, deliberately:** the obvious repair is to make an unfreeable capsule freeze rather than fall (rock you cannot be pushed out of is rock you cannot fall through), which is one line and is an authority statement rather than a damping term, but it is a walker behaviour change in a state reached once on one seed, and this defect class has already been closed twice on a hypothesis. It wants its own brief. `mtnfall.js` ships reporting `valid: true, pass: false` with three named failing assertions, which is `tunnelmouth.js`'s split and is how the fix gets held to account.
- **R19 (NEW, for whoever picks up the sinking complaint next): WHERE THE SINKING DEFECT IS NOT.** PH-45 rules out, by measurement at a real mountain site: the kinematic capsule, `floorBelow`, `resolveEmbedded`, the `deep` gate, `editsHandle` flicker, the first-person eye, the third-person spring arm, the drawn chunk sheet under the feet, the chunk stream, the stitch pass, the floating origin, the render rate and the reload path. **ALL THREE OF ITS LEADS ARE NOW CLOSED BY PH-47**: player-built structures (90 parts, four standing states, 0.000000 m), the real rAF loop (1440 and 1436 ticks at 121.5 and 100.8 Hz render, 0.000000 m) and the 2.0 s `Idle` clip (continuous, no period, 44 of 48 tiles pixel-identical). The two deck gaps this risk originally carried were re-derived and do not hold as periodic mechanisms; what survives of them is a single real defect of the SAME SHAPE as R18, recorded here so it is not lost: **a capsule embedded in a 4.00 m structural WALL is stuck**, because `deckUnder` correctly refuses to lift it a whole storey and there is no structural counterpart to `VoxelCollider.resolveEmbedded`. **THAT CLAIM IS WRONG AND WAS RETRACTED BY DRIVING IT (PH-63): the player walks out, 8.926664 m in 2 s in all four directions, because `StructureBody.resolveStep` has an escape hatch the audit never exercised.** I relayed it without re-deriving it, which is the same mistake the audit made one level down, and it is the second claim from that audit to fail when somebody finally drove it. What the driving found instead is R20. **The honest state of the hunt: there is no longer a next place to look that this lane can name.** Everything on the walker's own side is measured flat under conditions strictly harder than Reid's. What is needed now is not another hypothesis but Reid's actual coordinates and view mode, because the defect is either in a state the probes cannot construct blind or in a quantity nobody has thought to instrument, and the cheapest way to find out which is to stand where he was standing.
- **R20 (NEW, and it is R18's shape in the structural port): THE EMBEDDED ESCAPE HATCH IS GLOBAL WHEN IT SHOULD BE PER-SOLID.** Both `VoxelCollider.resolveStep` and `StructureBodies.resolveStep` accept ANY destination once the capsule is not free at its start position, on the stated grounds that "the destination cannot be worse than here". **It can be worse.** Structurally, measured: a player inside one wall walks through every OTHER wall and every closed door at the full 4.6 m/s, `blockedByBuild` 0 on all 480 driven ticks, so one bad free-placement turns collision off for the whole base until they leave the box. In the voxel path the same hatch is what carried the R18 capsule sideways deep into the mountain while it fell: between the entry tick and 34 ticks later the feet dropped 1.506 m while the air above went from inside the 0.55 m rung to 30.14 m, which is how a recoverable 3.5 m burial became one with nothing free within 40 m. **PH-61 rule 1 closes the voxel consequence** (a tick that ends buried is refused, so the hatch can no longer carry anybody into bedrock) **and does NOT close the structural one**, which needs the hatch narrowed to "ignore only the solids you are currently inside". That is safe by construction (a solid you are not inside cannot be one you need to pass through to get out) but it is a behaviour change in `web/src/game/StructureBody.ts` during a live gameplay week, and it needs its own driven case: embedded in wall A, blocked by wall B, still able to leave. **Also unfixed and worth its own line: the state is SILENT.** `onDeck`, `grounded`, `blockedByBuild` and the whole stand trace read exactly as they do for somebody standing on open deck, so nothing in the client can tell that a player is inside a wall, and there is no signal to hang a warning or an auto-eject on.
- **R21 (NEW, for whoever touches the walker next): the DEEP branch has no `climbGate`.** Above ground a horizontal move onto ground more than `CAPSULE.stepUpM` above where the feet started is refused (PH-8). Underground there is no such wall: `floorBelow`'s "feet in rock, seat on the top of the slab within `riseM`" branch will lift the capsule by up to one rung EVERY TICK with no bound over time, which is 33 m/s of vertical ratchet. Measured on seed 991733, the four ticks before the R18 fall: the feet rise 0.084, 0.085, 0.127 and 0.317 m per tick while `fallM` stays a constant -0.002685, i.e. **the state that produced the double null was manufactured by `floorBelow` walking the capsule up into the hillside**, not walked into. PH-61 rule 1 makes the outcome safe (the tick it can no longer find a slab top is refused rather than fatal), so this is now a feel defect rather than a fall, but a walker that can climb a vertical rock face at a rung a tick inside a tunnel is not what PH-8 decided anywhere else.
- **R4 (RC-4, RESOLVED → PH-4 hybrid):** Chaos-vs-custom for the active vessel. Resolved: custom fixed-step integrator for flight dynamics, Chaos for contact only (fallback: fully-custom analytic contact). Confirmation gate = G8 (= Spike-1 V4, no kraken at 3 km/s). Only escalates to D-001/Admin if near-origin low-speed contact itself fails — which the hybrid is designed to avoid.

- **R10 (STILL OPEN, for rendering/world-gen):** `Regime`'s `ORBIT_START` is **100 km**, but a stable orbit on Forge starts just above the 60 km atmosphere ceiling, so the whole demo orbit (92.6 x 79.7 km) sits in the **ASCENT** band. Nothing is broken by it: the near scene empties anyway (0 near chunks, 102 far) and `probes/ascent.js` asserts the HANDOFF rather than the band NAME. But a band called ASCENT while the player coasts in a closed conic is a label that will mislead the first gameplay gate that reads it, and it is not cosmetic in one place already: `Systems.ts:230` switches the shadow pass off on `band !== 'ORBIT'`, so from an 80 km orbit the client is still fitting and rendering cascades for a surface that is 80 km away. **Recommended value: `ORBIT_START = 62_000`, just above the atmosphere ceiling, because that is the altitude at which the physics stops acting on the vessel (`atmosphere.h`'s ceiling, PH-11) and it is therefore the same boundary `onRailsEligible` already uses.** `Regime.ts` is world-gen's file and that lane is live in `web/src/world`, so this is raised for Admin to route rather than changed here.
- **R11 (CLOSED, 2026-07-28, PH-64 to PH-67).** The ABI dependency it named was the whole blocker and it was exactly right about what it would cost to skip: "restoring by replaying `stage` calls gets the right hardware and **full tanks**, i.e. free delta-v on every reload". ABI 18's `of_fl_set_propellant` is the one export it asked for, and the restore path is `refillTanks`'s own skeleton with the fuel written back. Measured across a REAL browser reload in all three vessel states, fuel round-tripping with a difference of **exactly 0** at 6490.0 kg on the pad, 3056.955416307 kg mid-ascent and 923.002605375252 kg in orbit, the last two strictly below the full load so "the fuel came back" cannot be satisfied by a rocket that never burned any. PH-30's save refusal is retired with it, which was also load-bearing rather than tidy: with the refusal in place no autosave can fire in orbit and the orbital reload could never have been proven. Original text: flight is still **not persisted**, but it is no longer LOST SILENTLY. The world save is refused while aboard, the refusal is counted, and the navball carries a standing amber chip. **What real persistence needs, and why it was not done tonight:** a vessel is its tree plus per-part propellant plus `nextStageIndex`, and **the bridge has no propellant setter**.
- **R11 original detail (kept for the argument, not for the status):** flight is still **not persisted**, but it is no longer LOST SILENTLY. While the player is aboard the world save is refused, the refusal is counted (`__of.game().persist.saveInhibit`), and the navball carries a standing amber chip. Measured end to end by `tools/smoke/reload.mjs --phase=orbit`: at 92.6 x 79.5 km, **8 saves refused against 1 allowed**, and the reload comes back with 2 buildings, 1 link, the hotbar restored, no phantom vessel and no NaN. **What real persistence needs, and why it was not done tonight:** a vessel is its tree plus per-part propellant plus `nextStageIndex`, and **the bridge has no propellant setter**. `of_fl_*` publishes `set_pos_vel`, `set_attitude`, `set_ang_vel`, `set_throttle`, `set_sas`, `set_sas_command` and `stage`, and nothing that can put a half-empty tank back. Restoring by replaying `stage` calls gets the right hardware and **full tanks**, i.e. free delta-v on every reload, which is exactly the class of "looks right and is not" this project keeps paying for. So it needs an ABI bump (rule 9, atomic, ABI is at 8 tonight and another lane moved it twice), which is not a 3 a.m. move with five lanes live. **The field is additive and optional, so `SAVE_VERSION` does NOT need to go to 6 for it and existing worlds are not invalidated.**
- **R27 (NEW, and it is a CORROBORATION plus a warning): `/core` ALREADY HAD THIS DESIGN, in [`sim_world.h`](../../core/include/of/sim_world.h), and it has never been wired to the browser.** `SimWorld` carries `VesselMode::{Active,OnRails}`, `parkToRails()`, `promoteToActive()`, `makeActiveFromCurrentState()` and a `step()` whose on-rails branch is `state_ = orbital::resume(elements_, nextTime)`. **It keeps `elements_` fixed and moves only the time asked for, which is PH-65's rule arrived at independently in Wave 1**, and that agreement is worth more than either statement alone. Two differences, both deliberate on this lane's side. **(1) It advances per TICK; this lane PULLS.** `SimWorld` calls `resume` every tick whether anyone is looking or not, which is a Kepler solve per vessel per tick for a fleet nobody can see; the client evaluates on demand and costs exactly zero when nothing asks. **(2) It re-parks on an SOI change** (`switchVesselToFrame`), and that is the ONE legitimate re-fit and the named exception to PH-65: mu changes, so the old elements describe a gravity the vessel no longer feels. When SOI transitions reach the browser, that is the shape to copy and it must be the only re-fit anywhere. **The warning:** two implementations of "a vessel on rails" now exist in this repo, one headless and unwired and one in the client, and a third would be the failure this project has paid for five times. The right eventual move is one of them, and it is not obvious which: `sim_world.h` is the fuller model (it owns the frame graph, the SOI crossing and the factory in the same tick) and it is also 100% unreachable from the browser, with no bridge export and no caller outside `core/tools/journey_dump.cpp` and two tests. **RESOLVED 2026-07-28 by Admin: the wired one wins.** `sim_world.h`'s vessel-rails API is now marked SUPERSEDED at its symbols (PH-72); the header is kept for the coverage only it has. Build on the client's. Note also what the brief for this lane assumed and what is actually there: FS-4's `Demote`/`AdvanceOnRails`/`Promote` is not a framework, it is three concrete methods on `factory::FactorySim` with no interface, no base class and no concept; `ISimProxy` exists only in the spike documents. So "compose the two halves" meant adopting the CONTRACT and the vocabulary, not calling shared code, and no such shared code exists to call.

The command is `node tools/smoke/reload.mjs --url=http://127.0.0.1:<port>/ --setup=probes/padclear.js --setupargs='{"mode":"leave"}'` and it wants a run as soon as the tree builds. **Two assertions were deliberately left OUT of that branch and should be added by whoever runs it first, from the measurement rather than from a guess: `flightLive` and the pad's `holding` across a `leave` reload.** Both are unmeasured, and the reason they are absent instead of guessed is PH-73's lesson: a bound written from an assumption asserts the assumption, not the behaviour.
- **R29 (NEW, LOW severity, and it is PH-71's shape in a place PH-71 deliberately does not reach): a vessel resumed in ORBIT reads `massKg 0` and `altitudeDatumM 0` for one frame.** Measured at the instant of `resumeControl`: `steps 0, massKg 0, altitudeDatumM 0`, while `speedMS` is already a correct 2349.22. The honest options are a one-tick genuine `of_fl_step` at promote (legitimate, since the vessel IS active again) or having the HUD suppress a zero-mass frame. - **R32 (CLOSED 2026-07-28 by PH-80, and the answer is that it cannot be stood on BY CONSTRUCTION, not by accident: `DIG.radiusM` 1.5 makes the smallest cuttable cavity 3 m tall, so the reachable depth spectrum is {0} together with [3.1, 16.8] and any threshold in (0, 3.1) is equivalent.
