Measured: the solid set went **5 to 5** across a placed machine. **A player can walk straight through every factory building in the game.** Nothing in this lane depends on that and the walker is behaving correctly given what it is handed; the question is whether factory buildings are supposed to be solid, which is factory-sim's and gameplay's call. Flagged rather than fixed.
- **R34 (NEW, and it is placement's line, not mine): A STRUCTURAL PART PRESSED INSIDE A TUNNEL APPEARS ON THE MOUNTAIN ABOVE YOU.** `StructurePlacement` addresses the build grid off `Structures.groundRadius` (`web/src/game/Structures.ts:242`), which is `_of_surface_radius`, the HEIGHTFIELD. A bore with an intact roof does not move the heightfield, so the part is placed at the surface overhead: measured 0.004 to 0.09 m from the overhead surface across four presses. **`probes/tunnelsink.js` now carries two CHARACTERISATION checks for this**, which assert the current behaviour so they fail the day it changes, deliberately WITHOUT claiming it is correct. Related and measured at the deep site: presses are refused about half the time with "ground too uneven here, the ground stands 1.95 m into it" rising to 5.55 m, which is the mountainside's own slope across a 4.00 m cell; cutting a 249-cell room around the player moved that by **0.01 m**, confirming the cause is the cell footprint and not the room.
- **R35 (NEW, and it is a GIFT FOR THE FACTORY LANE, measured here while proving my own instrument): [`FactoryStale.ts:66`](../../web/src/game/FactoryStale.ts) differences f64 against a float32 readback with no `Math.fround`, so its "hard zero" will read about 1e-5 m for any building far from the origin and will look like a small real defect.** A `BatchedMesh` keeps per-instance matrices in a float32 DataTexture, so `matrixAt` returns the f64 value ROUNDED: the error is **2^-23 times the distance from the origin** and is a property of the readback, not of the placement. This lane hit the identical thing on the pad (1e-6 m at 137 m, 4e-6 at 244 m, 6e-6 at 367 m) and the fix is to round the EXPECTATION the same way rather than to widen a tolerance. **RETRACTED 2026-07-28, and the retraction is the useful part.** This entry originally also claimed that `probes/factoryrebase.js:66` calls `mustNum(of.world(), 'rebaseM', ...)` against a field that lives only on `Config`, and therefore could not run. **That is FALSE.** Checked at HEAD `c5c680c`: line 87 reads `OF_ARGS.rebaseM ?? null`, line 61 is a comment correctly naming `Config.rebaseM`, and `mustNum(of.world(), 'rebaseM'` has **zero** matches in the file. The working tree is identical to HEAD there. The claim came from a stale read and I relayed it without checking it at the source, which Admin caught before it reached the factory lane. **The `FactoryStale.ts` half above stands and was verified in source.** The lesson, and it generalises: a subagent of mine verified that every file in its subjects' path was byte-identical to HEAD before claiming its clean-archive run was not a weaker result, unasked, and that is exactly the check that would have caught this. **Cross-lane findings must name the sha they were read at.** An instrument's stated blind spot is the list of claims that cannot have come from it, and a claim read from a stale file is the same failure with a different cause. Neither factory file was touched by this lane.
- **R36 (CLOSED 2026-07-28 by PH-83, and BOTH of my proposed fixes were wrong: the cause was the heightfield against itself one tick apart, not jitter and not the voxels. Kept for the record): THE DEEP/SHALLOW GATE CHATTERED ON A STEEP FACE AND THE WALKER WENT BALLISTIC WHILE IT DID.** On a 45 deg face: **8 flips in 152 ticks**, 19 of them airborne, worst flip-tick step **0.190239 m**, and two flip ticks ungrounded with the feet moving **0.002686 m, exactly one tick of free fall**. Bit-for-bit reproducible. **The cause is NOT the threshold and moving it would not help.** At a steep mouth `surfaceR` and the voxel floor differ by more than `CAPSULE.groundSnapM` (0.35 m), and `deep` is evaluated at the STEP TARGET, so sub-centimetre horizontal jitter flips it; on the shallow ticks nothing is within the walking snap so the walker is ballistic, and on the deep ticks `floorBelow` catches it again. **The real gap is that the shallow branch has no fallback when the heightfield is further away than its own snap.** In `KinematicBody.step`, untouched. Reproduce with `probes/deepgate.js --evalargs='{"sites":["hill"]}'`, which is an opt-in precisely because it is red.
- **R37 (NEW, and it is WORLD-GEN's line, measured here while building PH-80's scene): THE 80 m BEDROCK CLAMP IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT, AND A PLAYER CAN STAND ON CLAMPED GROUND OVER OPEN AIR.** Eight `level` presses took a pit floor to `base - 80`. The ninth dug **2,753 more cells 10 m lower** and the walker **did not move by one bit**: 599334.952412 before and after, grounded, depth 0. It is standing on the clamped heightfield with air beneath it. That is `surface_field.h`'s `surfaceHeight` clamp (`kSurfaceMaxFillM` / the -80 m bound), so the decision is world-gen's, not this lane's. Flagged, not touched.

- **R39 (NEW, and it is the ENABLER R38 needs): `radialOrigin` IS NOT PUBLISHED TO THE CLIENT, because widening `of_vs_part_info` would silently empty the catalogue.** [`VesselCatalogue.ts`](../../web/src/game/VesselCatalogue.ts) reads it with `if (got !== PART_INFO_WORDS) continue`, an EXACT-STRIDE check, so going from 34 to 35 words without changing that constant kills every part in the bay rather than failing loudly. A two-line coordinated change, deliberately left to whoever fixes R38 so the two land together.
- **R40 (NEW, and it is the gameplay lane's evidence file, so it is theirs to invert): [`probes/radialsink.js`](../../web/tools/smoke/probes/radialsink.js) (GP-150) NOW FAILS BY NAME, EXACTLY AS DESIGNED.** Its three checks assert the defect EXISTS (`axis at 1.4 not 0.775`, `overlap -0.15 not > 0.4`, `touching would need the axis further out: 1.25 against 1.4`), and its own header says to invert section 2 rather than delete it. Left alone rather than edited mid-flight: a lane's evidence file is not something another lane should rewrite while they are in it.
- **R41 (NEW, and it is an INSTRUMENT defect in this lane's own probe, which is the worst place to have one): `deepgate.js`'s `hillDig` PICKS A DIFFERENT HILLSIDE DEPENDING ON WHAT RAN BEFORE IT.** It sweeps from wherever the feet are when it starts, so run alone it selects 2.2000, 144.3000 (44.81 deg), and run AFTER the spawn scene it selects **2.3821, 143.7000** (45.36 deg), because the spawn scene leaves the player 36 m of bore away. **The R36 numbers I published (8 flips, 152 ticks, 19 airborne, 0.190239, 0.002686) are from the SECOND site, not from the command I quoted beside them.** All of them reproduce exactly under `sites:["spawn","hill"]`. **A site that depends on what ran before it is not yet a measurement**, and this cost a subagent a whole pass. - **R43 CLOSED 2026-08-02 (PH-142), and it was ruled by Admin because it became the load-bearing number in Reid's autopilot ask.** A mixed-propellant stage divided ONE kind's fuel by EVERY engine's mass flow: `propellantKg 4300, burnTimeS 11.203, dv 656.02` against a flight that burned 9000 kg of solid for 27.95 s and ran the liquid engine to 69.58 s. Now phase-integrated per kind, with `fullThrustS` (first flameout) published beside `burnTimeS` (last). True figures for that stage: 13300 kg, 27.948953 / 69.578182 s, 3890.36 m/s. - **R44b CLOSED 2026-08-02 (PH-143), in the same ABI 22 bump as R43.** The per-stage table was read off the DESIGN, which never burns a gram, so no cadence could fix it. Kept for the record): `flight('report').propellantKg` WAS a cached total that could not move during a burn.** `FlightSession.propellantKg()` sums `this.parts`, and `refreshParts()` runs only on roll-out and on staging (`FlightSession.ts:165` and `:235`, plus `FlightVessels.ts:353`). **Measured 4340 to 4340 kg while the live read went to 0.** This is what made the gameplay lane's earlier attempt a non-result: their engine WAS lit and their instrument was stale. **Anything player-facing reading that field shows a full tank right through to burnout.** `flight('tanks')` now publishes the cached and live totals side by side so the disagreement is measurable.
- **R45 (NEW, and it is the gameplay lane's, stated because it makes their own measurement mean something different): THE VAB WILL NOT BUILD A RADIAL FUEL TANK AT ALL.** Aiming a large fuel tank at a radial node refuses with `has no radial mount` ([`VesselNodes.ts:208`](../../web/src/game/VesselNodes.ts)). Only seven parts set `radialMount` and **the only one carrying propellant is the Solid Booster**. PH-84 measured the radial case by building it through the design library, which reaches `_of_vs_attach` directly, and `/core`'s `attach` has no mount check. So the case is measurable but **not assemblable by a player**, and their VAB triple must have come from a path that bypasses `fitAt`. Whether side tanks SHOULD be buildable is a design call, not this lane's.
- **R22 (NEW, for the map lane, and it is the natural next consumer): A DEMOTED VESSEL IS NOT DRAWN AND IS NOT ON THE MAP.** The registry publishes exactly what a map needs and nothing reads it yet: `registry.list()` enumerates every vessel, and `stateOf(M, registry, rec, tick)` gives one a position **without promoting it and without advancing anything**, which is precisely the property a map marker wants (it costs one Kepler solve per marker per draw, and zero when the map is shut). DW-36/DW-37's map already switches focus to orbital objects, so the marker and the selection gesture are the same work. Flagged rather than started because `MapDraw`/`MapView` are mid-migration to a 3D camera (R17) and a half-migrated projection would break the conic that works.
- **R23 (NEW, and it is a TESTABILITY gap rather than a defect): `flight('report')` publishes no state vector.** The probe lane had to read the live integrator's position off `map().focus.centreM`, which is gated on `aboard`, so **a promoted-but-unboarded vessel has no exact published position anywhere in the client**. That is why `probes/vesselrails.js` checks radius and speed plus a conic re-fit rather than a raw vector, which is a weaker assertion than the one it wanted to make. Three numbers on the flight report would fix it. Related and worth the same line: `flight('report').propellantKg` does not move while an engine burns, because it sums the cached `partRows` which `refreshParts` only rewrites on a roll-out or a staging; `FlightCheats.ts` documents this and the record's own `fuelKg` is a live read, so the save is right and the report is stale.
- **R24 (NEW, for whoever owns `test_flight.cpp` next): PH-16's recorded 0.62 m is STALE BY TWO ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE, and the bound that should have caught the drift is 900x too loose.** Reproducing PH-16's own scenario today (120 km circular, same vehicle, 20 ms, 102,132 steps) measures **0.00570342 m and 1.75437e-5 m/s** against the recorded 0.62 m and 0.0003 m/s, and `test_flight.cpp:641` still asserts `posErrM < 5.0`. The millimetre figure is what theory gives, which is why it is the one to believe: velocity-Verlet's relative frequency error here is `(omega*dt)^2/24 = 1.9e-10`, so one revolution slips 1.2e-9 rad and 680 km of radius turns that into about a millimetre. Either the integrator improved since PH-16 or that number was taken at a coarser step, and **nobody can now tell which**, which is the actual cost. A bound at 900x its own residue cannot fail against any regression a player would notice. Not changed by this lane, because tightening another suite's bound on a number this lane did not originally take is how a bound gets tuned until it passes.
- **R25 (NEW, and it is this lane's own blast radius): `reload.mjs`'s PADCLEAR block now asserts the OLD behaviour.** Three of its rows say a reloaded pad carries no vessel, a reloaded session carries zero parts, and the pad comes back HOLDING. All three were true and are now false BY DESIGN for `--mode=leave`, because a vessel left on a pad survives a reload. They must be split by mode rather than deleted: the `recover` run should still assert all three (a recovered vessel really is gone) and the `leave` run should assert the opposite, which makes the pair a stronger control than it was. `reload.mjs` is a shared runner with another lane's uncommitted GP-103 work in it, so this is flagged for whoever holds it rather than edited underneath them. `LaunchPadSave.ts:8-19`'s "THE CLAMP STATE IS DELIBERATELY NOT SAVED, because the flight the clamps were released for is itself not in the save" is stale for the same reason and should be re-argued rather than left contradicting the code.
- **R26 (NEW, for the control-handoff lane): the client can hold exactly ONE live vessel, structurally, and that is fine until docking.** `FlightMode.session` is `readonly`, `VesselObserver.flight` is `private readonly`, and `FlightSession.rollOut` destroys before it creates. The registry removes the constraint on how many vessels EXIST but not on how many are PROMOTED. Nothing in the handoff needs more than one promoted at a time (you fly one rocket), so this is not a blocker for it. It becomes one at DOCKING, where two vessels are in the same near scene and at least one of them has to be drawn and collided against while the other is flown. Naming it now because the fix is a constructor signature, and a lane that discovers it mid-feature will be tempted to reach for a second global instead.
- **R12 (CLOSED as far as physics can close it, PH-36):** the flight entrance is a **roll-out stand-in**, not DW-29's launch pad, and it now says so on screen in those words. DW-29's real entrance is a pad gated behind ground progression, which does not exist at any layer (gameplay R9); **the gate remains an Admin sequencing call and physics should not invent one.**
- **R13 (CLOSED, 2026-07-28, PH-68).** It said this was "a two-field addition to `SaveSlot` and it is not physics' file", and both halves turned out to be wrong in a useful way: it is four fields (`lat`, `lon`, `alt`, plus `aboard`/`vesselId` so a handoff can tell a flying player from a walking one), and it became physics' file the moment a vessel persisted, because a rocket in orbit above a body teleported to the spawn is not a save, it is two half-saves of different worlds. Measured after: lat and lon round-trip to **5e-15 degrees** across a real reload in all three phases, against R13's own measurement of going in at lat 1.613 lon 107.822 and coming back at 2.000 / 144.000. The one subtlety, recorded because it is the sort of thing that reads healthy: the anchor is taken off `ViewRouter.walker` and never off the router, because the router reports whichever source is LIVE and a save taken while strapped in would have recorded a body 80 km up. Original text: **the player's own POSITION is not in the save either.** `SaveSlot` has no player key and `Boot.ts:166` teleports to `cfg.scenario` on every boot, so a reload returns you to the spawn point wherever you were and whatever you were doing, flight or no flight. Measured: `reload.mjs` went in at lat 1.613 lon 107.822 and came back at lat 2.000 lon 144.000. The base, the tunnels, the pack and the hotbar all come back correctly; only the person does not. That is a two-field addition to `SaveSlot` and it is not physics' file.
- **R15 (NEW, and it is the finding the coordinator asked for): a REACHABILITY SWEEP of this domain's own published surface, because `look()` was the fourth built-and-unreachable capability found in a day.** Method: for every public method and every bridge export this domain owns, find the input path that reaches it. **Fixed this lane:** `VesselObserver.look()` (no caller at all), camera zoom and first person (never existed), the four new SAS modes and hold-node (bound the same commit that added them, and `sasNode` on Digit 8 was very nearly the fifth instance: it was in `BINDINGS` and in `MAP_ALLOWED` with nothing reading it until `Systems.ts` was wired). **Still unreachable and NOT fixed, stated plainly:** (1) **the `Parachute` is on the reference rocket, is paid for, and nothing deploys it** - there is no action, no `of_fl_*` export and no deploy dynamics, so the part a player buys for the way down does nothing on the way down; (2) **`LandingLeg` is data** (R5, the landing end is deliberately unbuilt); (3) `Battery`, `SolarPanel` and `DockingPort` are inert by design until there is an electric budget and docking logic; (4) ~~`FlightSim::park`/`resume` exist in `/core` and have **no bridge export**, so on-rails parking is unreachable from the browser~~ **CLOSED 2026-07-28 by PH-66, and note the shape of the fix: the exports added are `of_orb_park`/`of_orb_resume`, the PURE `orbital::` pair, and NOT the `FlightSim` members the risk named.** Exporting the member form would have given a flight handle the ability to park itself, i.e. a second answer to "is this vessel on rails" held inside the object whose retirement is the question. The risk was right that the capability was unreachable and wrong about which capability to reach for, which is worth recording: a reachability sweep finds the gap, it does not design the door. Warp remains physics warp only (PH-26, by design). **Bridge exports with zero client callers**, which are API surface rather than features and are listed so nobody mistakes them for either: `of_atmo_density`, `_density_raw`, `_dynamic_pressure`, `_g0`, `_in_space`, `_lapse`, `_pressure_ratio` (all computed inside `of_fl_step` and reported through telemetry), `of_fl_capture_hold`, and the `of_vs_*` duplicates of `of_fl_*` reads (`_assign_stage`, `_count`, `_fire_stage`, `_next_stage_index`, `_part_index_of`, `_propellant_aboard`, `_remaining_dv_vacuum`, `_stage_clear`). **The general form, and it is cheap: a capability is not shipped until a KEY OR A BUTTON reaches it, and the check is one grep per public method.**
- **R16 (NEW, for whoever owns `web/src/game/GameplayInput.ts`): one word.** PH-43 moved `mute` off `KeyM` so the map could have it, and `GameplayInput.ts:63` still flashes `'sound off  (M)'` / `'sound on  (M)'`. The key is now Backslash. The physics lane may not touch that file. **Backslash is a holding pen and not a design**: mute deserves a real home, and the right one may well be a settings panel rather than a key at all.
- **R17 (NEW, for Admin, and it lands ON this work): DW-36 expands the map from an orbital view into ONE continuous player-centred map with discovery, ore counts and focus switching.** Read as a delta against what shipped: the MODE, the modal registration, the per-panel allow list, the readout column and the node UI all survive; what does not is `MapDraw`'s assumption that the projection is centred on the BODY and framed by the conic. A player-centred, continuously zooming map wants the projection origin to be a parameter and the drawn layer set to be a function of scale. `MapTypes.MapScene` is where that lands and it is already the seam: it carries `planeU`/`planeV`/`spanM` and would gain a centre. **Discovery and ore counts are not this domain's**: a visited grid is world-gen/persistence and deposit pools are `deposits.h`, so the map becomes a consumer of two more publishers rather than a new authority. Flagged rather than started, because the coordinator asked for this lane to land first and because a half-migrated projection would break the conic that works.
- **R46 (NEW, HIGH, for gameplay/persistence, found while stamping `dayT`): NAMED SAVES BYPASS THE `writeSlot` CHOKE POINT, so a named save carries NO vessels, NO player anchor and NO time of day.** `SaveSlots.save` calls `stampAssisted(this.snapshotOf(g))` and then `writeKey` directly (`SaveSlots.ts:152`), on the stated grounds that `writeSlot` derives the key from the mode; but `writeSlot` is ALSO where PH-67 stamps `vessels`, PH-68 stamps `player` and PH-86 stamps `dayT`, so the named path silently loses all three, and loading a named slot (a copy onto the auto key plus a restart) restores a world with the rocket gone, the body at the scenario spawn and the clock at the boot solve. PH-67's "every write in the client comes through this one function" was true when written and is false since GP-136. The fix wants ONE shared stamp function both writers call (the `stampAssisted` shape, widened), not three fields copied into a second path. Found by reading while adding a fourth stamped field; not driven, and not fixed here because `SaveSlots.ts` is the gameplay lane's live file.
- **R47 (NEW, LOW, a design note rather than a defect): THE WARP CREDIT REACHES THE SKY AND DELIBERATELY NOT THE FACTORY.** Under 1000x physics warp the vessel's MET and now the sun both advance 1000x while the smelters tick at 1x, so a player who warps through a night lands with the factory having lived 4 s of the hour the sky swept. This incoherence PREDATES the day cycle (warp has been flight-local since PH-26) and was invisible while the sky stood still; the cycle makes it observable from orbit. Making the factory warp is a world-clock decision touching core-engine's tick authority and factory-sim's budget, i.e. Admin's, and the day clock is written so that whichever way that lands, the sun follows the same one answer (`dayWarpCredit` is one call site). Also stated for the record: the sun advances while pause menus are open, because the day clock is the LOOP's clock, the same one the wind and water already run on during a pause. A player AFK in a menu for 30 minutes comes back at a different time of day. If Admin wants menus to freeze the world, that is one gate in `Systems.ts` and it should gate the wind and the ripples with it.
- **R14 (NEW, cosmetic, for whoever owns the readout next):** on the pad and for the first seconds of a vertical climb the trajectory is very nearly RADIAL, so `e` is 1 to within rounding and `/core` classifies it as unbound: **AP and PE read `---` for about the first ten seconds of every launch**, which KSP fills in immediately. It is correct arithmetic on a degenerate conic and it is not what a player expects. A radial ellipse has a perfectly good apoapsis and computing it is a special case in `summarize()`, in `/core`, not in the client.

| 2026-08-02 | **What does the existing maneuver machinery already SOLVE**, briefed to quote signatures verbatim, to read only `maneuver.h` / `orbital.h` / the `of_mn_*` exports / `probes/maneuver.js` / the vessel-record API, and to state plainly what is ABSENT, because a second orbital solver in TypeScript is the failure mode this project has paid for repeatedly | Done | **`maneuver::plan()` EVALUATES, it never SOLVES.** It is a forward map from a Node you already chose to the resulting conic: propagate to the node, build the triad, add the impulse, summarise. There is NO inverse layer anywhere: no Lambert, no Hohmann, no phase angle, no synodic period, no closest approach, no departure-time sweep, and nothing in the whole header takes a SECOND orbit or a second state vector, so there is no target concept at all. What IS there and must be composed rather than re-derived: `orbital::propagate` (universal-variable, Stumpff, one code path, hyperbolic handled throughout), `stateToElements`/`elementsToState`, `park`/`resume`, `basisAt`, `estimateBurn` (walks the REMAINING stages and inverts Tsiolkovsky for the partial one), `summarize`, `samplePath`. Wasm face: `of_mn_path` (count*3 polyline), `of_mn_orbit_meta` (18 words), `of_mn_plan` (26 words), `of_orb_park`/`of_orb_resume` (9 and 6, handle-free and pure). Records live entirely in TypeScript (`VesselRegistry.ts`); nothing advances per tick, `stateOf` is one `_of_orb_resume` at `clockAt(rec, tick)`, and `stampedTick === -1` means TIME DOES NOT PASS for that record. |

### 8.1 The demo script, and what a person can safely do to it

Written for whoever is standing behind the keyboard. Every line below was
driven, not reasoned about, by `probes/flightabuse.js` (14/14 rows) and
`tools/smoke/reload.mjs` (pad, orbit and ground).

**The path:** C builds a rocket in the bay. G rolls it out 26 m ahead and says
it is a stand-in for a launch pad. Walk to it, G again to board. Shift or Z
opens the throttle, Space lights the first stage and releases the clamp, W and S
fly the nose onto the guidance marker, Space again when the first stage runs
dry, X cuts at an 80 km apoapsis, Y cycles SAS, P and O warp, G gets out once
it is down.

**Verified safe to do wrong:** mash Space on the pad (refused, and it says
throttle up first); throttle up with no engine lit (clamp holds, TWR shown);
Escape, Tab, V and left-click in flight (nothing, and the click cannot dig a
crater under the parked avatar below); open the assembly bay at 20 km and close
it again (the flight continues, and G is deliberately inert while the bay owns
the screen, so C then G is the way out); try to climb out at altitude (refused);
fly retrograde under power (no tumble, no NaN); mash the warp ladder to its top
and back; abort the ascent and ride the whole re-entry at 1000x without touching
a key (48 telemetry samples inside the atmosphere, a real 139 kPa peak, deepest
AGL 0.0 m); stage and throttle a spent vehicle. **After all of it the player
gets out, walks and digs.**

**Known rough edges a viewer may notice, none of them faults:** AP and PE read
`---` for about the first ten seconds of a launch (R14, the trajectory is
radial and `/core` calls a radial conic unbound); the HUD says regime ASCENT at
80 km (R10, `ORBIT_START` is 100 km); and the standing amber chip says the
flight is not saved, which is true and is the point (R11).

## 9. References
**Vessel programme (PH-10 to PH-18):** DW-29 (the programme), DW-30 (real but greased, the enumerated concessions), DW-18 (`BodyParams::muM3S2`, the one gravity authority), D-006 (body constants, Forge's 5.6 km scale height, Cinder airless), [ASSET-SPECS §3.3](../web/ASSET-SPECS.md) (the 1.25 m stack contract, which every dimension in the catalogue is read off). Built: [`vessel.h`](../../core/include/of/vessel.h), [`atmosphere.h`](../../core/include/of/atmosphere.h), [`flight.h`](../../core/include/of/flight.h), [`test_vessel.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_vessel.cpp), [`test_flight.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_flight.cpp), `vessel_tests` + `flight_tests` targets in [`core/CMakeLists.txt`](../../core/CMakeLists.txt). Method: Tsiolkovsky; Barrowman's normal-force coefficients (PH-14); turbulent flat-plate skin friction (PH-15); velocity-Verlet with a velocity predictor for the drag term, reducing bit-exactly to `orbital::Integrator` in vacuum (PH-16).

MASTER_PLAN §6 (orbital), D-002, D-006. Spike-2 design + full citations: [spike2-physics.md](../spikes/spike2-physics.md) §9. **Built core (PH-6):** [`core/include/of/orbital.h`](../../core/include/of/orbital.h) (orbital math + symplectic integrator), [`core/tests/test_physics.cpp`](../../core/tests/test_physics.cpp) (9 tests / 71 checks), `physics_tests` target in [`core/CMakeLists.txt`](../../core/CMakeLists.txt). Key: universal-variable Kepler propagation (Stumpff C/S, Curtis Algorithm 3.4); patched-conic SOI intersection (poliastro); KSP Krakensbane (coordinate workaround); UE5 LWC jitter ≥3 km + Chaos Async Physics Tick; Kerbal Joint Reinforcement / autostrut (wobble mitigation). Principia (n-body, cautionary — not adopted, D-002).
